# STATE.md — Single source of truth

This is the **one file to read at the start of every session**. It describes where the project is *right now*. Rewrite it in place each session — never append a log. History lives in `docs/sessions/CHANGELOG.md`.

---

## What this project is

A custom **Digital Asset Management (DAM)** platform. Coperon uploads assets (images, video, audio, PDFs); clients log in and access only assets shared with their group. Files live in object storage; only metadata lives in the database.

---

## Version control

**The project is on GitHub: https://github.com/CoperonDev/damplatform** (private).
Committed 2026-08-18 — before that date the entire app was uncommitted on one machine.

**Branches:** `main` (default) and `dev`, created 2026-08-31 from `main` and tracking
`origin/dev`. Both are at the same commit as of that date.

**Auth — corrected 2026-08-31, this section had it wrong.** `gh` is authenticated for
**three** accounts, not two: `JoeYoussef44C`, `CoperonDev`, and `JoeYoussef44`. This
file previously claimed CoperonDev was the CLI's active account; it was not — the
active account was `JoeYoussef44C`, which **cannot see this repo at all**. It was
switched to `CoperonDev` on 2026-08-31 and left there. CoperonDev is a personal
account, not an org, and its token has no `delete_repo` scope.

**The failure mode to recognise:** pushing or `gh repo view`-ing as the wrong account
fails with **`Repository not found`**, never a permission error — GitHub returns 404
rather than 403 for a private repo you cannot see, so this reads like a deleted or
renamed repo when it is only the wrong identity. Check `gh auth status` first.

**And a second, independent trap:** `credential.helper` here is `manager` (Git
Credential Manager), so `git push` uses whatever github.com credential Windows has
stored — **`gh auth switch` alone does not change what git pushes with.** To push as
the gh active account, override for the one command:
`git -c credential.helper='!gh auth git-credential' push origin <branch>`.

**Never committed:** `.env` / `.env.local`, `dam_backup.sql` (live user rows +
bcrypt hashes), `minio_backup.tar.gz` (155 MB, over GitHub's file limit),
`.claude/settings.local.json`, `node_modules/`, `.next/`.

## Current architecture

**Single Next.js 16 full-stack app** — migration from NestJS is complete.

| Layer | Location | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Frontend + API routes | `frontend/` (Next.js 16.2.9, App Router) | Active — build + prod server re-verified 2026-08-31 |
| Schema migrations | `db/migrations/`, runner at `frontend/scripts/migrate.mjs` | New 2026-08-18 — **baseline still unverified** against the live DB (plan 2.1) |
| Database | PostgreSQL 18 **in Docker**, host port **5433** | Live, fully seeded — 136 resources / 9 users / 32 collections |
| Object storage | MinIO in Docker, **not pinned** — `damInfra/docker-compose.yml` says `minio/minio:latest`; the container happens to be running `RELEASE.2025-09-07` because that is what `latest` resolved to when it was pulled (corrected 2026-08-31 — this row previously claimed it was pinned) | Running, holding **307 objects / 206.5 MB**. **MinIO was archived 25 Apr 2026** and is unmaintained; move off it before beta (plan 3.2). Until then, avoid `docker compose pull` — `latest` can move under you. |
| Legacy NestJS backend | Archived outside the repo | Removed from the tree 2026-08-18 |
| Redis / BullMQ | In `docker-compose.yml`, unused by code | Not needed — a DB-backed job table would do if a queue is ever wanted |
| Worker process | Not built | Media processing runs in-request; acceptable on a single server |

**Directory layout changed 2026-08-18.** The tree was flattened from
`Coperon/damplatform/damplatform/frontend` to `Coperon/damplatform/frontend`.
Any path in notes written before that date is stale. The repo root now holds
`frontend/`, `damInfra/`, `db/`, and `docs/`.

## How to run

**Database:** PostgreSQL 18 runs **in Docker**, not as a native Windows install.
Start Docker Desktop, then `docker compose start` in `damInfra/`. It is published
on **host port 5433** (5432 is taken by another project on this machine), which is
what `DATABASE_URL` points at.

> Corrected 2026-08-18: this section previously claimed a "native Windows install"
> reachable via `C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe`. That path does not
> exist on this machine and there is no native Postgres — it has always been the
> container. The wrong version nearly sent the production setup down a bad path.

**MinIO:** starts with the same `docker compose start`. Console at `http://localhost:9001`.
Use `docker compose stop`, **never** `down -v` (deletes data).

**Migrations:**
```
cd frontend
node scripts/migrate.mjs            # apply anything pending
node scripts/migrate.mjs --status   # show applied vs pending
```
Append-only. The runner stores a sha256 per file and refuses to run if an already
applied migration changed on disk. Never edit an applied file — add a new one.

**Next.js app:**
```
cd frontend
npm run dev     # http://localhost:3000
```

That is all. There is no separate backend process to start.

## Security — outstanding (opened 2026-08-18)

Real and unfixed. Do not treat the codebase as clean.

**1. Secret rotation is NOT done — the old credentials are still live.**
They were scrubbed out of this file before the first commit, so they never entered
git history, but scrubbing a document does not invalidate a credential.

| Secret | Status | Who |
|---|---|---|
| `JWT_SECRET` | **Still a short, guessable dictionary-word string** (the live value is in `frontend/.env.local`, not repeated here). Anyone who guesses it can mint a super-admin token. Highest priority. | me, once Postgres is up |
| Postgres password | Unrotated | me |
| MinIO root credentials | Unrotated — pair with creating a bucket-scoped service account, so the app stops using root | me |
| SMTP app password | Unrotated | **only the user** can reissue (Google) |
| `SHARE_TOKEN_KEY` | **Never rotate.** Doing so makes every existing share link permanently undecryptable. | — |

Blocked on 2026-08-18 because Docker Desktop and Postgres were both down.

**2. `GET /api/cover` is an IDOR.** It takes an arbitrary storage `key` from the query
string and returns a presigned URL for it, gated only by `requireAuth`. Any authenticated
user — including a role-5 *pending* user with no collection access at all — can read any
object in the bucket. Keys are `uploads/<timestamp>-<filename>` and leak through list
responses. This bypasses the entire tenant cascade the rest of the codebase is built on.
Fix: accept a resource/collection id and run the same check as `GET /api/download/[id]`.
**Scoped 2026-08-31 — the whole route is 21 lines and there are exactly 6 call sites in
two shapes; see plan 1.1 for the id-based replacement and the file/line list.**

**3. The tenant access-check CTE is duplicated across route files** even though
`tenantHasResourceAccess` / `tenantHasCollectionAccess` already exist in
`lib/permissions.ts`. Any access-control fix has to land in every copy, and one miss is
a cross-client data leak. Highest-value refactor in the codebase. **Measured
2026-08-31: 16 files, and the copies have drifted into at least 3 different shapes,
while the lib helpers are used by only a handful of routes — two sources of truth.**
See plan 4.2.

**4. The JWT lives in `localStorage`**, hand-read at **88** call sites (measured
2026-08-31; 141 raw `fetch()` calls total, no shared API client). Any XSS is full
account takeover. Should be an httpOnly + Secure + SameSite cookie behind a
`middleware.ts` gate — there is no middleware at all today. Note this is *caused* by the
all-client-side frontend (19/19 pages are `"use client"`): the token must be reachable
from client JS. See plan 4.1 and 4.3 — fix the API client first, or the cookie
migration means editing 88 sites.

**5. No rate limiting anywhere** — login, forgot-password, invite redeem, and public
share tokens are all unthrottled. Confirmed by grep 2026-08-31: no rate-limit code
exists. See plan 1.3.

**6. No index on `collections(parent_id)`**, which every recursive access check walks.
Confirmed 2026-08-31 — `collections` carries only `collections_pkey`. See plan 2.3.

**7. No tests, no CI, no staging environment.** Every stage to date was verified by hand.

## Deployment readiness plan (opened 2026-08-31)

Goal: get the app onto Coperon's Windows server as a beta, on Neon + hosted object
storage, without shipping the known security holes. Work in phase order — each phase
is independently shippable, and Phases 1–2 are deliberately cheap because they are
easier to do now, while the only data at risk is test data.

**Already landed (2026-08-31, second session) — the Vercel-readiness code changes.**
Hosting was re-decided (Vercel + Neon + R2; see "Target hosting" and plan 3.3) and the
four things that would have broken a Vercel deploy are fixed, `tsc --noEmit` and
`next build` both clean:

| Change | File | Why it was a blocker |
|---|---|---|
| `outputFileTracingIncludes` for ffmpeg/canvas/pdfjs | `next.config.ts` | Their binaries and data files were untraced, so they'd be **absent from the deployed function while the build still succeeded** — a production-only ENOENT. Verified present in the built `.nft.json`. |
| `maxDuration` — 300 s thumbnail, 60 s both upload routes | 3 route files | No route declared one; a serverless default of 10–15 s kills every video thumbnail. |
| Bounded, env-configurable pool (`PG_POOL_MAX`, default 10) | `lib/db.ts` | Unbounded pool × N warm instances exhausts Postgres. |
| **Pool deadlock in both upload routes** | `upload/complete`, `upload/from-url` | Post-`COMMIT` pool work ran while the transaction client was still held — a permanent hang at a small pool. Found while sizing the pool, not suspected beforehand. See "Conventions that bite". |

Verified live, not merely compiled: a real upload (`/api/upload/url` → presigned PUT →
`/api/upload/complete`) returned **201 in 203 ms with `PG_POOL_MAX=1`**, where the
pre-fix code hangs forever — the old and new shapes were also reproduced side by side
against the live DB at `max: 1` (old: hung; new: completed). Test resource deleted; DB
back to its 136-resource baseline. **Phases 1 and 2 are untouched and still block a
real deployment.**

**Runtime status confirmed 2026-08-31** (re-verified live, not assumed): `next build`
exits 0, `next start` serves in 643 ms, login mints a correct JWT, `GET /api/collections`
returns 6, and `GET /api/download/[id]` → presigned URL → 177,271 real bytes of JPEG.
The restored dataset is intact (136 resources / 9 users / 32 collections). The app is
runnable and buildable as-is; nothing below is blocking *running* it, only *deploying*
it to real users.

### Phase 1 — Security blockers (must land before any real user)

**1.1 Fix the `GET /api/cover` IDOR.** The route is 21 lines: `requireAuth`, then it
presigns whatever `?key=` the client sent. Any authenticated user — including a role-5
*pending* account with zero collection access — can read any object in the bucket.
The fix is scoped and known: **stop accepting a storage key from the client; accept an
id and look the key up server-side.** There are exactly **6 real call sites** in two
shapes, so the route needs two:

| New shape | Anchor | Look up | Callers |
|---|---|---|---|
| `?collectionId=<uuid>` | collection | `collections.cover_storage_key` | `app/home/page.tsx:280`, `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx:646` |
| `?resourceId=<uuid>` | resource | `resources.thumbnail_storage_key` | `app/admin/media/page.tsx:362,644`, `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx:682,1535`, `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx:194` |

Gate each with the existing `tenantHasCollectionAccess` / `tenantHasResourceAccess`
helpers in `lib/permissions.ts` — do not hand-roll another CTE here (see 4.2).

**1.2 Rotate the live secrets.** `JWT_SECRET` is a short guessable dictionary word;
anyone who guesses it mints a super-admin token. Rotate it, the Postgres password, and
the MinIO/storage credentials. **Never rotate `SHARE_TOKEN_KEY`** — that permanently
breaks every existing share link. The SMTP app password can only be reissued by the
user, via Google. Rotating `JWT_SECRET` signs everyone out, which is free right now and
expensive later — that is the argument for doing it in this phase.

**1.3 Add rate limiting** to `POST /api/auth/login`, `POST /api/auth/forgot-password`,
`POST /api/invitations/redeem`, and the public `GET /api/share/[token]` routes. All four
are unthrottled and reachable from the open internet once deployed.

### Phase 2 — Schema hygiene (one migration, ~30 minutes)

**2.1 Verify `db/migrations/0001_baseline.sql` against the live database.** Written in
the Phase 0 session and **never checked**. Everything about the Neon move depends on it
being right. Postgres is running again, so this is now cheap.

**Sharpened 2026-08-31 by `scripts/check-env.mjs`: the live dev database has no
`schema_migrations` table at all.** The migration runner has therefore never been run
against it — unsurprising, since the database was restored from `dam_backup.sql` rather
than built by migrations, but it means the ledger and the schema have *never* agreed and
the baseline has never executed anywhere. So 2.1 is not merely "diff the file against
reality"; nothing has ever proven the file can even run. Verify it by applying it to a
**scratch empty database** and diffing the result against a `pg_dump --schema-only` of
the live one — do not apply it to the dev database to find out.

**2.2 Drop three dead tables.** Verified 2026-08-31 against both the live DB and a full
`app/`+`lib/`+`scripts/` reference sweep — all three have zero rows *and* zero code
references:

| Table | Rows | Code refs | Superseded by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `resource_types` | 0 | **0** | nothing — and 0 of 136 resources have `resource_type_id` set |
| `renditions` | 0 | **0** (1 comment only) | `resources.thumbnail_storage_key` (134/136 populated) |
| `audit_log` | 0 | **0** | `access_log` (Stage 96) |

Drop `resources.resource_type_id` with `resource_types`, FK first. Append-only: this is
a new migration file, never an edit to 0001.

**2.3 Add the missing index:** `CREATE INDEX ON collections(parent_id);` — every
recursive access check in the app walks that column and only the PK exists today.

### Phase 3 — Infrastructure move

**3.1 Neon.** Use the pooled (`-pooler`) connection string — verified 2026-08-31 that
the codebase is transaction-mode-pooler safe: every transaction takes a dedicated client
via `db.connect()` before `BEGIN`, and there is no `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY`, no session-scoped
`SET`, and no SQL-level `PREPARE` anywhere. Free tier compute suspends after 5 min idle
(cold start on the next request) — acceptable for beta. Set **`PG_POOL_MAX=1`** (the
knob added 2026-08-31; see `lib/db.ts`).

**The project now exists and has been reached — measured 2026-08-31 with
`scripts/check-env.mjs`, not assumed:**

| | |
|---|---|
| Project / branch | `long-frost-00407846` / `production` |
| Region | **`us-east-2`** (Ohio) — see the open question below |
| Database / role | `neondb` / `neondb_owner` (not a superuser) |
| Server version | **PostgreSQL 18.6** — *newer* than local 18.4, so a `pg_dump` restores forward with no version problem. The version risk flagged below is **closed, favourably.** |
| `pg_trgm` / `pgcrypto` | **Available** (v1.6 / v1.4) and `neondb_owner` **is permitted to create them** — proven by running the `CREATE EXTENSION` inside a transaction and rolling back. The baseline will not fail at line 31. |
| Contents | **Empty — 0 tables.** Nothing is loaded yet. |
| Connection | Pooled endpoint reached over TLS with `sslmode=verify-full`. |

Both strings are configured in `frontend/.env.local`: `DATABASE_URL` is the **pooled**
endpoint (the app), `DIRECT_DATABASE_URL` is the **direct** one. `scripts/migrate.mjs`
now prefers `DIRECT_DATABASE_URL` when set and warns if it is about to run DDL through a
`-pooler` host, so the right endpoint is used without anyone having to remember. Both
were rewritten from Neon's `sslmode=require` to **`sslmode=verify-full`**. The previous
local Docker URL is preserved as a commented line directly beneath them and was
re-verified to still work, so switching back is one line.

**Open, and cheapest to settle before any data is loaded: the region.** The project is in
`us-east-2` (Ohio) while the app ships **English and Italian** locales
(`locales/en.json`, `locales/it.json`). If the beta users are in Europe, every request
pays a transatlantic round trip — and this app issues several queries per request (auth,
permission resolution, the recursive tenant access check, then the query itself), so it
compounds. **A Neon project's region is fixed at creation**; changing it means a new
project. Raised 2026-08-31, not yet decided.

*Check before creating the project — these are unverified and one of them can force
rework:*

- **Postgres version.** Local is **18.4**. If Neon's newest offering is older, a
  `pg_dump` taken from 18 is **not guaranteed to restore into it** — dumps are forward-
  compatible, not backward. Check Neon's available versions *first*; if 18 is not
  offered, take the dump with the older `pg_dump` binary matching the target.
- **Extensions.** `0001_baseline.sql` creates **`pg_trgm`** and **`pgcrypto`**. Both are
  normally available on Neon, but confirm before assuming — the baseline fails at line 31
  otherwise.
- **`resources.search_vector` is a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (...) STORED` tsvector.** It
  restores as a generated column and must not be dumped as data; verify it is present and
  populated after the load, since search silently returns nothing if it is not.

*Load order matters, and the obvious approach corrupts the migration ledger:*

1. **Do Phase 2.1 first.** `0001_baseline.sql` has still never been verified against the
   live database. Everything here assumes it is right; if it is wrong, it is wrong on
   Neon too, only now with data on top.
2. **Restore a full `pg_dump` (schema + data)** into the empty Neon database. Do *not*
   run `migrate.mjs` first and then load a `--data-only` dump: data-only restores do not
   guarantee foreign-key ordering, and the usual escape hatch — `--disable-triggers` —
   **requires superuser, which Neon does not grant.**
3. **Then mark the baseline as applied by hand**, or the ledger will claim the schema was
   never created. `migrate.mjs` records a sha256 per file and will otherwise report
   `0001_baseline.sql` as PENDING and try to re-run it. Compute the hash the same way the
   runner does (sha256 of the file read as utf8), then insert one row into Neon:
   `INSERT INTO schema_migrations (filename, checksum) VALUES ('0001_baseline.sql', '<hash>');`
   Confirm with `node scripts/migrate.mjs --status` → `applied  0001_baseline.sql`.
4. **Verify row counts against the known baseline: 136 resources / 9 users / 32
   collections.**

*Two operational facts that are easy to trip over:*

- **Migrations can never run on Vercel.** `migrate.mjs` resolves `../../db/migrations`,
  and `db/` sits *outside* `frontend/`, which is the Root Directory Vercel deploys — the
  directory simply is not there. Migrations run from a local checkout or CI with
  `DATABASE_URL` pointed at Neon. This is fine, but it must be someone's documented job.
- **`dam_backup.sql` is not a production seed.** It carries live user rows, bcrypt
  hashes, and a shared test-account password that is a **super_admin** login. Decide what
  happens to those accounts *before* the restore, not after.

**3.2 Object storage — Backblaze B2 (decided 2026-08-31; replaces the R2 recommendation).**
**MinIO was archived 25 April 2026** — repo read-only, development stopped Feb 2026,
community edition source-only — so the local container is pinned to nothing and will
receive no security updates. **Cloudflare R2 was the original recommendation and was
rejected on a hard constraint: Cloudflare requires a payment method to enable R2, even on
the free tier, and the team does not want to put card details there.** Backblaze B2 needs
no card for its permanent 10 GB free tier, and its S3-compatible API was verified against
this application's actual requirements rather than assumed.

**The bucket exists and has been proven end-to-end — measured 2026-08-31 with
`scripts/check-env.mjs`:**

| | |
|---|---|
| Bucket | `coperon-dam-assets`, **private**, SSE-B2 encryption on, Object Lock off |
| Endpoint / region | `https://s3.eu-central-003.backblazeb2.com` / **`eu-central-003`** (Amsterdam) |
| `S3_REGION` | the region slug from the endpoint host — **not `auto`**, which is R2-only |
| Path-style addressing | **Works.** `lib/storage.ts` hardcodes `forcePathStyle: true`; this was the one compatibility unknown and it is now closed. |
| Presigned PUT/GET | **Work**, including the checksum fix — a real put → get → delete round-trip returned byte-identical data. |
| CORS | Configured for `http://localhost:3000` and `https://damplatform.vercel.app`; both `PUT` and `GET` verified by real preflight. |

**No application code change was needed for B2** beyond the checksum options already in
`lib/storage.ts`. The storage layer is genuinely portable, as designed.

*The CORS trap, which cost real time and will cost it again on the next bucket:*

- **B2's web-UI CORS presets are read-only.** "Share everything in this bucket with this
  one origin" writes a rule allowing **`GET` and `HEAD` only**, with
  `allowedHeaders: ["authorization", "range"]`. That is built for serving files publicly.
  This app needs the browser to **`PUT`** (every upload) and to send `content-type`, so
  the preset can never work no matter which origin is typed into it. It also accepts
  exactly **one** origin, while the app needs at least localhost plus the deployed domain.
- **The origin field rejects a trailing slash** — `https://x.vercel.app/` is a URL, not an
  origin, and B2 answers "an allowedOrigin doesn't look like an origin".
- **`PutBucketCors` over the S3 API is refused** once native rules exist: *"The bucket
  contains B2 Native CORS rules. Please use B2 Native API instead."* So custom rules must
  go through the **B2 Native API** (`b2_authorize_account` → `b2_update_bucket` with a
  `corsRules` array) or the B2 CLI, not the S3 SDK.
- The working rule, for reference — `allowedOperations` uses B2's `s3_*` verbs, not HTTP
  method names:
  ```json
  { "corsRuleName": "damAppOrigins",
    "allowedOrigins": ["http://localhost:3000", "https://damplatform.vercel.app"],
    "allowedOperations": ["s3_get", "s3_head", "s3_put"],
    "allowedHeaders": ["*"], "exposeHeaders": ["etag"], "maxAgeSeconds": 3600 }
  ```
- **Diagnose with `GetBucketCors`, not by guessing.** Reading back what B2 actually stored
  is what revealed the preset was `GET`/`HEAD`-only; the browser-side symptom is an opaque
  403 with no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header, identical for every possible cause.

*Considered and rejected 2026-08-31 — **Neon Object Storage** (noticed in the Neon
console, S3-compatible, and appealing because it would collapse everything to one
provider and one bill):*

- **It is available in `AWS us-east-2` only.** That is the decisive one. The B2 bucket is
  already in EU Central, and the whole point of the pending region move is to stop
  straddling the Atlantic; adopting this would drag the assets back to Ohio.
- **CORS configuration is not documented anywhere in its overview.** For this app that is
  not a detail — the browser PUTs every upload and fetches every thumbnail directly from
  storage. Undocumented-but-mandatory is not a foundation.
- **Beta**, with "known limitations" in S3 compatibility that the docs do not enumerate,
  and it rate-limits with `503 SlowDown` expecting client retry logic that
  `lib/storage.ts` does not have.
- **5 GB free during the beta only**, then $0.023/GB-month, versus B2's 10 GB free
  permanently with no card.

Revisit only if it leaves beta with a European region and documented CORS.

*Two things still outstanding on this bucket:*

- **File Lifecycle is "Keep all versions".** That silently defeats the app's *permanent*
  delete (`DELETE /api/resources/[id]` and the tenant-deletion cleanup both remove stored
  bytes): B2 keeps the bytes behind a delete marker, so a client asking for their assets to
  be destroyed would not actually get that. It also accumulates garbage, since
  `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail?force=true` overwrites the same key on every
  regeneration. **Change to "Keep only the last version"** — versioning contradicts this
  app's own stated behaviour rather than protecting it.
- **The application key is over-privileged.** Its capabilities include `writeBuckets`,
  `writeBucketEncryption`, `writeBucketReplications` and more. The app only ever needs
  `listFiles`, `readFiles`, `writeFiles`, `deleteFiles` on this one bucket. Narrow it before
  the key is pasted into Vercel — it will live there as a long-term secret. (Note that
  narrowing it removes `writeBuckets`, so change the CORS rules *first*, or keep a separate
  admin key for that.)

*Data still to migrate:* the local bucket holds **307 objects / 206.5 MB**, of which 277
keys are referenced by the database, **0 referenced-but-missing**, and 30 orphans. Copy
with `rclone` under **byte-identical keys** — every `storage_key`, `thumbnail_storage_key`
and `cover_storage_key` in the database is relative, so nothing needs rewriting. Afterwards
re-run the reconciliation: all 277 referenced keys must resolve and missing must still be 0.

**3.3 Vercel for the app (decided 2026-08-31, reversing the IIS plan).** IIS was only
ever chosen because Postgres and MinIO were going to live on that same Windows box.
Once 3.1 and 3.2 move both off it, the server's only remaining job is running Node —
and every objection recorded against Vercel on 2026-08-18 was a consequence of
self-hosting the database:

| 2026-08-18 objection | Status now |
|---|---|
| No PgBouncer build for Windows | **Gone** — Neon pools server-side (3.1) |
| Docker Desktop unfit for an unattended server | **Gone** — no containers to run |
| Postgres must be exposed to the public internet, no IP allowlist possible | **Gone** — Neon is not our socket to expose |
| Every video round-trips to Vercel and back, paid egress twice | **Half gone** — R2 charges zero egress (3.2); what remains is Vercel-side compute, not transfer cost |
| 250 MB serverless bundle vs. the FFmpeg binary | **Real, and measured** — see below |

Root Directory = `frontend`. **Pro plan is mandatory, and there are now three independent
reasons rather than one** — the first was known, the other two were hit in practice on
2026-08-31 while trying to deploy from the Hobby plan:

1. **Licensing.** Hobby is non-commercial-use only.
2. **Hobby blocks deployments whose commit author is not a project collaborator**, and it
   does not support collaboration on private repositories at all. The first `dev` push
   was refused outright: *"The deployment was blocked because the commit author did not
   have contributing access to the project on Vercel."* The repo is owned by
   **CoperonDev** while commits were being authored as **JoeYoussef44C** — two of the
   three GitHub accounts on this machine (see "Version control"). Fixed by setting a
   **repo-local** git identity (`git config user.email dev@coperon.com`), leaving the
   global one alone.
3. **`maxDuration = 300` exceeds Hobby's 60-second ceiling.** The thumbnail route
   declares 300 (plan 3.3 below); Hobby caps function duration at 60 s and rejects a
   larger value. Even if 1 and 2 were solved, this route would not deploy — and lowering
   it to 60 is not a fix, it just moves the failure to the first large video.

What remains genuinely Vercel-specific was measured and closed on 2026-08-31:

- **Function weight.** Only `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` imports the heavy
  three. Measured from `node_modules`: `pdfjs-dist` 36 MB total (~17 MB actually
  needed), `@napi-rs/canvas` 36 MB (27 MB Skia + 10 MB `icudtl.dat`, in a *per-platform*
  package — the parent is a 152 KB shim), and `ffmpeg-static` **18 MB on Windows but
  ~78 MB on Linux**. So the thumbnail function lands ≈130–140 MB against the 250 MB
  uncompressed limit: it fits, with less headroom than the Windows numbers suggest.
  **Always measure this on Linux, never locally.**
- **File tracing, the trap.** `serverExternalPackages` stops these three being bundled
  but does **not** make them ship. Next decides a function's files by following
  `require()` graphs, and all three reach their payload by a *computed path* instead —
  so untraced they are absent from the deployment, the build still succeeds, and the
  route fails in production. Closed by `outputFileTracingIncludes` in `next.config.ts`.
- **`maxDuration`.** No route declared one. Now set: 300 s on the thumbnail route
  (Vercel Pro's ceiling; Hobby caps at 60 and will reject it at deploy time), 60 s on
  both upload routes.
- **Pool size.** `lib/db.ts` created an unbounded pool; on Vercel every warm instance
  holds its own. Now `PG_POOL_MAX` (default 10, set **1** on Vercel).

Still true and unchanged by the host: browser uploads go straight to object storage via
presigned URL and never traverse the app, so no platform request-size limit is the
upload ceiling — it only constrains the server-side Import-from-URL path (itself capped
at 15 MB in code).

**If Vercel is ever abandoned, IIS remains viable** and its notes are worth keeping:
one site, one application pool set to "No Managed Code"; no Web Gardens (each extra
worker opens its own DB pool); idle time-out and recycling off so IIS does not fight the
process manager; Node 22 LTS + URL Rewrite 2.1 + ARR 3.0 (**proxy is off by default and
must be enabled after install**) + a service manager (NSSM/PM2/node-windows) + a TLS
cert bound in IIS; **not** iisnode (unmaintained). The gotcha there: ARR's default
response time-out is **30 s** and thumbnail generation can exceed it — raise it to 120 s
or users get a 502 mid-job. The `maxDuration` exports above are inert under IIS.

**3.4 Operational minimums, none of which exist today:** a health endpoint (there is no
`/api/health`), real error logging (19 bare `console.error` calls, no logging or
monitoring dependency), and a backup story for both Neon and the object store.

### Phase 4 — Architectural seam (after beta is up, before more features)

The architecture is sound and needs **no restructuring** — single full-stack app, raw
parameterized SQL, fixed-admin/configurable-editor permission model, portable storage
layer. But there is **no seam between the route handlers and the database**, and that
missing seam is what makes the Phase 1 security work harder than it should be. Fix the
seam and everything after it gets cheaper.

**4.1 Add `lib/apiClient.ts`.** The frontend is 100% client-side: 19/19 pages are
`"use client"`, with **88** hand-rolled `localStorage.getItem("token")` reads and **141**
raw `fetch()` calls and no shared client. This is *why* the token has to live in
`localStorage` — it is read by client JS at 88 sites. One API-client module collapses
that to a single place and turns the later httpOnly-cookie migration into a one-file
change. Highest leverage-to-risk ratio in the codebase.

**4.2 Consolidate the tenant access check.** `WITH RECURSIVE` appears in **16 route
files** and the copies have **drifted into at least three different shapes** —
resource-anchored (`download`, `resources/[id]/metadata`), collection-anchored
(`collections/[id]/files`), and an all-collections root mapping (`search`) — while
`tenantHasCollectionAccess`/`tenantHasResourceAccess` exist in `lib/permissions.ts` and
are used by only a handful of routes. The single most security-critical rule in the
system therefore has **two sources of truth and three implementations**. That is the
mechanism by which a cross-tenant leak eventually ships. Target: three helpers
(resource-anchored, collection-anchored, subtree-mapping), all 16 sites migrated.
Related measurement: **201 of 229** `db.query`/`client.query` calls live in
`app/api/`, only 28 in `lib/`.

**4.3 Then** httpOnly + Secure + SameSite cookie behind a `middleware.ts` gate (there is
no middleware at all today), and only after that consider moving read-heavy pages to
Server Components.

**4.4 Decompose the large components** — `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` is 3,120 lines
(then `admin/media` 1,791, `admin/users` 1,038). Not urgent, but it is the page both
editors and viewers use most, so it is where changes are riskiest.

**Deliberately not scheduled:** a job queue for media processing.
`POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` pulls the whole file into a `Buffer` and spawns
FFmpeg in-request — survivable on a single server (FFmpeg is a subprocess, so the event
loop is not blocked) but it spikes memory on large videos. If it ever becomes a problem,
a DB-backed job table is enough; there is no need to bring Redis back.

| 30 | Import image from URL — Stage 1 (API). New `POST /api/upload/from-url` (requireAdmin), body `{ url, collectionId? }`: securely fetches a public image server-side and stores it exactly like a normal upload (same `resources` INSERT shape as `upload/complete`; MinIO `putObject` — a new non-presigned helper in `lib/storage.ts` alongside the existing presigned-URL ones; links to `collectionId` if given, else Unassigned). Images only — allow-list `image/jpeg`/`png`/`gif`/`webp`; `image/svg+xml` deliberately excluded (XML, can carry scripts). SSRF guardrails, all verified: (1) scheme allow-list, http/https only — `file:`/`ftp:`/`data:` etc. rejected; (2) hostname resolved via DNS, then an **IP allow-list** (not deny-list) rejects anything whose `ipaddr.js` `.range()` isn't plain `unicast` — covers loopback (127/8, ::1), private (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16), link-local incl. the cloud-metadata address 169.254.169.254 (and IPv6 fe80::/10), unique-local, unspecified, multicast, carrier-grade NAT, and IPv4-mapped IPv6 in one check; (3) DNS-rebinding (TOCTOU) defense — uses Node's core `http`/`https` (not `fetch`, to avoid pulling in a second dependency for a custom dispatcher) with a custom `lookup` that pins the socket to the exact pre-validated IP, so the connection can never silently re-resolve to a different address between check and connect; (4) redirects are never auto-followed — each 3xx target re-runs full scheme+DNS+IP validation before being followed, capped at 3 hops; (5) 10s timeout, 15MB size cap enforced by a running byte counter as data arrives (not trusted from `Content-Length` alone); (6) no info leakage — every URL/IP rejection returns the same generic `"URL not allowed"`, resolved IPs never reach the client, internal errors only `console.error`'d server-side. Filename/extension are derived from the verified content-type, not the URL or any server-supplied filename. New dependency: `ipaddr.js@2.4.0` (zero transitive deps). Verified: a real public image (Wikimedia) imports successfully (201, resource row created, MinIO object present); `169.254.169.254`, `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, `file://...`, and non-image URLs all correctly rejected with 400; non-admin token → 403. **Bug found and fixed during testing:** the first `pinnedLookup` implementation didn't honor Node's `{ all: true }` lookup-option call shape (Node expects an array `[{address, family}]` in that mode, not scalar `(address, family)` args) and threw `ERR_INVALID_IP_ADDRESS` on otherwise-valid imports; fixed to detect and return the correct shape for both call signatures. **Known limitation (accepted):** no magic-byte/file-signature check — a malicious server could send non-image bytes under an `image/*` content-type header; not a code-execution risk here since bytes are only stored and later served back as downloads, but noted as a possible future defense-in-depth addition. | ✓ tested |
| 30b | Import image from URL — Stage 2 (UI). "Import from URL" control added on both the Media page (`app/admin/media/page.tsx`) and the collection page (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`), each a text input + "Import" button placed near the existing upload control. Media page: posts `{ url }` (no `collectionId`) → lands Unassigned, same as direct upload there; calls `fetchMedia()` on success so the new file appears immediately. Collection page: posts `{ url, collectionId: id }` → lands in the current collection; refetches `GET /api/collections/[id]/files` on success. Both: client-side non-empty check before submit; busy state disables the button while in flight; on failure the API's `message` (e.g. `"URL not allowed"`, `"URL did not point to a supported image type"`) is shown inline rather than a generic error; input clears on success. Collection-page control is gated the same as its upload control (`canUpload`); Media-page control is admin-only (whole page is admin-gated). No API changes — calls the Stage 30 endpoint unchanged. This closes the loop: Stage 30's SSRF-hardened API is now reachable from the UI, completing the import-from-URL feature end to end. | ✓ tested — API and UI |
| 31 | Breadcrumb real collection name — new `GET /api/collections/[id]` (`app/api/collections/[id]/route.ts`, previously PATCH/DELETE only): `requireAuth`; returns `{ id, name }`; 404 if the collection doesn't exist; non-admins additionally run the same root-ancestor cascade CTE used by the files/download routes and get 403 if their group has no access to the resolved root; admins bypass the check. Collection page (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`): new fetch added to the mount `useEffect` alongside the existing sub-collections and files fetches, sets `collectionName` from the response (silent catch on failure — falls back to the pre-existing `collectionName ?? "Collection"` breadcrumb logic, which was already correct but previously only ever got a real value from the Rename action). This also fixes the bulk-download zip filename (`sanitizeFilename(collectionName ?? "")`, stage 24) to reflect the real collection name from first load instead of only after a rename in that session. | ✓ tested |
| 32 | User avatar / account menu — the top-right "Users" + "Sign out" text buttons on the home page (`app/home/page.tsx`) replaced with a single circular avatar button (initials derived from the JWT `email` claim, e.g. `georges.k@...` → `GK`; falls back to a generic user-icon SVG if no email is present — no fabricated display name). Clicking it opens a dropdown anchored top-right: a non-interactive header row (avatar + email + `groupName` role label), a divider, "Users" (admin-only — reuses the exact same `isAdmin`/`canAdmin` gating and `/admin/users` route the old button used, verbatim, no new condition), and "Sign out" (calls the unchanged `handleSignOut` — same token removal + redirect). Close behavior reuses the app's existing kebab-menu pattern: a shared transparent full-screen backdrop (`fixed inset-0 z-10`, click to close) plus a new Escape-key listener. Presentation and wiring only — no API, SQL, or auth changes. Scoped to `app/home/page.tsx` only (the only file where both buttons appeared together; `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` has its own separate Sign-out-only nav, left untouched — no shared header component exists in `app/layout.tsx`). Verified in-browser for both admin (sees Users + Sign out) and viewer (sees Sign out only). | ✓ tested |
| 33 | Video thumbnails — Stage 1 (schema + generation API), the previously-deferred FFmpeg feature. Schema: nullable `thumbnail_storage_key text` added to `resources`. New dependency `ffmpeg-static` (bundles a real FFmpeg binary — no system install required). `next.config.ts` sets `serverExternalPackages: ["ffmpeg-static"]` so Next/Turbopack doesn't bundle and mangle the binary's path — this was required: without it the bundler rewrote the import to a nonexistent `\ROOT\...` path and `spawn` failed with `ENOENT`; confirmed fixed by inspecting the compiled route output (no inlined `ffmpeg` reference; the real `ffmpeg.exe` path appears correctly in the route's node-file-trace list). The route additionally has a `resolveFfmpegPath()` fallback that reconstructs the path from `process.cwd()` if the imported path doesn't exist on disk, as a second line of defense. `lib/storage.ts` gained `getObject(key)` (returns object bytes as a `Buffer`) — the server has to pull video bytes locally for FFmpeg, unlike normal uploads/downloads which never touch server memory. New `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` (`app/api/resources/[id]/thumbnail/route.ts`, requireAdmin): 404 if the resource is missing; 400 "not a video" if `mime_type` doesn't start with `video/`; if `thumbnail_storage_key` is already set, returns it immediately rather than regenerating (chosen because resources are immutable once uploaded, so a stored thumbnail never goes stale — regenerate-on-demand was not built). Otherwise: downloads the video from MinIO to a uniquely-named temp file (`os.tmpdir()`); runs `ffmpeg-static` via `child_process.spawn` with a fixed **argument array** (never a shell string — no injection surface) to grab one frame ~1s in, scaled to `320:-2`, as JPEG; a 20s timeout kills the process with `SIGKILL` if exceeded; if the video is shorter than the seek point (or the first attempt otherwise fails) it retries once at frame 0 with no `-ss`; the output file is checked non-empty; the JPEG is uploaded to MinIO under `thumbnails/<id>.jpg` and only then is `thumbnail_storage_key` updated (parameterized) — the DB write never happens before the thumbnail is durably stored, so a failure can never leave the resource in a half-updated state. Both temp files (input video + output JPEG) are removed in a `finally` block regardless of outcome. Any failure (fetch error, ffmpeg exit code, timeout, empty output) returns a clean `422 "could not generate thumbnail"` — never crashes, never corrupts the resource. Verified: real frame extracted from a test mp4 (key returned + recorded on the row); no temp files left behind after success or failure; a deliberate ENOENT during path-resolution debugging produced a clean 422 with no leftover files; calling the endpoint again on an already-thumbnailed resource returns the existing key instantly with no re-processing. | ✓ tested |
| 33b | Video thumbnails — Stage 2 (UI: display, auto-generate, manual regenerate). `thumbnail_storage_key` added to the SELECTs in `GET /api/collections/[id]/files` (both admin and non-admin branches) and `GET /api/media`, and to the `FileItem`/`MediaFile` TypeScript interfaces on both pages. Display: the existing image-thumbnail-fetch `useEffect` on both `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` and `app/admin/media/page.tsx` was extended (not replaced) — for a file where `mime_type` starts `video/` **and** `thumbnail_storage_key` is set, it now also fetches `GET /api/cover?key=<thumbnail_storage_key>` → presigned URL → blob → object URL, feeding the same `thumbnailUrls` state the image path already populates, so the existing card JSX (`thumb ? <img> : <type label>`) needed no branching change; a video with no thumbnail yet still shows the type label as before. A small pointer-events-none play-icon overlay renders on top of any video thumbnail so it reads as a video, not a still image. On the media page, the pre-existing `loadedThumbIdsRef` dedup guard was extended to also cover video-with-thumbnail, but deliberately does **not** mark a thumbnail-less video as "loaded," so the fetch fires automatically once a thumbnail key appears (from auto-generate or the manual action below) without needing a full page reload. Auto-generate: after `upload/complete` returns the created resource in both pages' upload flows, if the uploaded file's mime is `video/*` a `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` fires with no `await` and a swallowed `.catch()` — genuinely fire-and-forget, never touches the upload's success/failure counts or its end-of-batch summary; the thumbnail appears on the next list refresh. Manual: a "Generate thumbnail" kebab item, shown only when `mime_type` starts `video/`, added to the file-card kebab on both pages (already admin-gated on both); shows a "Generating…" busy state, and on success writes the returned `thumbnailStorageKey` into that file's local state, which the display effect above then picks up automatically to fetch and render it — no separate manual blob-fetch code was needed; on failure shows a red inline error. Nothing else on either page (filters, select mode, tabs, other kebab items, image lightbox) was touched. Known accepted limitation: the generation endpoint loads the entire video into memory (`getObject` → `Buffer` → temp file) — fine for reasonable file sizes, a memory spike for very large videos; streaming to disk instead is a possible future optimization. No custom-frame-picker — the endpoint always auto-grabs the ~1s frame; a future "choose your own frame" feature is possible but not built. | ✓ tested |

| 40 | Per-asset metadata read/write. New `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` (`requireAuth`; `LEFT JOIN` from `metadata_fields` to `resource_field_data` for the given `resource_id`, so every defined field is returned with `value: null` where unset, ordered `sort_order, id`; 404 if the resource doesn't exist). Non-admin access isolation **reuses the exact cascade CTE from `GET /api/download/[id]`** (not the single-collection CTE used by the collections routes) — walks every collection this resource belongs to (via `collection_resource`) upward to each root, then checks `group_collection_access` for the user's group; 403 if none match; admins bypass. This is the correct primitive because a resource can live in several collections and access should be granted if *any* accessible collection contains it — the collections-page CTE starts from a single collection context and would have quietly checked the wrong thing. `PUT /api/resources/[id]/metadata` (`requireAdmin`; body `{ values: [{ fieldId, value }] }`) runs in one transaction (`BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` via `db.connect()`, mirroring the `upload/complete` transaction pattern): 404 if the resource doesn't exist; every `fieldId` is validated against `metadata_fields` up front and **the whole PUT is rejected with 400** if any is unknown (chosen over silently skipping, to keep the write atomic and honest about what was actually saved); each value is either upserted via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (resource_id, field_id) DO UPDATE SET value = EXCLUDED.value`, or, if blank/whitespace-only, the row is **deleted** rather than storing `''` (keeps `resource_field_data` clean for the Stage 4 search join — a deleted row costs nothing, an empty-string row is still a row to filter). Value capped 5000 chars. Returns the refreshed metadata in the same shape as GET. New shared `components/ResourceMetadata.tsx` — self-contained (fetches its own `/api/resources/[id]/metadata` on mount, no bulk fetch alongside the file list), renders nothing if no field definitions exist at all; for admins, a click-to-edit block (mirrors the description editor's dirty-state/Save/Cancel/error/saved-flash UX, `text` fields as `<input>`, `textarea` fields as multiline) showing every defined field (unset shown as a muted "—"); for clients, read-only and **only** the fields that actually have a value — empty rows are never shown to clients, and if none have a value the component renders nothing. Dropped into `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` (`<ResourceMetadata resourceId={f.id} canAdmin={canAdmin} />`, gated per-viewer) and `app/admin/media/page.tsx` (`canAdmin={true}` — the whole page is already admin-gated) immediately after each page's existing description block, no other card restructuring. Verified live against the running dev server: GET returns all fields with `value: null` for an untouched resource; a viewer (group 3) got 403 on a resource that only lives in a group-2 collection and 200 on one in their own group, editor (group 2) mirrored; non-admin PUT → 403; admin PUT sets a value and it's immediately visible to a group member's GET; clearing via a whitespace-only value deletes the row (value back to `null`); unknown `fieldId` → 400; PUT/GET on a nonexistent resource → 404. No changes to search, `search_vector`, the metadata-fields definition routes, or upload/download logic. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean |
| 41 | Full-text search upgrade — `GET /api/search`'s file matching replaced ILIKE with a **three-branch predicate** inside a `matched` CTE, in both the admin and non-admin paths: `r.search_vector @@ websearch_to_tsquery('english', $q)` (ranked via `ts_rank(r.search_vector, ...)`) **OR** `r.original_filename ILIKE '%'||$q||'%'` (uses the pre-existing trigram index `idx_resources_filename`) **OR** `EXISTS (... resource_field_data rfd INNER JOIN metadata_fields mf ... WHERE mf.searchable = true AND rfd.value ILIKE '%'||$q||'%')` (searchable metadata only — non-searchable fields never match, verified). The metadata `EXISTS` needs no group filter of its own: on the non-admin path the `matched` CTE's `FROM resources r ... INNER JOIN accessible ac ON ac.collection_id = cr.collection_id` runs first, so the predicate only ever decides whether an *already access-filtered* row counts as a match — isolation is inherited, not re-implemented, and `root_finder`/`accessible` are byte-for-byte unchanged from before this stage. `DISTINCT ON (r.id)` inside the CTE still picks one `collection_id` per resource and computes `rank`; wrapping in a CTE was necessary because `DISTINCT ON` forces its own `ORDER BY r.id, ...`, which would otherwise conflict with the outer `ORDER BY rank DESC, id LIMIT 20`. Collection-name search, `q` validation (required/trimmed/400/100-char cap), the 20-result cap, and the response shape (`id, original_filename, mime_type, size_bytes, description, collection_id`) are all unchanged. Landed as two sub-passes: the initial three-signal upgrade, then a same-day fix restoring the `original_filename ILIKE` branch after live testing caught a regression — Postgres's `english` text-search parser tokenizes a dotted filename like `AlexPereira.jpg` as one lexeme (no split on capitalization or the dot), so full-text alone silently lost partial-filename matching (`Pereira`, `OIP` no longer matching by filename, only by an unrelated title/description word). `EXPLAIN` on the bare `ILIKE` showed a sequential scan; forcing `enable_seqscan = off` confirmed `idx_resources_filename` is real and usable (`Bitmap Index Scan`) — the planner is simply and correctly choosing the cheaper plan at the table's current size (18 rows), not failing to use the index. Verified live against the running dev server, isolation checked in both directions for **all three signals**: a unique searchable-metadata value on a group-2-only resource — viewer (group 3) 0 results, editor (group 2) and admin found it, and mirrored for a group-3-only resource; a non-searchable-field value never matched for any role; a filename fragment unique to a group-2-only file (`iu`, and `OIP` matching 3 files) — viewer 0, admin/editor found them. Full-text word matches (`Pereira` via description, `UFC`) and `q` validation/response shape all reconfirmed intact after the filename fix. Only `app/api/search/route.ts` changed; no new index, no new dependency. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean |

| 45 | Upload → "Save and next" metadata workflow — Stage C of the metadata rollout (Stage A was Stage 42, Stage B was Stage 44; all three are now done). `components/ResourceMetadata.tsx` gained two more **additive, optional** props/API alongside Stage 44's `autoEdit`: `hideActions?: boolean` (default `false`) — when true, the internal Save/Cancel footer is not rendered; and an imperative handle via `forwardRef`/`useImperativeHandle` exposing `save(): Promise<boolean>` (the same `handleSave` the internal Save button already called, now also callable from outside). `components/MetadataDrawer.tsx` and the standalone "Edit metadata" page path both pass neither prop nor a ref, so both are byte-for-byte unchanged — the component now has **three** mount sites, not two. `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx` gained an optional queue mode driven entirely by query params (survives a refresh — verified live): `batch` (ordered, comma-separated resource ids), `i` (0-based index), `return` (destination path, restricted client-side to a leading `/` — never trusted as an open redirect). Workflow mode only activates when `batch[i]` actually matches the page's own `[id]`; otherwise it behaves exactly like the pre-existing standalone editor. In workflow mode: the top-bar eyebrow reads "Step {i+1} of {N}" (replacing "Edit metadata"), the top-bar action becomes "Finish later" (replacing "Cancel"), `<ResourceMetadata autoEdit hideActions ref={metadataRef} />` replaces the plain `autoEdit` render, and a page-level action bar renders **Skip** (advances to the next index without saving, or to `return` if already last) and a primary button reading **"Save and next"** (or **"Save and finish"** on the last item) that calls `metadataRef.current?.save()`, only advancing on a truthy result — a failed save (shown inline by `ResourceMetadata`'s own existing error state) leaves the user on the same step. Last-item navigation has no off-by-one: advancing from the final index goes straight to `return`, never to a phantom `N+1` URL. The multi-file upload handlers on both `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` and `app/admin/media/page.tsx` now collect successfully-created resource ids (in upload order) alongside the pre-existing `succeeded`/`failed` bookkeeping; failed uploads never obtain an id and are naturally excluded from the queue. After the existing upload pipeline finishes (presigned URL → PUT → `upload/complete`, per-file progress, fire-and-forget video-thumbnail generation — all untouched), if ≥1 file succeeded the page does `router.push` to `/resources/{firstId}/metadata?batch={ids}&i=0&return={originPath}` (`/collections/[id]` for the collection page, `/admin/media` for the media page); a single-file upload is just a batch of one, so it renders as "Step 1 of 1" / "Save and finish" immediately. | 46 | External share links — admin half only (Stage 1 of 2; the public `/share/...` viewing route is deliberately not built yet). New `shares` table (see Database section) — a capability row: exactly one of `collection_id`/`resource_id` (CHECK constraint, DB-enforced), `access_level` (`view`/`download`), optional `expires_at` (null = never), `label`, `revoked`, `last_used_at` (column exists, not yet written to — no reader exists until Stage 2). Token model: a high-entropy 32-byte `base64url` raw token is generated once; only its **bcrypt hash** (`bcryptjs`, 10 rounds — same as password hashing) is stored in `token_hash`; the public URL shape is `{APP_URL}/share/{share.id}.{rawToken}` — the uuid path segment lets a future Stage-2 lookup fetch exactly one row and bcrypt-compare, instead of hashing every row. `POST /api/shares` (requireAdmin): body `{ collectionId? \| resourceId?, accessLevel, expiresIn: '1d'\|'7d'\|'30d'\|'never', label? }`; exactly-one-target and whitelist checks all return 400; target existence checked (404 if missing); `expiresIn` branch-selected to a computed `Date`/`null` in JS, never interpolated into SQL; response `{ id, url, accessLevel, expiresAt, target }` — **the only place the raw url ever appears**, never logged, never re-fetchable. `GET /api/shares` (requireAdmin): list ordered newest-first, `target: {type, id, name}` resolved via LEFT JOIN to `collections`/`resources`; token/hash never selected. `PATCH /api/shares/[id]` (requireAdmin): only `{ revoked: true }` accepted — un-revoking or editing scope is rejected by design (a changed share should be a new share); 404 if missing. `DELETE /api/shares/[id]` (requireAdmin): hard delete; 404 if missing. UI `app/admin/shares/page.tsx` (new "Shares" sidebar nav item in `components/AppShell.tsx`, admin-only, `dam-glass` styling matching the other admin pages): create-share panel (target-type toggle Collection/Resource — collection is a depth-indented flat dropdown built by recursively walking `GET /api/collections`; resource is a debounced search against `GET /api/media?q=`; access level, expiry with a caution note on "Never", optional label), success state shows the URL once with a copy button and an explicit "you won't see this link again" note; a shares table below with target/access/status (Active/Expired/Revoked, computed client-side from `revoked`+`expiresAt`)/expires/created/last-used columns and confirm-gated Revoke/Delete actions. Kebab deep-link entry points (collection page / file card → pre-targeted create-share flow) were **deferred**, not built — the task allowed deferring this if it complicated the stage; the standalone `/admin/shares` create flow covers both target types already. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; end-to-end via curl for collection-target and resource-target creation, both expiry branches including `never`, all validation 400s (both/neither target, bad accessLevel, nonexistent target id), revoke (idempotent-by-design 400 on re-revoke attempt with `revoked:false`), delete + re-delete 404, non-admin 403; `psql` confirmed `token_hash` in the DB is a real bcrypt hash (`$2b$10$...`), never plaintext; browser-verified via Playwright screenshots (nav item present, form renders, create → success banner with copy button → row appears in table with correct status/label/expiry, zero console errors). Stage 2 (public `/share/[token]` viewing route, unauthenticated access, `last_used_at` writes) is not started. | ✓ tested — API (curl) + UI (Playwright) + DB (psql hash check) |
| 47 | External share links — Stage 2 (public viewing route, completes the feature). New `lib/shares.ts`: `validateShareToken(param)` is the single fail-closed gate every public endpoint calls first — splits `{id}.{rawToken}` on the first `.` (both halves are dot-free by construction: uuid, base64url), fetches the one `shares` row by `id`, `bcrypt.compare`s the raw token against `token_hash`, then checks `revoked`/`expires_at`; returns a discriminated `{status: 'valid'\|'invalid'\|'expired'\|'revoked'}` where **not-found and bad-token both collapse to `'invalid'`** — no oracle distinguishes them anywhere in the stack. Also exports `touchShareLastUsed` (writes `last_used_at = now()` — the column existed since Stage 46 but nothing wrote to it until this stage) and `isResourceInShareScope(share, resourceId)`, the independent anti-scope-escape check: for a resource share, exact id equality; for a collection share, a **downward-only** recursive CTE from the shared collection through `parent_id` to its descendants, joined to `collection_resource` — deliberately never touches `group_collection_access` or any group/user table, since a share's scope is structural (this subtree) not the group-isolation model. `GET /api/share/[token]/route.ts` (no auth guard, public): runs the gate; on `'invalid'` returns 404, on `'expired'`/`'revoked'` returns 410 with that status and no file data; on `'valid'` touches `last_used_at`, resolves the target name, and for a collection share runs the same subtree CTE (`SELECT DISTINCT ... ORDER BY r.created_at DESC`, deduped since a resource can sit in more than one collection within the subtree) to list every in-scope file — response is `{status, accessLevel, expiresAt, target, files:[{id, original_filename, mime_type, size_bytes, hasThumbnail}]}`, never a storage key, token, or hash. `GET /api/share/[token]/file/[resourceId]/route.ts` (public): re-runs the full gate independently (never trusts that a resourceId came from a prior in-scope listing), then calls `isResourceInShareScope` before touching the resource row at all — a valid token for collection A requesting a resourceId that only exists in collection B is rejected here, not just filtered out of the gallery. `mode=download` requires `access_level='download'` server-side (403 otherwise, regardless of what the UI shows) and returns a `getDownloadUrl` presigned URL (`lib/storage.ts`, already 300s/5min, unchanged) with `attachment`-style client-side handling (fetch-blob-then-`<a download>`, matching the existing collection-page single-file-download pattern — MinIO itself isn't asked for `Content-Disposition`). `mode=view`: images get a presigned URL to the original; videos get the presigned URL of `thumbnail_storage_key` **only** (never the original video bytes, regardless of access level); everything else gets no URL (icon-only) on a view-only share, or the original on a download share (`view may preview originals` — but only for non-video/non-image types; a video with a thumbnail always serves the thumbnail even on a download share, per spec). New public page `app/share/[token]/page.tsx` — deliberately outside `AppShell`, no auth guard, no redirect-to-login possible (own `<style>` block duplicating just the `.share-glass` frosted treatment rather than depending on `AppShell`'s auth-gated component): navy header strip with the (still-missing, pre-existing gap) Coperon logo, then a grid of file cards fetched from the two endpoints above — thumbnail/preview (only rendered as an `<img>` when the preview response's own `mimeType` is `image/*`, so a download-share's original-file preview URL for a non-image type like `.txt` shows the file-type icon instead of a broken image — caught and fixed during browser verification, see below), filename, size, and a Download button **only when `accessLevel === 'download'`** (button hidden entirely for view shares, on top of the server-side 403). Distinct friendly states: expired ("This link has expired"), revoked ("This link is no longer available"), invalid (generic "This link is invalid or has expired" — identical copy for bad-token and not-found). A click-to-close lightbox for images only. No middleware file exists in this project (confirmed by its absence), so nothing could have intercepted these routes anyway — the guard is entirely each page/route's own logic, and this page/these routes carry none. Verified thoroughly against the live dev server with real test collections/resources (not just unit-level): valid collection share → gallery shows exactly that collection's files (confirmed against a second, unrelated collection to prove no over-inclusion); valid resource share → exactly the one asset; **scope-escape test** — a valid token scoped to "Album Covers" requesting (via the file endpoint) a resourceId that exists only in the unrelated "UFC" collection → 404, while an in-scope file from the same request → 200 with a presigned URL; bad-token (right id, wrong token), nonexistent id, and a malformed no-dot param all returned byte-for-byte identical `{"status":"invalid"}`/404; revoked share (via the Stage 46 admin `PATCH`) → `{"status":"revoked"}`/410; expired share (`expires_at` moved to the past directly in `psql` for the test) → `{"status":"expired"}`/410, and the file endpoint independently re-denies it too; view-only share's file endpoint with `mode=download` → 403 "Download not permitted for this link"; a download share's `mode=download` → 200 with a presigned URL; presigned URLs confirmed short-lived (`X-Amz-Expires=300` in the query string); `last_used_at` confirmed populated in the DB and reflected back through the Stage 46 admin `GET /api/shares` list after a real page load (both via curl and via a real headless-Chromium session with no login step and no token ever placed in `localStorage`, confirming no redirect to `/` — `page.url()` stayed on `/share/...` throughout). `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; console errors during browser verification were only the pre-existing missing-logo 404 (see tech debt), not a regression. All test share rows created during this stage's verification were deleted afterward; two pre-existing rows the user had already created via the Stage 46 admin UI (visible by their earlier timestamps) were left untouched. | ✓ tested — API (curl, including a real scope-escape attempt against real cross-collection data) + browser (Playwright, anonymous session, no auth) + DB (`last_used_at` + no plaintext token/hash confirmed) |

Schema: `metadata_fields.required boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (`ALTER TABLE`, applied directly — see the direct-DDL list below); all 12 existing fields defaulted `false`, confirmed via `\d metadata_fields`. `POST`/`GET /api/metadata-fields` and `PATCH /api/metadata-fields/[id]` now accept/return `required` (boolean-coerced, same whitelist/409-on-duplicate-name pattern as every other field, requireAdmin on writes); `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` now includes `required` in its shaped rows so the component can see each field's flag. Admin page `app/admin/metadata-fields/page.tsx`: a "Required" toggle styled identically to the existing "Searchable" toggle, in both the create-field form and each row's inline edit, wired through the same dirty-state Save flow. `components/ResourceMetadata.tsx` gained an additive `enforceRequired?: boolean` prop (default `false`) and a new `isValueEmpty(fieldType, value)` helper — "empty" per type: `text`/`textarea`/`date` → blank/whitespace; `checkbox_group`/`tag` → no selections (`parseList(value).length === 0`). When `enforceRequired` is true, `handleSave()` checks every `required` field before the network call; if any are empty it sets a per-field inline error ("This field is required," cleared as soon as that field's value changes), returns `false`, and **never calls the API** — no partial save. `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx` passes `enforceRequired` only on its workflow-mode `<ResourceMetadata>` (alongside the pre-existing `autoEdit hideActions ref={metadataRef}`); the standalone-editor branch passes neither, matching `components/MetadataDrawer.tsx` (unchanged, still passes only `resourceId`/`canAdmin`/`key`) — required is a submission-gate rule for the workflow only, not an edit rule, so casual edits elsewhere are never blocked. "Don't advance on false" needed no new code: the workflow page's `handleSaveAndNext` already had `const ok = await metadataRef.current?.save(); if (!ok) return;` from Stage 45 (Stage C), so a failed validation simply falls through that existing guard. `Skip`/`Finish later` still bypass entirely (neither calls `save()`). The `PUT /api/resources/[id]/metadata` API itself was **not modified** and stays permissive by design — confirmed via `curl`: clearing a `required=true` field's value returned `200` and deleted the row, exactly as for any other field; search (`GET /api/search`) untouched. Verified live end to end (Playwright, real dev server): toggling "Title" required in the admin UI persisted across reload; in the workflow, "Save and finish" with Title blank stayed on the same URL and showed the red asterisk + "This field is required" inline, then filling it and re-saving advanced to the return path normally; the same resource's Title was then cleared and saved successfully via both the standalone editor (`/resources/[id]/metadata`, no `batch` param) and the `MetadataDrawer` (`/admin/media` → Details) with **no** blocking in either — confirming `enforceRequired`'s default-`false` gating holds. This completes the full metadata arc: definitions (Stage 39) → 5 field types (Stage 42) → per-asset values (Stage 40) → drawer (Stage 43) → full-page editor (Stage 44) → Save-and-next workflow (Stage 45) → required enforcement (this stage). | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean; `psql` schema check; API (`curl`, confirms PUT stays permissive); browser (Playwright: admin toggle persistence, workflow block/unblock, drawer + standalone permissive saves) |

SET group_id` into the same dynamic-SET-from-only-fields-present pattern already used by `PATCH /api/resources/[id]` and `PATCH /api/collections/[id]` (Stage 22) — body may now contain `groupId` and/or `name`, each validated and appended to `setClauses`/`params` independently, 400 if neither is present; the pre-existing self-edit block (an admin can't PATCH their own row, to prevent accidentally stripping their own admin access) now covers name edits too for simplicity, so an admin's own display name must be set by a *different* admin or at registration — not considered a gap worth solving this stage. `app/admin/users/page.tsx` gained a "Name" column: a per-row text input on the same dirty-state/Save/Cancel pattern as the existing group `<select>` (independent `pendingNames` state alongside `pendingGroups`; `handleSave` now builds the PATCH body from whichever of the two actually changed, sends neither key if nothing did); disabled on the admin's own row like the group select already was. List views are `.dam-glass` tables; select mode, bulk actions, and kebabs (`toggleSelect`, `toggleSelectAll`, `handleBulk*`, rename/delete/generate-thumbnail/add-to-collection) are defined once per page and shared by both the grid and list JSX — no forked selection logic. `GET /api/collections` gained `subcollection_count`/`file_count` via correlated subqueries (`(SELECT COUNT(*)::int FROM collections sc WHERE sc.parent_id = c.id)` / `... FROM collection_resource cr WHERE cr.collection_id = c.id`) appended to the `SELECT c.*` in **all four** existing branches (admin/non-admin × top-level/child) — placed after the existing `WHERE`/`JOIN`/recursive-CTE logic, which is otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged, so counts ride on the already-access-filtered row set rather than a separate unfiltered lookup. Verified live via `psql` against real data: a group-3 (Viewers) query through the same recursive CTE returned exactly the 4 collections that group has access to (excluding "Admin Only Files" and "Root B (Editors only)"), each with correct scoped counts — confirming isolation held with the added columns. | 59 | Automatic collection covers — `collections.cover_storage_key` (nullable since Stage 16) now auto-populates instead of staying `NULL` until a manual "Change thumbnail". Three write sites, every one gated `AND cover_storage_key IS NULL` (conditional UPDATE, not read-then-write) so an automatic pick can never race another upload or clobber a manually-set (or already-auto-set) cover: (1) `POST /api/upload/complete` — inside the existing `collectionId`-present transaction, immediately after the `collection_resource` INSERT, `UPDATE collections SET cover_storage_key = $1 WHERE id = $2 AND cover_storage_key IS NULL` fires only when the uploaded file's `contentType` starts with `image/` (video/PDF thumbnails don't exist yet at this instant — handled by site 2 instead); (2) `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` — after a video/PDF thumbnail is generated and `thumbnail_storage_key` is written (existing behavior, byte-for-byte unchanged up to that point), a new `UPDATE collections ... FROM collection_resource cr WHERE collections.id = cr.collection_id AND cr.resource_id = $2 AND collections.cover_storage_key IS NULL` sets the new thumbnail as the cover for every still-coverless collection containing that resource — this is what gives a video/PDF-only collection a cover once thumbnails exist, since site 1 couldn't set one at upload time; endpoint's response, idempotent short-circuit, and 422 failure paths are all unchanged, this write only happens on the success path. (3) `DELETE /api/resources/[id]` — before the `resources` row (and its MinIO object) is gone, `UPDATE collections SET cover_storage_key = NULL WHERE cover_storage_key IN ($1, $2)` (the resource's `storage_key` and `thumbnail_storage_key`, either may be `NULL`) clears any collection pointing at that resource's file(s), so it falls back to the folder-glyph placeholder instead of a permanently broken cover — the next image upload (site 1) re-picks. New `scripts/backfill-covers.mjs` — a standalone, manually-run, idempotent one-time script (not an API route, no boot hook): for every collection with `cover_storage_key IS NULL`, picks the oldest `image/*` member resource by `created_at`, falling back to the oldest member resource with a `thumbnail_storage_key`, and applies the same conditional UPDATE; prints a summary (updated / skipped / no-candidate counts). **Run once against the dev DB** — see the "Scripts" section below for the result. No changes to the upload response shape, the thumbnail endpoint's contract/short-circuit/422 behavior, delete semantics (still DB row → MinIO bytes, in that order), access rules, or any UI — `app/home/page.tsx` and the sub-collection grids already rendered `cover_storage_key` when present and fell back to the placeholder otherwise, so nothing needed to change there. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean; live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB/MinIO: first image upload into a coverless collection sets the cover, a second upload doesn't change it, deleting the cover resource clears `cover_storage_key` to `NULL` and the next upload re-picks; a manually-set cover survived an upload into the same collection (confirmed the `IS NULL` guard holds); `scripts/backfill-covers.mjs` run against dev found 25/32 collections coverless, updated 16 (oldest image, else oldest thumbnailed resource), left 9 with no eligible member — re-run immediately after touched 0 (idempotent) |
