| 61 | Media pagination — resolves the reported "files missing from media library" (it was never a data bug: `GET /api/media` had a hardcoded `LIMIT 100`, no offset, and the UI's "100 files" label was the fetched row count, not the library total, so anything past the 100th row was simply never requested). `GET /api/media` now accepts `page` (1-based, default 1) and `pageSize` (whitelisted `[25, 50, 100]`, falls back to 25 on any other value), both bound as query parameters (never interpolated) into `LIMIT $n OFFSET $n`. Response shape is now `{ files, total, page, pageSize }`: `total` is a second parameterized query, `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (... same FROM/JOIN/WHERE/GROUP BY/HAVING as the page query ...) counted`, reusing the identical `whereClause`/`havingClause` strings the page query builds — verified against independent `psql` counts for unfiltered (137), `type=image` (114), `unassigned=true` (0 at the time), and `q=jpg` (114): all exact matches. An out-of-range `page` clamps server-side to the last valid page (`Math.min(requestedPage, lastPage)`, computed from `total`) rather than returning an empty result, and the returned `page` reflects the clamp; the client syncs its own `page` state from that value. Frontend (`app/admin/media/page.tsx`): a numbered pager (windowed with `…` ellipsis for long ranges) below the grid/list, `‹ Prev`/`Next ›` disabled at the ends, "Showing X–Y of {total}" replacing the old fetched-row-count label, and a page-size `<select>` persisted to `dam:pagesize:media` (mount-`useEffect` + `try/catch`, matching `dam:view:*`/`dam:sort:*` — `page` itself is intentionally never persisted). `setPage(1)` fires on every type/search/sort/unassigned/pageSize change — the classic "land on page 7 of a 2-page result" bug, handled explicitly rather than left implicit. Select mode is now per-page by construction (`files` only ever holds the current page, so "Select all" already only selects what's visible) and relabeled "Select all on this page"; a `useEffect` on `[page]` clears `selectedIds` and exits select mode on any page change. Deleting the last item on the last page steps back automatically — no special-cased "if now empty, go back" code was needed: the existing bulk-action `fetchMedia()` refetch already re-syncs `total`/`page` from the server's clamp. Grid and list share one `files` array and one pager block — no forked logic. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean; live against the real dev DB (137 resources): `page=1`/`pageSize=25` → 25 rows; `page=6` (last page) → 12 rows (137 − 125); `page=99` → clamps to page 6, same 12 rows; `pageSize=30` (not whitelisted) → falls back to 25; walked all pages of a 114-image `sort=name_asc` filtered set and collected exactly 114 unique ids with no gaps or duplicates |
| 62 | Filter media by collection — `GET /api/media` accepts an optional `collectionId` (uuid; malformed → 400). Restricts results to that collection and every descendant via a static, parameterized recursive CTE (`WITH RECURSIVE subtree AS (SELECT id FROM collections WHERE id = $n UNION ALL SELECT child.id FROM collections child INNER JOIN subtree s ON child.parent_id = s.id)`) plus `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM collection_resource cr2 WHERE cr2.resource_id = r.id AND cr2.collection_id IN (SELECT id FROM subtree))` — a **fresh `cr2` alias**, deliberately not a condition on the pre-existing `LEFT JOIN collection_resource cr`, whose LEFT-ness is load-bearing for the "Unassigned only" `HAVING COUNT(cr.resource_id) = 0`; constraining that existing join directly would silently turn it into an inner join and break that filter. The subtree-CTE prefix and the same `whereClause` (now including the `EXISTS` predicate when present) are applied to **both** the page query and the `COUNT(*)` query, so the total can't diverge from what's actually paged. A `collectionId` that doesn't exist yields an empty subtree, so `EXISTS` is vacuously false for every resource — `total: 0` and an empty page fall out of the predicate logic with no separate existence check. `collectionId` + `unassigned=true` together is contradictory (a file can't be both in a collection and unassigned) and is left unhandled by design: `EXISTS` requires ≥1 link in the subtree while the HAVING clause requires 0 links total, so the combination naturally yields zero rows rather than an error. Frontend: a depth-indented "Collection" `<select>` in the filter toolbar, built by recursively walking the existing `GET /api/collections` (`parentId` → children, using each row's `subcollection_count` to know when to descend) into a flattened list — a separate fetch/state from the pre-existing top-level-only `allCollections` used by "Add to collection," which is untouched. Selecting a collection resets `page` to 1 and clears "Unassigned only" if it was checked; the checkbox itself is disabled (dimmed, tooltipped) whenever a collection is selected, so the mutually-exclusive combination can't be produced from the UI at all. Not persisted — a collection filter is a transient query, not a display preference, so no new `localStorage` key. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean; a 4-level-deep subtree (54 resources) matched a hand-written ground-truth `psql` query exactly and paged to exactly 54 unique ids with no gaps/duplicates; a leaf collection, a malformed uuid (400), and a well-formed-but-nonexistent uuid (`total: 0`) all verified; `collectionId` + `unassigned=true` verified to return `total: 0` **with a real unassigned file present elsewhere** (deliberately created, confirmed surfaced by `unassigned=true` alone, then deleted) — proving the zero comes from the predicate logic, not an empty dataset; "Unassigned only" alone (no collection filter) re-verified the same way as a regression check, since the dev DB otherwise has zero unassigned files and two coincidental zeros would have proven nothing |
| 63 | Collection detail page pagination — `GET /api/collections/[id]/files` now accepts `page` (1-based, default 1) and `pageSize` (whitelisted `[25, 50, 100]` via `.includes()`, default 25), both bound as `$2`/`$3` into `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`; response shape is now `{ files, total, page, pageSize }`, with `total` a second parameterized query (`SELECT COUNT(*)::int FROM resources r INNER JOIN collection_resource cr ON cr.resource_id = r.id WHERE cr.collection_id = $1`) sharing the exact `FROM`/`JOIN`/`WHERE` of the page query — no `ORDER BY`/`LIMIT`/`OFFSET` — the same convention as Stage 61/62's media pagination. An out-of-range `page` clamps server-side to the last valid page (`Math.min(requestedPage, lastPage)`) and the clamped value is returned. **The non-admin cascade access CTE was not restructured** — confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged by diffing against the pre-edit file; pagination wraps around it rather than altering it. The no-access branch's shape changed from a bare `[]` to `{ files: [], total: 0, page: 1, pageSize }`, with identical denial semantics — it still does not leak that files exist. **Sub-collections remain deliberately unpaginated** — this stage covers the Files section only. Frontend (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`): `page`/`pageSize`/`total`/`filesLoading` state scoped to Files; a single `fetchFiles()` (replacing the old unpaginated `refetchFiles`) shared by the pagination effect, upload, URL-import, and both bulk handlers; `dam:pagesize:collection` persisted (mount `useEffect` + `try/catch`, default 25), `page` itself never persisted; a `useEffect` on `[page]` clears `selectedIds` and exits select mode; select-all relabeled "Select all on this page"; the same pager as media (`‹ Prev` / numbered-with-ellipsis / `Next ›`, "Showing X–Y of {total}", page-size `<select>`) sits under the Files grid/list, shared by both views — no forked logic. **Bulk download-zip / remove / permanent-delete handlers and their endpoints are unchanged** — only their scope narrows to the current page's selection; the server's clamped response on refetch makes "delete the last item on the last page" step back automatically, no special-case code needed. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean; live against a temporary 30-resource collection (cleaned up after): admin `page=1` → 25 rows, `page=2` → 5 rows, `total: 30`; walking both pages collected 30 unique ids with no gaps or duplicates; `page=3` (out of range) clamped to `page: 2` with rows identical to the real page 2; a non-admin granted access to the collection saw the same total/pages as admin; revoking that group's access returned `{ files: [], total: 0, page: 1, pageSize: 25 }` |
| 64 | Widened automatic collection covers — **extends Stage 59, does not replace it.** Diagnostic (`SELECT ... own_files` per coverless collection) confirmed all 9 remaining coverless collections were pure containers (collections holding only sub-collections, zero direct files) — the gap Stage 59 left: candidates were only ever searched among a collection's own files, so a container could never get a cover no matter how many images lived in its descendants. Fixed with a new shared candidate query (`scripts/backfill-covers.mjs`): a recursive `subtree` CTE (static, `$1`-bound, depth-tracked) feeding a 4-way `UNION ALL` — priority 0 = own `image/*`, 1 = own resource with `thumbnail_storage_key`, 2 = descendant `image/*`, 3 = descendant thumbnail — `ORDER BY priority ASC, depth ASC, created_at ASC LIMIT 1`, so a cover prefers the collection's own files, then the *nearest* descendant, not an arbitrarily deep leaf. On upload (`POST /api/upload/complete`), after the existing own-collection auto-cover UPDATE (unchanged), a recursive `ancestors` CTE (same shape as the pre-existing `root_finder` access-check CTE, just walking upward unconditionally) drives one additional set-based `UPDATE collections SET cover_storage_key = $1 WHERE id IN (SELECT id FROM ancestors WHERE id <> $2) AND cover_storage_key IS NULL` — one query, no round-trip loop — so uploading into `ghidini/2026/shoot` also covers `ghidini` if it lacked one. The thumbnail second-chance path (`POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail`) does the equivalent from *all* of a resource's collection memberships at once via a `starts` + `ancestors` CTE, excluding the direct-membership rows (already handled by its own pre-existing guarded UPDATE). **Every write everywhere keeps `AND cover_storage_key IS NULL`** — critically, this guards each ancestor row *independently*, so propagation does not stop at the first already-covered ancestor; a still-coverless grandparent beyond a manually-covered parent still gets covered in the same query (verified explicitly, see right). **Delete path needed no change** — its existing `WHERE cover_storage_key IN ($1, $2)` matches by value, not by direct membership, so it already clears an inherited ancestor's cover exactly as it clears the original collection's. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean; **live backfill re-run against the dev DB: the 9 coverless collections identified by the diagnostic went to 0, all 9 covered from a descendant (0 from own files)** — matches the diagnostic finding exactly; an immediate second re-run found 0 coverless (idempotent); `ghidini` itself confirmed to now carry a real cover pulled from its subtree. Synthetic hierarchy ROOT3 → MID3 (manual cover) → LEAF3, real upload into LEAF3 through the actual `/api/upload/url` → MinIO PUT → `/api/upload/complete` pipeline: LEAF3 got the new cover, MID3's manual cover was untouched, and ROOT3 — coverless, one level *beyond* the already-covered MID3 — still got the new cover in the same request, proving propagation skips a covered ancestor rather than halting there. A resource linked to two sibling branches under one shared coverless root produced exactly one `UPDATE` row (the root), reached via two CTE paths without duplication. Deleting the resource behind the propagated cover cleared it from both LEAF3 and ROOT3 while leaving MID3's manual cover alone. All temporary collections/resources cleaned up afterward. |

Older shares don't store a retrievable link — recreate them above to get a copyable one." **Validation is unchanged and does not use decryption:** `lib/shares.ts`'s `validateShareToken` still only reads `token_hash` and `bcrypt.compare`s it — confirmed by inspection and by the public route still resolving a real share via the hash path, with `token_encrypted` never referenced there. `SHARE_TOKEN_KEY` is a required, must-be-backed-up secret as of this stage (see "Deployment notes"): losing or rotating it makes every existing `token_encrypted` value permanently undecryptable (Copy-link breaks for those rows), though the hash-validated public link itself is unaffected either way, since that path never depends on this key. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean; live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB (dev server restarted for the new env var, per the established `.env.local`-loads-at-startup convention): created a real share, confirmed `hasLink: true` and zero `token_hash`/`token_encrypted` occurrences anywhere in the list response, the link endpoint returned a URL byte-identical to the one-time creation URL, the public route still validated it via the hash path, a viewer token got 403 and no token got 401 on the link endpoint, a corrupted `token_encrypted` failed closed (500, not a wrong URL), a simulated old share (`NULL`) got 409 and `hasLink: false`; both test shares deleted afterward |
| 73 | Filename dedupe on create — Windows-Downloads-style: uploading `photo.jpg` into a collection that already has one now stores `photo (1).jpg`, then `photo (2).jpg`, etc. New `lib/filenames.ts`: `splitFilename(name)` splits on the **last** dot only (`my.file.name.jpg` → base `my.file.name`, ext `.jpg`); a dot at index 0 or no dot at all (`.gitignore`, `README`) means no real extension — the whole name is the base, so the suffix still lands before anything meaningful. `dedupeFilename(desiredName, existingNames)` compares case-insensitively (`Photo.jpg` collides with `photo.jpg`) and **never parses an existing "(N)" out of the incoming name** — an incoming `photo (1).jpg` that collides becomes `photo (1) (1).jpg`, not `photo (2).jpg`, so a legitimately-named file that happens to already look suffixed is never misread as one. `resolveUniqueFilename(executor, collectionId, desiredName)` does the scoping query (one query, not a round trip per candidate N): `collectionId` present → names of resources `JOIN`ed to that collection via `collection_resource`; `collectionId` null → names of resources with `NOT EXISTS` a `collection_resource` row at all (the same "Unassigned" set `GET /api/media` uses) — free N computed in JS from the fetched name list. Wired into both create paths that had it duplicated: `POST /api/upload/complete` (both the `collectionId` branch, already transactional, and the previously-non-transactional Unassigned branch — now wrapped in `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` too, so the dedupe check runs immediately before the insert on the same client either way) and `POST /api/upload/from-url` (same helper, same two branches; the dedupe only ever touches the DB-stored `original_filename` — the `uploads/<timestamp>-<filename>` storage key still derives from the original undeduped name, untouched, exactly as required). Nothing else changed: upload response shape, presigned/PUT flow, access checks, thumbnail generation, and the auto-cover side effects are all byte-for-byte the same as before this stage. Downloads and the bulk-download zip need no extra plumbing — both already read `original_filename` straight from the API response. **Known limitation, accepted, not fixed this stage:** dedupe only runs at create time. `POST /api/resources/[id]/collections` ("Add to collection," Stage 26/27) does not rename — `original_filename` lives on the resource row, shared across every collection it's linked into, so renaming at link time would silently rename the file in every other collection it already belongs to. A resource added to a second collection that happens to already contain a same-named file will show that collision undeduped until a manual rename. **Residual TOCTOU, accepted, not engineered around:** the scoping SELECT and the INSERT run in the same transaction/client but under default READ COMMITTED, so two genuinely concurrent requests could both see a name free and both insert it — uploads are sequential in this app's UI so the window is narrow in practice; no unique constraint or advisory lock was added for it. | ✓ tested — `dedupeFilename` verified against `photo.jpg`→`photo (1).jpg`→`photo (2).jpg`, `my.file.name.jpg`, `README`, `.gitignore`, case-insensitive `Photo.jpg` vs `photo.jpg`, and the `photo (1).jpg` collision→`photo (1) (1).jpg` non-parsing rule; `tsc --noEmit` clean. `next build` not verified this session — sandbox has no network access to fetch Google Fonts (`next/font/google` used by `AppShell`/`AuthShell`/share pages), a pre-existing environment limitation unrelated to this change, not a regression it introduced. |

| 75 | Dark-mode Stage 2 — the toggle, the palette, and the transition. Completes the arc Stage 74 prepped. **Palette:** a `[data-theme="dark"]` block in `globals.css` redefines every Stage 74 token — `--color-ink #E6EDF5`, `--color-slate #9BAAB9`, `--color-line #2A3A4D`, `--color-mist #16222F`, `--color-content-base #0D1620`, `--color-card #131E2B`, `--color-surface-tint #16232F`, `--color-surface-tint-2 #1B2836`, `--color-border-soft #2F4459`, `--color-selected-ring #3A5A7A`, `--color-selected-tint #1C2E42`, `--color-table-header #16222F`, `--color-input-border #33475C` — plus `--color-brand #1878C4`/`--color-brand-hover #136099` (brightened from the light-mode blue so brand text/links clear AA against the new dark surfaces; `--color-brand-dark`/`--color-brand-mid`/`--color-brand-light` are deliberately **not** redefined, see Sidebar below). Tailwind's `dark:` variant is reconfigured via `@custom-variant dark (&:where([data-theme="dark"], [data-theme="dark"] *));` — the **selector strategy**, keyed off the explicit attribute, not `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` (which Stage 74 already removed) — so `dark:` utilities follow the app's own toggle, never the OS. **Glass:** `--glass-bg`/`--glass-bg-fallback` (+ topbar/light variants) get dark values with the base channel inverted white→near-ink at the same alpha (`rgba(255,255,255,0.9)` → `rgba(15,27,42,0.9)`, etc.) — one edit, since Stage 74 already consolidated every `.dam-glass` variant to read these variables. **Aurora:** the three blob `opacity` values in `components/AppShell.tsx` were pulled into `--aurora-opacity-1/2/3` custom properties (`:root` defaults unchanged: 0.28/0.3/0.22) so `[data-theme="dark"]` can retune them (0.16/0.14/0.12) — tuned by eye against the live dark render; blob positions/colors/blur untouched. **Semantic colors:** `dark:` variants added to every red/amber/emerald/green Tailwind utility in the app (errors, the amber "Unassigned" badge, share Active/Expired status, "Saved" confirmations) and to all 9 of `FileTypeIcon`'s per-type accents, each brightened toward its existing hue's 400-weight shade (e.g. `text-red-600 dark:text-red-400`) — the hue coding is unchanged, only legibility on dark. **Toggle:** a "Dark mode" item in the `AppShell` account-menu dropdown (all roles, no gating), a track-and-knob switch matching the existing Searchable/Required toggle styling; click flips `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` and persists to `localStorage['dam:theme']` (`'light'`\|`'dark'`) via the same mount-`useEffect` + `try/catch` pattern as every other persisted preference in the app. **Anti-flash:** a blocking (non-async, non-deferred) inline `<script>` added to `app/layout.tsx`'s `<head>` reads `dam:theme` and sets `dataset.theme` synchronously, before `<body>` is even parsed — confirmed by inspecting the served HTML source (the script is the last node in `<head>`, ahead of `<body>`) and by a live persistence test (theme was already `"dark"` ~20ms after both a fresh navigation and a hard reload, both from a real `localStorage` value, not a race). Without this the OS/browser default (light) would paint first on every load for a dark-mode user — see the new "Conventions that bite" entry. **Transition:** `toggleDarkMode` captures the toggle button's `getBoundingClientRect()` centre and, when `document.startViewTransition` exists **and** `prefers-reduced-motion` doesn't match `reduce`, wraps the theme flip in a View Transition and animates a `clip-path: circle(...)` wipe (0→viewport-covering radius, 450ms ease-in-out) on `::view-transition-new(root)` by hand — the browser's default cross-fade on `::view-transition-old/new(root)` is disabled via a small `globals.css` rule so the two animations don't run at once. Otherwise (no View Transitions support, or reduced motion): instant flip, deliberately with **no CSS color transition** as a fallback — interpolating ~15 independent tokens produces a contrast-dipping smear mid-transition, and `backdrop-filter` doesn't interpolate at all, so instant is the correct baseline, not a degraded one. Verified live (Playwright + Edge, ad hoc + removed after, manifest diff-confirmed clean): the circular wipe fires and was captured mid-animation as an expanding dark circle from the toggle's position under normal motion; with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` emulated, `document.startViewTransition` was confirmed never called and the flip was instant with no wipe artifact. **Deliberately not themed (intentional, not gaps):** the login illustration panel (`login-mm2.jpg`) stays light — it's a photograph with a tuned white legibility gradient baked into its overlay, and a light image panel beside a dark form is a normal split-screen composition, not a missed spot; PDF thumbnails keep their white plate — it's baked into the stored JPEG server-side (`ctx.fillStyle = '#FFFFFF'` in the thumbnail route, Stage 33/53) and a real PDF page is white paper, so a dark PDF thumbnail would misrepresent the actual asset (Google Drive does the same); real image/video thumbnails are content, not chrome, and were never in scope; the sidebar is unchanged — already `--color-brand-dark` navy with white text in light mode, so it's already correct as a permanently-dark panel and needed no `dark:` treatment at all (this is also why `--color-brand-dark`/`--color-brand-mid`/`--color-brand-light` aren't redefined in the dark block — redefining them would have *changed* the one thing meant to stay constant). `bg-black/80` (the lightbox) and native `window.confirm()` needed no work either way. **Toggle-label fix (small follow-up, same session, folded in here rather than its own stage row — it's a one-file wording/aria correction to the exact control this stage just shipped, not a separable feature):** the account-menu item originally always read "Dark mode" with a fixed moon/sun pairing tied to current state, which was confusing in dark mode (still said "Dark mode" while already dark). Fixed to name the action: light theme → "Dark mode" + moon icon (clicking goes dark); dark theme → "Light mode" + sun icon (clicking goes light); `aria-label` reads `"Switch to dark mode"`/`"Switch to light mode"` to match; `aria-pressed={darkMode}` unchanged, since it correctly describes current state independent of the label wording. Only `components/AppShell.tsx` touched for the fix. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean (build ran to completion this pass; an earlier attempt in this doc-audit session was cut short by a dropped connection, re-run confirmed clean). | 78 | Editor metadata access (team-approved) — opens per-asset metadata authoring to Editors (`canUpload`, not `canAdmin`), scoped to assets in collections their group can access; fixes the live bug where an editor's upload redirected into the (previously admin-only) Save-and-next metadata workflow and hit "Not authorized." New `lib/permissions.ts` exports `canEditResourceMetadata(user, resourceId)`: admin → unconditionally true; editor → true only if the resource is linked (via `collection_resource`) to a collection whose root carries a `group_collection_access` grant for the user's group — reusing the exact `root_finder` cascade already used by `GET /api/download/[id]` and (inline) `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata`'s non-admin read check, factored into a shared `groupHasResourceAccess` helper rather than a second, looser variant; everyone else (viewers, no-permission accounts) → false. Applied to `PUT /api/resources/[id]/metadata` and `GET /api/resources/[id]` (both `requireAdmin` → `requireAuth` + the scope check, 403 after the existing 404 existence check — matching the file's own established order). `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` needed no change — it already ran the identical cascade for reads, open to any user with resource access (including Viewers, whose read-only access is unaffected and not governed by this new helper). `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx`'s gate widened from admin-only to `canAdmin || canUpload` (`notAdmin` state renamed to `notAuthorized` for accuracy), with a new 403 branch on the asset fetch reusing the same "Not authorized" UI — an editor without scope on a *specific* resource still lands on a clean denial screen rather than a broken page. `ResourceMetadata`/`MetadataDrawer`: the `canAdmin` prop renamed to `canEdit` throughout (both components, every call site) — callers now pass `canAdmin || canUpload` (or `true` where the page already scope-verified via its own gate/API call). The collection-detail page's "Edit metadata" kebab item (grid and list views) widened from `canAdmin &&` to `(canAdmin || canUpload) &&` to match — an editor viewing a file card on a collection page they already have access to is, by construction, always in scope for that file, so no extra client-side check was needed there. Deliberate scope decisions: assets in no collection stay admin-only (no "or I uploaded it" clause — a second access path would be a new leak surface, and the Media library that surfaces unassigned files is admin-only anyway, so editors can't reach them regardless); field *definitions* (`/admin/metadata-fields`) stay admin-only — editors author per-asset values via the kebab/drawer, never the field-definition sidebar page. | 79 | Post-creation collection access editing — closes the long-standing gap that access could only be set once, at creation. Investigation first (recorded here since it decided the whole build): `group_collection_access` is a plain `(group_id, collection_id)` table with **no schema constraint** tying grants to root collections — it is root-only **by query shape, not by schema**. Confirmed three ways: every existing grant row in the dev DB points at a `parent_id IS NULL` collection; `POST /api/collections`'s sub-collection (`parentId` present) path never writes a `group_collection_access` row at all, by design — access is inherited from the root ancestor; and every consuming `root_finder`/cascade CTE across the app (collections, files, download, ancestors, search, metadata routes) walks a collection **up** to its root before joining `group_collection_access` — a grant row on a non-root `collection_id` would simply never be joined by any of them, silently inert. Also confirmed: `requireAdmin` (`lib/session.ts`) gates purely on the JWT's own `canAdmin` claim and never touches `group_collection_access`, so admins were never dependent on a grant row — no lockout guard was needed for "remove all groups." New `GET`/`PUT /api/collections/[id]/access` (`app/api/collections/[id]/access/route.ts`, both `requireAdmin`): a shared `resolveRoot()` walks any given id to its root via the same recursive-CTE shape as `GET /api/collections/[id]/ancestors`; `GET` returns `{ collectionId, isRoot, root: {id, name}, groups: [{id, name, granted}] }` (groups = `user_groups` minus Pending, id 4 — the same exclusion the "New collection" create form already applies, kept consistent rather than diverging); `PUT` (body `{ groupIds: number[] }`) does a **whole-set replace in one transaction** — `DELETE FROM group_collection_access WHERE collection_id = $1 AND NOT (group_id = ANY($2::int[]))` then `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (group_id, collection_id) DO NOTHING` per requested id — validates every id against the same Pending-excluded whitelist (400 on unknown/non-grantable ids), and rejects a write on a non-root id with 400 (defense-in-depth: the UI never sends this for a sub-collection, but the API stays honest about what it just did rather than silently redirecting to the root). Removing every group is deliberately allowed and un-guarded — confirmed above that it can never lock an admin out. UI (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`): an "Access" item added to the top-bar collection kebab and to both the grid and list sub-collection card kebabs, all opening one shared modal (plain top-level `fixed` overlay, positioned the same as the existing lightbox — **not** nested inside a `.dam-glass` card, so no portal was needed here, unlike the collection-actions kebab (Stage 77) or `CollectionTreePicker` (Stage 71)). The modal always fetches `GET .../access` for whatever id opened it and renders per the response, never per where it was opened from: editable checkboxes with Save/Cancel (confirm on save, worded differently when the resulting set is empty) when `isRoot` is true, or a read-only "Access is inherited from `<root>`" view with a link to the root and the root's granted groups as chips when it's false — so a sub-collection never shows an editable control that wouldn't actually do anything. `app/home/page.tsx` deliberately untouched: its collection cards have no kebab menu at all today (no Rename/Delete either), so adding Access there alone would mean building a new menu system from scratch — out of scope; every root collection remains one click away from its own detail page, where Access is fully functional (see the new "Known characteristics" note below). No schema change. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB (not just unit-level): a scratch root + sub-collection were created — root's access is editable, sub's is read-only-inherited and points at the real root; `PUT` on the sub-collection → 400; `PUT` with Pending (id 4) → 400; a disposable Viewers account was registered and promoted, confirmed it could see a root granted to Viewers, then the admin revoked Viewers' grant (`PUT groupIds: []`) and **the same viewer was denied on the very next request (403) with no re-login or token refresh**; the admin remained fully unrestricted the whole time despite zero groups being granted; re-granting Viewers restored the viewer's access immediately; a combined grant (`[Editors, Viewers]`) round-tripped correctly with Administrators correctly still unchecked. All scratch collections and the temp account were deleted afterward. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; live end-to-end via a real dev-server/DB verification script (scratch root/sub create, root-editable vs sub-inherited, non-root PUT 400, Pending PUT 400, real disposable-Viewer grant/revoke/re-grant with immediate effect and no re-login, admin unaffected at zero grants, cleanup confirmed) |
| 80 | `CollectionTreePicker` dark-mode opacity fix — the portal-rendered tree panel was translucent in dark mode, unlike every other dropdown in the app (collection kebab, breadcrumb overflow, etc.), which are plain opaque `bg-card`. Root cause: the panel's `className` carried both `dam-glass` and `bg-card`; `.dam-glass` sets a translucent `background` (`--glass-bg`, plus `backdrop-filter`) that, at equal CSS specificity, wins over the `bg-card` utility by source order in the compiled stylesheet — so whatever card image sat behind the portal showed through. Fixed by dropping `dam-glass` from that one div in `components/CollectionTreePicker.tsx`, keeping `border-line`, `shadow-lg`, and `rounded-[10px]` exactly as they were — the panel is now plain opaque `bg-card` (`#131E2B` dark / `#FFFFFF` light via the existing theme-aware token), matching every other dropdown rather than the frosted-glass content cards `dam-glass` is meant for. Presentation only — the tree fetch/expand/collapse logic, the per-node lazy-load cache, and the trigger-rect `position: fixed` computation are all byte-for-byte unchanged; nothing else in the file was touched. Verified without Playwright or a screenshot suite: pulled the actual compiled `globals.css` from the running dev server and rendered a static side-by-side comparison page — the exact original class string (`dam-glass ... bg-card ...`) next to the new one (`bg-card` only) — over an identical busy background under `data-theme="dark"`, screenshotted via Edge's native headless mode (`msedge --headless=new --screenshot`, the browser binary only, no automation framework). The "before" panel is visibly translucent with the background bleeding through; the "after" panel is fully opaque solid navy, matching every other dropdown. Scratch HTML/CSS/screenshot files deleted afterward. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; visually verified via a compiled-CSS before/after comparison rendered with Edge headless (no Playwright, no screenshot suite) |
| 81 | EXIF/IPTC/XMP metadata auto-fill on upload. New `metadata_fields.exif_source text` column (nullable = no mapping). New `lib/exifSources.ts` (pure data, no server deps — safe for the "use client" admin page): a fixed whitelist of 23 sources (`iptc.ObjectName`, `iptc.Caption-Abstract`, `iptc.Keywords`, `iptc.By-line`, `iptc.Credit`, `iptc.Copyright`, `iptc.City`, `iptc.Country-PrimaryLocationName`, `iptc.DateCreated`, `xmp.title`, `xmp.description`, `xmp.subject`, `xmp.creator`, `xmp.rights`, `exif.DateTimeOriginal`, `exif.Make`, `exif.Model`, `exif.LensModel`, `exif.ISO`, `exif.FNumber`, `exif.ExposureTime`, `exif.FocalLength`, `exif.ImageWidth`, `exif.ImageHeight`) as `{value, label, appliesTo: fieldType[]}`, plus `isValidExifSource`/`exifSourceAppliesTo`. GPS deliberately excluded (privacy-sensitive, no location field to hold it). New dependency `exifr` (pure JS, parses EXIF/IPTC/XMP) — confirmed via the compiled route's chunk output that its code is fully inlined by Turbopack with zero dangling `require("exifr")`, so unlike `ffmpeg-static`/`pdfjs-dist`/`@napi-rs/canvas` it did **not** need adding to `serverExternalPackages` (no filesystem-path-at-import-time, no native binary). New `lib/exif.ts` (server-only): `extractMetadata(buffer, mimeType)` calls `exifr.parse` with `mergeOutput:false` (segments stay separated: IPTC under `iptc` with exifr's own unhyphenated names e.g. `Byline`/`Caption`/`Country`, XMP Dublin-Core under a top-level `dc` key not `xmp`, Make/Model/ImageWidth/ImageHeight under `ifd0`, the rest of EXIF under `exif`) and `gps:false`; wrapped in try/catch, returns `{}` on a corrupt file or an image with no embedded metadata at all (exifr returns `undefined`) — never throws. `coerceExifValue(raw, fieldType, options)` handles per-type coercion: text/textarea unwrap XMP LangAlt `{lang,value}` objects and cap ~2000 chars; tag/checkbox_group unwrap to a string array and `", "`-join (matching the existing serialization convention), with checkbox_group additionally filtered to only values already present in the field's own `options` (never inventing an option); numbers (ISO, FNumber, etc.) stringified for text fields. **Date handling (the classic EXIF-date pitfall, handled deliberately):** exifr revives some tags (`DateTimeOriginal`) into a native `Date` built via *local* `Date` methods (`new Date(y, m-1, d)` + `setHours/Minutes/Seconds`, confirmed by reading exifr's own `reviveDate()` source) — reading it back with UTC getters/`toISOString()` would re-apply a timezone conversion and can shift the date by a day near midnight, so `coerceExifValue` always reads such a `Date` with local getters (`getFullYear`/`getMonth`/`getDate`) to exactly undo the local construction; IPTC `DateCreated` is a different shape again (an unrevived raw `"CCYYMMDD"` digit string), parsed with its own regex. Empirically verified end-to-end (not just unit-level): a real photo tagged at `2023:11:02 00:45:12` local time round-trips through exifr as `2023-11-01T22:45:12.000Z` (a UTC day-boundary shift, since the dev machine's TZ offset is +2h) but `coerceExifValue` correctly recovers `2023-11-02` through the live upload route, not the wrong `2023-11-01` a naive UTC-based implementation would produce. `applyExtractedMetadata(resourceId, buffer, mimeType)`: looks up every `metadata_fields` row with a non-null `exif_source`, and for each one with a usable coerced value, `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM resource_field_data WHERE resource_id=$1 AND field_id=$2)` — same never-clobber guard shape as the auto-cover feature's `cover_storage_key IS NULL`; swallows every failure (bad EXIF, DB hiccup) so a bad file can never fail the upload it's attached to. Wired inline (not fire-and-forget) into both `POST /api/upload/complete` (fetches the just-uploaded bytes back via `getObject` after `COMMIT`, since the backend never sees the file directly — it's PUT to MinIO straight from the browser) and `POST /api/upload/from-url` (reuses the buffer already in memory from the SSRF-guarded fetch, no redundant `getObject` round-trip) — both call it after their transaction commits but before the 201 response returns, so the Save-and-next workflow's first render already has the values. Admin API: `POST`/`PATCH /api/metadata-fields` accept `exifSource` (whitelist- and appliesTo-validated server-side against the field's effective type — rejects both an unrecognized string and a recognized-but-wrong-type source, e.g. `iptc.Keywords` on a `text` field; a field-type change that leaves the current mapping inapplicable clears it, mirroring the existing options-clear-on-type-change behavior); `GET` returns it. Admin UI (`app/admin/metadata-fields/page.tsx`): new "Auto-fill from" column/dropdown in both the add-field form and each row's inline edit, offering "None" plus `EXIF_SOURCES` filtered to the row's current field type; switching field type to one the current selection no longer applies to resets it to "None" client-side (server-side clears it too if not resubmitted). Verified end-to-end against the real dev DB and a real dev-server upload (no Playwright, no screenshot suite — this is a data-pipeline feature, not a visual one): mapped the app's actual Title/Caption/Keywords-Subject/Keywords-Other/Credit/Date fields, ran a real tagged JPEG through the full presigned-URL → PUT-to-MinIO → `upload/complete` pipeline — Title/Caption/Credit landed correctly, `Keywords - Subject` (checkbox_group, options include "Woman"/"Family"/etc.) kept only "Woman, Family" and correctly dropped a tagged "NotARealOption" keyword that isn't one of the field's options, `Keywords - Other` (tag) got "wharf, dusk" from `xmp.subject`, `Date` got the correctly-normalized value described above; a metadata-stripped photo uploaded clean with every mapped field left `null`; a non-image byte stream declared as `image/jpeg` uploaded successfully (201) with the extraction failure visibly logged and swallowed, not thrown; re-running `applyExtractedMetadata` a second time on the same already-populated resource, after manually overwriting one field to a value distinct from what the source image would produce, left that manual value untouched and changed nothing else; `POST /api/upload/from-url` against a real externally-hosted EXIF-tagged photo (a Canon EOS 40D sample from a public GitHub test-fixture repo) populated a temporary `exif.Make`-mapped field with "Canon" and `Date` with the correctly-parsed non-edge-case `2008-05-30`, confirming parity with the `upload/complete` path. Test resources, the temporary field, and the test exif_source mappings on the real fields were all cleaned up afterward. No backfill for the existing ~137 files (deliberately out of scope, same follow-up shape as `backfill-covers.mjs`/`regenerate-thumbnails.mjs`). No GPS mapping (deliberate, privacy). | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean (including confirming `exifr` needs no `serverExternalPackages` entry); verified against the real dev DB, MinIO, and dev server with real tagged photos, no Playwright, no screenshot suite |
| 82 | Server-side image thumbnails — closes the "real image thumbnails" gap noted since Stage 33 (images were the last asset type whose card fetched the full original). Extended the existing `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` route (no new endpoint) with a third branch alongside video/PDF: `isImage` gated on `SUPPORTED_IMAGE_MIME_TYPES = {image/jpeg, image/png, image/gif, image/webp}` (the same 4 raster types `upload/from-url` already allow-lists; `image/svg+xml` deliberately excluded — XML, can carry scripts, same reasoning as from-url — even though `@napi-rs/canvas` can technically rasterize a simple SVG buffer, confirmed by testing it directly). New `renderImageThumbnail()`: `loadImage()` (already installed, already in `serverExternalPackages`) → scale to `IMAGE_THUMBNAIL_LONG_EDGE = 640` on the long edge, **never upscaling** (`Math.min(1, target/longEdge)`) → white-fill (JPEG has no alpha; source may be PNG/GIF/WEBP with transparency, same reasoning as the PDF path's white fill) → `canvas.encode('jpeg', 0.8)`. Shares the exact `getObject → render → putObject → thumbnails/<id>.jpg → DB-write` tail the video/PDF branches already used — that tail, `?force=true`, the 422-on-failure catch, and the second-chance auto-cover/ancestor-propagation queries are all byte-for-byte unchanged, just reached from a third `if/else if/else` arm instead of an `if/else`. **The EXIF-orientation "classic bug" turned out to already be solved for free:** the task's plan was to read `Orientation` via `exifr` (already installed, Stage 81) and manually rotate — building that, a real bug surfaced first: `@napi-rs/canvas`'s `loadImage()` already auto-rotates according to EXIF orientation internally (confirmed empirically, not assumed — a synthetic marker image built with an unambiguous "blue square top-left, arrow pointing up" reference, deliberately mis-rotated at the pixel level and tagged `Orientation=6/8/3` via exiftool's `-Orientation#=N` numeric write, round-tripped through `loadImage` + a **zero-transform** `drawImage` and rendered upright every time; repeated against real camera-orientation test photos (`recurser/exif-orientation-examples`, explicit "top/bottom/left/right" labels) for orientations 1/3/6/8, same result). Manually rotating on top — which was actually written and tested first — **double-rotated** the output (caught via `image.width`/`.height` already reflecting the corrected, swapped dimensions before any manual transform ran) and was removed; `renderImageThumbnail()` deliberately does not touch `Orientation` at all, with a code comment recording why, so a future reader doesn't "fix" this by re-adding rotation. Widened `supportsGeneratedThumbnail()` (the shared video/PDF/image-thumbnail-applicability helper, independently duplicated in `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` and `app/admin/media/page.tsx`) to include the same 4 image types — this one change cascades to the fire-and-forget post-upload trigger and the kebab "Generate/Regenerate thumbnail" visibility on both pages with no further edits, since both already gated on this helper. Added the same trigger to both pages' `handleImportFromUrl` (previously **no** thumbnail trigger existed there at all, since URL-import only ever created images and images had no thumbnail pipeline until now). **Display-path rewrite, the part that's bitten before, done separately on every surface (none shared an effect):** `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` and `app/admin/media/page.tsx` (`admin/media`'s effect also has a `loadedThumbIdsRef` dedup guard, Stage 33b) both flipped their thumbnail-fetch branch from "image → always fetch original" to "has `thumbnail_storage_key` → fetch `/api/cover?key=` (any type); else if image → fetch original as a fallback" — the fallback is deliberately *not* dedup-guarded on `admin/media` so a later-arriving thumbnail (fire-and-forget lands, or a list refresh) naturally upgrades the card, same pattern already used there for thumbnail-less videos. `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx`'s single preview effect got the identical restructure. **The lightbox/preview-vs-thumbnail split (verified explicitly, per the task's own warning that this is the recurring bug):** both `collections/[id]` and `admin/media` had their lightbox `onClick` reusing the same state (`thumbnailUrls[f.id]`) the card's `<img>` now shows — for an image with a generated thumbnail this would open the *small* derivative in the lightbox, not the original. Fixed with a new `openLightbox(f)` in both files: for images, fetches `/api/download/[id]` fresh **on demand, only when the lightbox is actually opened** (not eagerly per-card, which would defeat the whole point of switching cards to thumbnails) before setting the lightbox state; video/PDF (no full-res image equivalent to show in an `<img>` lightbox) keep showing their already-loaded thumbnail, unchanged. The public share page (`app/share/[token]/page.tsx` + `app/api/share/[token]/file/[resourceId]/route.ts`) needed a third server-side mode: `mode=view` (image branch) now prefers the thumbnail, same as everywhere else, but its lightbox previously reused that same `view` URL — and pre-feature, `view` for images returned the full original **unconditionally, with no `accessLevel` gate** (the view-only-share anti-casual-saving mechanism, Stage 69, is a client-side `share-no-save`/`draggable=false`/`onContextMenu` deterrent, not a resolution restriction). Reusing `mode=download` for the lightbox would have been wrong — that mode 403s for view-only shares, which would have been a real regression (silently shrinking what a view-only share could ever show). Added a third mode, `mode=original` (images only, 400 otherwise), that preserves that exact old no-gate reach; the page's new `openLightbox()` fetches it on click, mirroring the other two pages' pattern, and the grid still shows the light `view`-mode thumbnail. `handleDownload` (all three internal-app pages) and `mode=download` (share page) were already independent of `thumbnailUrls`/`previews` — confirmed unchanged by reading, not just by inference. Extended `scripts/regenerate-thumbnails.mjs`: `--type=image` added to the existing `--type=pdf`/`video` filter (kept in sync with `SUPPORTED_IMAGE_MIME_TYPES`); new `--missing` flag flips the query from `thumbnail_storage_key IS NOT NULL` (regenerate) to `IS NULL` (first-time backfill) — `force=true` is passed unconditionally in both modes since it's a no-op when there's no existing key. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev DB/MinIO/dev-server (no Playwright, no screenshot suite): a real portrait phone-style photo (`Orientation=6`, fetched from the same public orientation-test-photo repo used above) uploaded through the full presigned-URL pipeline produced a 3.7 KB, 640×427 **upright, landscape** thumbnail from a 353 KB, 1200×1800-raw-raster source — confirmed both by file dimensions (proving the auto-rotate carried the corrected aspect ratio through the real route, not just the standalone probe) and by eye; `/api/download/[id]` on that same resource still returned the full 353 KB original, confirmed by byte-identical `ImageWidth`/`ImageHeight`/file size; a real existing video and PDF resource were force-regenerated through the now-three-branch route and came back at their unchanged 320px/800px widths with unchanged content; a `.svg` uploaded as `image/svg+xml` was cleanly rejected (400, no crash) by generation; a garbage-bytes file declared `image/jpeg` was cleanly rejected (422) with `thumbnail_storage_key` confirmed still `NULL` (no half-written row); a fresh coverless collection, given an image via "add to collection" (not upload, so no upload-time auto-cover) and then thumbnailed, had its cover auto-populate to that image's own thumbnail key via the pre-existing second-chance logic — fetched successfully, then `force=true`-regenerated, then fetched again successfully from the identical deterministic key; and the bulk backfill script's `--missing --type=image` run against the real dev DB processed all 115 pre-existing thumbnail-less images (114 succeeded, 1 correctly failed — a deliberately-corrupt test file — without aborting the run), a second `--missing` run afterward found 0 remaining candidates (idempotent), and a full default (`--type=image`, no `--missing`) run afterward force-regenerated all 116 now-thumbnailed images with 0 failures. All test resources/collections created for verification were deleted afterward; the 114 real backfilled images were left in place as the intended feature outcome, not test residue. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; verified against the real dev DB, MinIO, and dev server with real photos (including a real portrait-orientation photo and a bulk real-world backfill of 115 pre-existing images), no Playwright, no screenshot suite |
| 83 | JPEG-quality unit bug — image thumbnails (Stage 82), then extended same-session to PDF thumbnails once asked to check "other file types" too. Stage 82's `renderImageThumbnail()` called `canvas.toBuffer('image/jpeg', IMAGE_JPEG_QUALITY)` with `IMAGE_JPEG_QUALITY = 0.8`, copying the 0–1 fractional convention browser Canvas APIs use. `@napi-rs/canvas` actually takes an **integer 0–100** (default with no argument is 92) — so every generated image thumbnail was encoded at an effectively-zero quality: large solid/blocky quantized regions and washed-out color, reported by the user as "blocky, desaturated, with grey/magenta patches." Diagnosed by isolating each stage of the pipeline independently rather than guessing: a synthetic RGB color-swatch source round-tripped perfectly through decode→drawImage(scaled)→JPEG-encode; a real photo decoded and drew correctly at native size with **no** scaling (verified via `canvas.data()` — the raw pre-encode RGBA buffer was byte-correct); only the JPEG-encode step itself was ever wrong. Confirmed the unit mismatch by sweeping the quality argument against the real library: 0, 0.1, 0.8, and 1 all produced byte-identical output size (~6 KB) since they all round down to the same near-zero integer quality, while 80 produced a properly-sized, correct output matching a default (92) encode. Fix: `IMAGE_JPEG_QUALITY` changed `0.8` → `80` in `app/api/resources/[id]/thumbnail/route.ts`, with a comment recording the unit convention so it isn't "corrected" back to a fraction later. Verified live against the real dev server/DB/MinIO in two steps before batching, per instruction: force-regenerated one resource first (a photo, confirmed correct before touching anything else), then ran `scripts/regenerate-thumbnails.mjs --type=image` against the whole dev DB — all 114 pre-existing image thumbnails regenerated, 0 failures; spot-checked raw MinIO object sizes across all 114 (median jumped from corrupted-era tiny to ~30 KB, 6 legitimately small ones — flat-color product renders — visually confirmed clean) and re-verified the exact file the user had screenshotted as broken (`25-6.jpg`) now renders correctly. **`PDF_JPEG_QUALITY` had the identical `0.8` bug**, initially left unfixed and noted as a pending decision — it wasn't visually obvious in Stage 82's own verification because a mostly-white-background/black-text PDF page tolerates near-zero JPEG quality far better than a photograph does (large headings stay legible; only fine print/small text washes out, easy to miss at a glance). Confirmed this empirically before fixing: a real product datasheet PDF's pre-fix thumbnail had every field of small print blurred to illegible grey bars while section headings and the logo stayed readable; after the fix, every field is crisp. Fixed the same way (`PDF_JPEG_QUALITY` → `80`, same unit-convention comment) and regenerated all 17 existing PDF thumbnails via `scripts/regenerate-thumbnails.mjs --type=pdf` — 0 failures. Video thumbnails were checked and confirmed **not** affected — the video branch renders frames via `ffmpeg-static`/`spawn` with its own JPEG encoding, never touching `@napi-rs/canvas`'s `toBuffer`, so this bug had no path to reach it; a real pre-existing video thumbnail was pulled and visually confirmed normal, not regenerated. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` clean; live end-to-end against the real dev DB/MinIO/dev-server; one-then-all regeneration per instruction for both images (114) and PDFs (17), all confirmed non-corrupted by size sweep and/or visual spot-check; video confirmed unaffected and left untouched |
| 84 | Multi-tenancy schema, Stage 1 of the companies/roles migration — purely additive, nothing in the app reads any of it yet. Read real DB state first rather than trusting this file (which was stale): actual account count is 7, not the previously-documented 4, and `viewer@test.local` is confirmed still in **Editors** (group 2), not Viewers. New tables `companies` (`id uuid PK`, `name`, `address`, `phone`, `created_at`), `roles` (`id int PK`, `name unique` — seeded 1=super_admin, 2=admin, 3=editor, 4=viewer, 5=pending; fresh sequential ids, deliberately not reused from `user_groups` so "1" never means two different things), `company_collection_access` (`company_id`, `collection_id`, composite PK, both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE` — the future company-scoped analog of `group_collection_access`, same shape), `invitations` (`id uuid PK`, `token_hash`, `email`, `company_id`, `role_id`, `invited_by uuid REFERENCES users(id)`, `expires_at`, `accepted_at`, `created_at` — not used by any route yet). `users` gained `company_id uuid REFERENCES companies(id)` (nullable — `NULL` means super admin, no company), `role_id int REFERENCES roles(id)` (see NOT NULL note below), `phone text` (nullable — "required" will be enforced on a future invitation-redemption form, not the DB, since existing users have none). New `scripts/migrate-to-companies.mjs` (idempotent, manual-run, same shape as `backfill-covers.mjs`): finds-or-creates a single "Test Company", maps every user's `role_id` from `group_id` (1→1 super_admin, 2→3 editor, 3→4 viewer, 4→5 pending — **role 2 `admin` is never assigned by this script**, company admins arrive only via a future invitation flow), sets `company_id` to `NULL` for super admins and Test Company's id for everyone else, and copies `group_collection_access` into `company_collection_access` (`SELECT DISTINCT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, deduped since multiple groups can grant the same collection). Every write is guarded (`role_id`/`company_id` only set where still `NULL`; the grants insert is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) — confirmed idempotent by running it twice: second run's own "newly set/inserted" counters were all 0 with identical totals. Verified against the real dev DB: role counts 2 super_admin / 0 admin / 3 editor / 1 viewer / 1 pending (7 total, matching the corrected account count above); `company_collection_access`'s collection set matched `group_collection_access`'s distinct-collection set exactly in both directions (`EXCEPT` both ways returned 0 rows), 4 collections, all rows pointing at Test Company. `users.role_id` set `NOT NULL` afterward, once confirmed every row had one. **Regression found and fixed before this could be called done:** setting `role_id NOT NULL` with no default broke `POST /api/auth/register` (`lib/users.ts::createUser`'s `INSERT INTO users` never mentions `role_id`) — a real "additive migration changes behavior" failure, caught by testing registration live rather than trusting `tsc`/`next build` (both stayed clean throughout; this was a runtime-only break). Fixed with `ALTER TABLE users ALTER COLUMN role_id SET DEFAULT 5` (5 = pending, matching the same `PENDING_GROUP_ID` = 4 every registration already assigns) — a DB-level default, no application code touched, so the "no app code reads/writes the new columns" constraint still holds; registration re-tested and confirmed working, with the new row correctly landing at `group_id=4, role_id=5`. Known, accepted asymmetry from this fix: a newly-registered user gets `role_id=5` automatically but `company_id` stays `NULL` (no default exists for it) rather than Test Company, unlike every existing Pending-group user the backfill script touched — harmless today since nothing reads `company_id`, but worth resolving (a trigger, or a default, or handled by the future invitation flow) before any stage that actually reads it. Verified zero application-code references to any new table/column (`company_id`, `role_id`, `companies`, `company_collection_access`, `invitations` — grepped across `app/` and `lib/`, no matches) and zero behavior change end-to-end with real logins: a super admin (`admin@test.local`), an editor (`test2@test.local`), and a genuine viewer (a disposable temp account registered and promoted, then deleted afterward, since no documented account's Viewer-group password is known — see the stale-test-account note elsewhere in this file) each saw exactly the same JWT shape, collection counts/names, and admin-route 403/200 behavior as before this stage. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; DDL and backfill applied directly against the real dev DB via `psql` (see the Database section's direct-DDL list); idempotency proven by a real second run; zero-behavior-change verified live with a super admin, an editor, and a genuine viewer, not assumed |
| 85 | Multi-tenancy Stage 2 of the companies/roles migration — adds `companyId`/`roleId`/`roleName` to the JWT payload, additively; nothing reads them yet. Now computes "topmost granted": collections directly granted to the company (`company_collection_access` on the collection's own id) that have **no granted ancestor** — a `root_finder` walk tagged with `depth` (0 = self, incrementing per level up), an `ancestor_granted` CTE keeping only collections with a grant at `depth >= 1`, then `INNER JOIN company_collection_access cca_self ... LEFT JOIN ancestor_granted ag ... WHERE ag.collection_id IS NULL`. This is provably equivalent to "directly granted AND no granted ancestor," not merely "accessible AND no granted ancestor" — a collection can only pass the `ag IS NULL` filter after already being required to have its own `cca_self` row, so a granted child of an already-granted parent correctly never surfaces at top level (it's still reachable by browsing into the parent). `GET /api/collections?parentId=<id>` (browsing into a specific collection) keeps the older "does this child or any of its own ancestors carry a grant" cascade check — genuinely a different question from the home page's "what's topmost," and left as the mechanical any-depth fix, restructured to reuse the same `accessible` CTE-join pattern the search route already established rather than the old root-only `roots` join. Super admins are unaffected on both branches — the `if (user.canAdmin)` short-circuit still returns true `parent_id IS NULL` top-level collections before any company logic runs, exactly as the task requires ("Super admins still get true top-level collections"). **`GET`/`PUT /api/collections/[id]/access` rewritten, not patched** — the root-redirect (`resolveRoot`/`isRoot`, the 400 "Access is controlled by the root collection" guard) is gone entirely; every collection, at any depth, now reads and writes its own `company_collection_access` rows directly. Added a `fetchNearestGrantingAncestor()` helper (a plain, non-recursive-role `ancestor_chain` CTE plus `DISTINCT ON (company_id) ... ORDER BY depth ASC` to find, per company, the *nearest* granting ancestor at `depth >= 1`) so the response can show `inheritedFrom: {id, name} | null` per company — context only ("also accessible via Products"), never editable, and always `null` for a company with its own direct grant. The frontend Access modal (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`) collapsed from two branches (editable-at-root / read-only-inherited-elsewhere) into one always-editable form; each row shows "(also via {ancestor})" next to an ungranted-but-inherited company. The empty-selection confirm copy was corrected for accuracy now that removing a collection's own grants doesn't necessarily mean nobody can see it (an ancestor grant can still cover it) — reworded from "no company will be able to see it" to "companies without an inherited grant from an ancestor will no longer see it." Breadcrumbs (`GET /api/collections/[id]/ancestors`) needed **no code change** — `isAccessible()`'s root-restriction removal alone made its pre-existing `name: null` / `accessible: false` per-ancestor redaction correct for the new model, since each ancestor's accessibility is now independently recomputed from its own position in the chain rather than uniformly inheriting the one shared root's answer; this guard had never actually fired before (root-only made every ancestor in a chain resolve to the same true/false), confirmed by a live test — the first real exercise. Sub-collection creation is unchanged: still writes no grant rows, access inherited from whatever ancestor is granted. **Verified live end-to-end against the real dev DB/server, no Playwright, no screenshot suite, using the real collection tree** (`products / lucitag / {floor, suspension}` standing in for the task's Lamps example, `products / ghidini / kenzia` for its Fixtures/Products-sibling): a disposable test company granted **only** `lucitag` (not `products`, not any sibling) — its user's home page returned exactly `["lucitag"]`, not `products` and not any of the other four unrelated roots; opening `lucitag` directly succeeded (200) while `products` and `ghidini` both 403'd; children of `lucitag` (`floor`, `suspension`) were visible via cascade with no separate grants; files inside a `lucitag` descendant listed and downloaded successfully while a file inside `ghidini/kenzia` 403'd on download; search matched a granted-subtree filename and the `lucitag` collection name itself, but returned nothing for an ungranted-subtree filename or the `ghidini` collection name; per-asset metadata GET 200'd for the granted file and 403'd for the denied one. A brand-new child created live under `lucitag` was immediately visible to the test company with zero new grants (cascade, "now and future"); a sibling child created under `products` stayed invisible. The breadcrumb (`ancestors`) call for a grandchild inside `lucitag` returned `products` as `{name: null, accessible: false}` and `lucitag`/`floor` with real names and `accessible: true` — the redaction guard's first real, confirmed firing.
