| 89 | Multi-tenancy Stage 6 — splits `requireAdmin` into two tiers and switches capability derivation from `group_id` to `role_id`. Highest-risk stage of the migration (introduces a second admin tier); classified every `requireAdmin` call site by grep across `app/` before editing, reported as a table, and only then edited. **New guards, `lib/session.ts`:** `isSuperAdmin(payload)` (`roleId === 1`, fails closed on a missing/old-shape token) and `requireSuperAdmin` (role 1 only); `requireAdmin` itself is unchanged code (still checks `canAdmin`) but now means "role 1 or 2" purely because canAdmin's derivation changed underneath it. **Capability, `lib/auth.ts::login()`:** `deriveCapabilitiesFromRole(role_id)` replaces the old `user_groups`-joined flags — super_admin(1)/admin(2) → `canAdmin`; +editor(3) → `canUpload`; everyone but pending(5) → `canDownload`; claim names on the JWT payload unchanged, only the source. Verified against the live DB before and after: all 7 real users' role-derived tuple is byte-identical to their prior group-derived one (the Stage 84 backfill was clean) — no user's effective capability changed. **20 sites reclassified `SUPER_ADMIN`** (structure/cross-company — collection create/delete/rename, `GET`/`PUT /api/collections/[id]/access` (the Access modal), `GET /api/collections/[id]/descendant-count`, metadata-field definitions (`POST /api/metadata-fields`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/metadata-fields/[id]`), `GET /api/media`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/resources/[id]`, `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail`, `POST /api/resources/[id]/collections`, `POST /api/upload/from-url`, all of `shares`/`shares/[id]`/`shares/[id]/link`, and `GET /api/companies` — the last one reclassified *from* `requireAuth`, not `requireAdmin`, since it feeds two now-super-admin-only forms) switched `requireAdmin` → `requireSuperAdmin`. **2 sites stayed `COMPANY_SCOPED`** (`GET /api/users`, `PATCH /api/users/[id]`) — kept `requireAdmin`, gained same-company enforcement (below). **Leak found during classification, not in the original literal spec, fixed with explicit sign-off before proceeding:** ~10 routes gate an unconditional cross-company bypass on `if (user.canAdmin)` rather than on `requireAdmin` itself — `search`, `download/[id]`, `collections` (list + `[id]` + `[id]/files` + `[id]/files/[fileId]`), `collections/[id]/ancestors` (both branches), `upload/complete`, and `lib/permissions.ts`'s `canEditResourceMetadata` (governs `GET`/`PUT /api/resources/[id]/metadata` and `GET /api/resources/[id]`) — these are the same Stage-86/88 access-CTE sites, deliberately left untouched in shape. Once `canAdmin` became true for company admins too, every one of these would have silently handed a company admin unrestricted, cross-company read/download/search/metadata-edit access — the exact leak this whole migration exists to prevent. Fixed by swapping the bypass condition only (never the CTE query text) from `user.canAdmin` to `isSuperAdmin(user)` at all ~10 sites — a company admin now falls through to the identical company-scoped cascade every editor/viewer already uses. **Company-scoped enforcement, the user-management leak surface:** `GET /api/users` — super admin unfiltered; company admin gets `AND u.company_id = $companyId` (from the token, never a request param), fail-closed (`[]`) if the caller's own `companyId` is null. `PATCH /api/users/[id]` — same-company check runs before the body is even parsed (so a name-only edit is covered, not just role/company fields); **the peer-admin-edit rule** (the subtle, security-critical part): if the caller isn't a super admin, editing is refused (403, same generic "Access denied" as the cross-company case, so the reason is never distinguishable) when the *target's current* `role_id` is 1 or 2 — a company admin can never touch another admin-tier user **on any field**, not merely be blocked from role-escalating one; list visibility (`GET`) is unaffected, so a company admin still sees a peer admin in the list, just can't edit the row. `roleId` became a settable PATCH field: unknown-id → 400; a company admin may only set it to 3 (editor) or 4 (viewer) — 1/2 → 403 ("only a super admin can assign this role"); `companyId` stays super-admin-only to set at all (403 for a company admin regardless of value). **The escape hatch:** a super admin is unrestricted by every one of the above and is the only role that can create, edit, or reassign an admin-tier user — verified live (renamed and then demoted a company admin to viewer as super admin, both 200). **UI:** `components/AppShell.tsx`'s `NAV_ITEMS` gained a `superAdminOnly` flag (Media/Metadata/Shares now hidden from a company admin, who keeps only Users) alongside the pre-existing `adminOnly`; `app/home/page.tsx`'s "New collection" button/form and the three admin sub-pages' own entry gates (`admin/media`, `admin/metadata-fields`, `admin/shares`) switched from `canAdmin` to `roleName === 'super_admin'`; `app/admin/users/page.tsx` gained a Role column (dropdown options tier-restricted to what the caller may actually assign), the Company column renders as read-only text (not a disabled-looking `<select>`) for a company-admin viewer since they can never edit it, and a peer-admin's row locks with the same lock-icon/tooltip treatment as the caller's own ("you") row. Deliberately **not** reworked: `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`'s ~19 `canAdmin`-gated kebab items mix genuinely-now-super-admin-only actions with ones still reachable (e.g. remove-from-collection) — every action is correctly enforced server-side regardless, so this is a UI-polish gap (a company admin sees a button that 403s on click for the super-admin-only ones), not a security one; flagged, not fixed, out of this stage's scope. | ✓ tested — `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean after both the initial build and the peer-admin-rule refinement; verified live, real accounts, two real companies (a third pair used for the peer-admin refinement, cleaned up separately): a company admin saw only their own company's 2–3 users; 403 on a cross-company user (list and edit); 403 creating a collection, opening the Access modal, and hitting `GET /api/media`/`POST /api/metadata-fields`/`GET /api/shares`; 403 setting a user's company or promoting to admin/super_admin; 200 changing an own-company user's role between editor/viewer; 403 editing a peer admin's name (the new rule) while still able to edit an own-company editor's name (200); the anti-leak proof re-run at the collection/file level, not just user management — the same company admin got 403/empty on `search`/`download`/`collections`/`files`/`metadata` against a real second company's real collection and file, while retaining full working upload/browse/download/search on their own company's; a root-level grant on the same company coexisting with a mid-depth one (from the Stage 88 session) confirmed no interference; super admin re-confirmed unrestricted everywhere including both scratch companies; editor and viewer capability tuples and their 403 on `GET /api/users` reconfirmed unchanged. All scratch companies/users/collections/resources deleted after each round; DB re-verified back to the 7-user/1-company baseline both times |

| 90 | Invitation backend (multi-tenancy migration remaining item (a), API + email half only — UI redemption page is a separate stage, not built). New `lib/invitations.ts`: `validateInvitationToken(param)` — the fail-closed gate, mirrors `lib/shares.ts`'s `validateShareToken` but collapses expired/already-accepted into the same generic `'invalid'` too (shares still distinguishes expired/revoked for UX; this was asked to be stricter); `isCompanyStub(company)` — true when a company has neither `address` nor `phone` set; `INVITABLE_ROLES`/`COMPANY_ADMIN_INVITABLE_ROLES`/`ROLE_TO_LEGACY_GROUP_ID` constants. `lib/mail.ts` gained `sendInvitationEmail`. Four routes: `POST`/`GET /api/invitations` (create+send / list, both `requireAdmin`), `DELETE /api/invitations/[id]` (revoke), `GET /api/invitations/validate/[token]` and `POST /api/invitations/redeem` (both public). Token mechanism: random 32-byte `base64url`, bcrypt-hashed at rest (`token_hash`), `{id}.{rawToken}` link shape identical to shares — raw token exists only in the outbound email, never in any API response or the DB. Expiry: 7 days. Tier authorization: only a super admin invites role 2 (admin); a company admin may invite role 3/4 (editor/viewer) only, and only for their own company — **verified live that a company admin's `companyId` in the request body is silently overridden by their token's own companyId**, not merely rejected (the created invite's `company` in the response was the admin's own company, not the requested one); role 1/5 rejected with 400 before any authorization check. `needsCompanyDetails` (surfaced by validate, applied by redeem) is recomputed from live company state at both read time and redeem time, never trusted from an earlier call — true only when the invite is role 2 and the target company is still `isCompanyStub`. Redemption is atomic and single-use by construction, not just by a pre-check: the accept-and-create runs as one DB transaction whose first statement is `UPDATE invitations SET accepted_at = now() WHERE id = $1 AND accepted_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now() RETURNING id` — a reused or expired token matches 0 rows there and the whole transaction (including the user insert that would otherwise follow) rolls back with nothing created, rather than a separate check-then-act race. The email on the created user is taken from the invite row itself, never from the redeem request body — verified live with a spoofed `email` field in a real redeem call, silently ignored in favor of the invite's own. Redemption creates the user with `status: 'approved'` (no separate approval step) and maps `role_id` onto a legacy `group_id` (`ROLE_TO_LEGACY_GROUP_ID`) purely for schema consistency with the still-present group model — nothing reads it for capability. Does not auto-login; the client is expected to redirect to `/login` after a successful redeem. Verified live end-to-end (real accounts, real Gmail SMTP, no Playwright): super-admin-invites-admin → email delivered → validate returned the right company/role/`needsCompanyDetails: true` → redeem created the role-2 user, filled the company's stub `address`/`phone`, marked `accepted_at`; that new company admin then invited an editor (forced to their own company); the same admin's attempt to invite for a different company had its `companyId` silently overridden; the same admin's attempt to invite another admin got 403; inviting role 1/5 got 400; reusing the now-accepted token, and a wholly bogus token, both produced the identical generic `{status:'invalid'}`; an existing-user email and a duplicate active-invite both got 409; a spoofed `email` in the redeem body was ignored in favor of the invite's own; cross-company DELETE got 404, same-company and super-admin DELETE succeeded. All test users/invites/the temporary second company cleaned up afterward; Test Company's `address`/`phone` reset back to `NULL` so it stays a stub for the future redemption-page (UI) stage's own testing. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean.

| 91 | Invitation redemption UI (`app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`), the piece Stage 90 explicitly deferred — closes multi-tenancy migration item (a) end to end (create/send → list/revoke → validate → **redeem via a real page**, not just the API). Reproduces `app/page.tsx`'s split-screen shell locally (same `login-mm2.jpg` panel, wordmark, tagline, brand tokens — copied rather than imported, since login doesn't export its pieces) so the two screens read as the same product, not a bolted-on one. Four states (`loading`/`invalid`/`valid`/`success`); `invalid` is one generic screen for every backend failure mode, never distinguishing not-found/bad-token/expired/already-accepted (matches `validateInvitationToken`'s own no-oracle discipline, Stage 90). Email is a locked, non-`<input>` display block — never editable, never sent to `/redeem`. Password rules (length/upper/lower/digit/special) are restated client-side as a live checklist, since `lib/users.ts::validatePasswordStrength` isn't reachable from a client component, plus a confirm-password field checked for a match. `needsCompanyDetails: true` renders an additional "Company details" card (company address + company phone) with its own state (`companyAddress`/`companyPhone`), kept structurally separate from the person's own `name`/`phone` so the two phones can never be conflated — verified live with deliberately different values landing on the correct rows (see below). On a failed submit, the page string-matches the backend's one fixed token-failure message (`"This invitation link is invalid or has expired."`) to decide whether to fall back to the generic `invalid` screen (the atomic `/redeem` re-check is authoritative — a link valid on load but redeemed/expired before submit fails exactly like a dead link) versus showing any other error inline on the still-live form (weak password, missing name, duplicate email). Submit button disabled while in flight — belt-and-braces on top of the backend's own atomic single-use lock (Stage 90), not a substitute for it. Two other files touched, both in scope: `app/page.tsx` dropped its "Need an account? Sign up" link in favor of plain "Contact your administrator for access" text (public self-registration is gone); `app/signup/page.tsx` is now a bare server component (`redirect("/")` unconditionally) rather than a registration form — kept only so a stale bookmark doesn't 404, confirmed live (`307` → `/`); the backend `POST /api/auth/register` endpoint itself was deliberately left untouched (out of this stage's touched-files scope) — still callable directly, just unreachable from the UI; grepped the whole `frontend/` tree and confirmed no other file links to `/signup`. Verified live end-to-end (real accounts, real Gmail SMTP for the invite emails, no Playwright, no browser-automation tool available — verification drove the same `GET /validate`/`POST /redeem` calls the page's own `fetch`s make, plus confirmed the page's SSR shell and the login page's copy change via direct HTTP): an editor/viewer invite (`needsCompanyDetails: false`) validated and redeemed cleanly, new user's next-login JWT carried the invite's `company_id`/`role_id`; a first-admin invite into a disposable stub company (a fresh company created for this test, deliberately not reusing Test Company so its own stub state — preserved at the end of Stage 90 for exactly this purpose — stayed intact) validated with `needsCompanyDetails: true` and redeemed with `phone: "555-9999-PERSONAL"` (user) vs. `companyDetails.phone: "555-1111-COMPANY"` (company) — both the `users` row and the `companies` row (address + phone) confirmed correct and un-conflated afterward; reusing the now-accepted token produced the identical generic invalid result on both a fresh validate and a fresh redeem, no second account created; `/signup` confirmed to 307-redirect to `/`, and `/`'s rendered HTML confirmed to contain the new "Contact your administrator" copy and no "Sign up" text. Disposable company, users, and invitations all deleted afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. |

| 92 | Company-management and invitation-management admin UI — closes the last gap in the invitation feature: 7a/Stage 90 built the endpoints, Stage 91 built redemption, but nothing let an admin create a company or manage invites without hitting the API directly. **`POST /api/companies`** (new, `requireSuperAdmin`): name-only stub creation (`address`/`phone` stay `NULL`, filled later at first-admin redemption, unchanged from Stage 90); case-insensitive duplicate-name check (`LOWER(name) = LOWER($1)`, no DB constraint exists for this) → 409, not a silent merge; 400 on blank/over-200-char names. **`GET /api/companies`** widened, opt-in: absent `?detail=true` still returns the exact bare `[{id,name}]` array it always has (verified live — both existing callers, `app/admin/users/page.tsx`'s Company picker and `app/home/page.tsx`'s grant checkboxes, use it this way and were re-checked unaffected); `?detail=true` returns `{companies: [{id, name, userCount, hasAdmin, isStub}]}` — `userCount`/`hasAdmin` via correlated subqueries against `users`, `isStub` via `lib/invitations.ts`'s existing `isCompanyStub` (reused, not reimplemented) — for the new companies page only. New `app/admin/companies/page.tsx` (`requireSuperAdmin`, gated client-side on `roleName === 'super_admin'` exactly like `admin/media`/`admin/metadata-fields`/`admin/shares`): create-company form; companies table (name, user count, an "Has admin"/"No admin yet" pill, a "Complete"/"Stub" pill) with a per-row "Invite admin" button that pre-fills the invite form below with that company and role 2, then scrolls/focuses it; a general "Invite someone" form (email + company `<select>` + role `<select>`, all three native, `bg-card` per the Stage 76 popup-legibility convention — a flat company list needs no `CollectionTreePicker`-style portal, that trap is specifically about custom-built dropdowns inside a `.dam-glass` card, which this isn't); a pending-invites table (all companies, revoke per row). **Placement decision, stated as asked:** super-admin invite management lives only on this new Companies page, not duplicated onto `/admin/users` — company-admin invite management (their own company only, editor/viewer only, no company picker since there's nothing to pick) was added directly to the existing `app/admin/users/page.tsx` instead, gated `!isSuperAdmin`, since that's already where a company admin manages their own people; a super admin visiting `/admin/users` sees no invite section there at all. Both invite forms call the identical `POST /api/invitations` (Stage 90, untouched) — the UI only narrows which options are ever shown; the server remains the sole authority, reconfirmed live (see below). Nav: `AppShell`'s `NAV_ITEMS` gained `{ key: "companies", ... superAdminOnly: true }` between Collections and Media — the pre-existing `(!item.superAdminOnly || isSuperAdmin)` filter (Stage 89) hides it from company admins/editors/viewers with no new gating logic needed. Verified live end-to-end with real accounts and real Gmail SMTP (no Playwright): bare-array and `?detail=true` shapes both confirmed correct on the real dev DB; created a real company via `POST /api/companies`, confirmed the case-insensitive-duplicate 409 and the blank-name 400; invited a real admin into it (real email delivered, 201 with no token in the response), then — mirroring exactly what the companies page's "Invite admin" flow drives — redeemed a crafted token for the same shape of invite, confirming the company's stub `address`/`phone` filled and `?detail=true` flipped `hasAdmin`/`isStub` correctly on the next fetch; logged in as that new company admin and drove the `/admin/users` invite control to invite an editor with no `companyId` in the request, confirming the response's `company` was their own; `GET /api/invitations` as that company admin returned only their own company's invite, never the earlier admin-tier one; **direct API abuse re-confirmed post-UI, not assumed still true:** the same company-admin token POSTing `roleId: 2` still got 403, and POSTing a different real company's uuid as `companyId` still came back silently overridden to their own company, not honored, exactly as Stage 90 established — the new UI changed nothing about the server-side guarantee; a cross-company `DELETE` on another company's invite still 404'd for a company admin, same-company and super-admin `DELETE` both succeeded; `GET /api/companies` (the plain, un-detailed form) still 403'd for both an editor and a company admin, confirming the companies page's own data source stays locked down even if someone found the URL. All test companies/users/invitations deleted afterward; Test Company's stub state (untouched throughout this stage's testing — a fresh disposable company was used instead) reconfirmed still `NULL`/`NULL`. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. |

| 93 | Country-code phone selector on the invite redemption page — the person's Phone and, for a first-admin invite, the company's Phone were both bare text inputs storing an ambiguous raw number; both now compose a `+<dial> <number>` string via a shared picker. New `lib/countries.ts`: static, dependency-free (grepped `package.json` first — no phone library existed) — 228 entries (`{code, name, dial}`, ISO 3166-1 alpha-2), a solid global list rather than a Coperon-only focused set, since it's just static data and the cost of "too many countries" is zero. Flag emoji are computed from the ISO code (`flagEmoji()`, regional-indicator-letter pairing) rather than hand-typed per row — 228 hand-typed emoji is 228 chances for an invisible-in-diff copy/paste mistake. `DEFAULT_COUNTRY_CODE = "LB"` (Lebanon) — Coperon's primary locale. New `components/PhoneInput.tsx`: one reusable control (country `<select>` + number `<input>`) used for both phone fields — self-contained state, emits only the composed string (plus the raw parts) via `onChange`; the parent never reassembles anything itself. Number input allows digits/space/dash while typing (readability — "70 123 456" or "70-123-456" both read fine) but strips to digits-only at compose time, so `compose(dial, "70-123 456")` and `compose(dial, "70123456")` produce the identical stored string; verified directly (not just read). Country `<select>` renders `{flag} {dial}` (e.g. "🇱🇧 +961") — kept deliberately compact per the task's own spec ("flag + dial code in the trigger"), not the fuller `{flag} {name} ({dial})` alternative also considered, since a 420px-wide form column with the number input beside it doesn't have room for both without one crowding the other; noted here as the tradeoff made, not an oversight. Native `<select>`, not a custom dropdown — a flat, alphabetical 228-option list needs no `CollectionTreePicker`-style portal (that convention is specifically about custom-built popovers trapped inside a `.dam-glass` card's `backdrop-filter` stacking context; native select chrome isn't affected by it); `bg-card dark:bg-card` on the select — `bg-card` is already dark-reactive on its own via the `[data-theme="dark"]` token swap (Stage 74/75, confirmed by reading `globals.css`'s `@custom-variant dark` rule), so `dark:bg-card` is redundant in effect but included anyway to match the Stage 76 convention by the letter, not just in spirit. Wired into `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`: both `phone` and `companyPhone` state now hold whatever `PhoneInput` last emitted directly (no more manual `.trim()` in `handleSubmit` — the component already emits a clean composed string or `""`); the two phone fields stay two separate `PhoneInput` instances writing to two separate state variables (unchanged from Stage 91's original person-vs-company separation), so the country-selector addition couldn't introduce a new way to conflate them even by accident. No backend change — `POST /api/invitations/redeem` already took `phone`/`companyDetails.phone` as opaque strings and still does; grepped the whole `frontend/` tree for anything that touches `.phone` and confirmed the only consumers are `lib/invitations.ts`'s `isCompanyStub` (a truthy/falsy check, not a parse) and the redeem route's own trim/`slice(0, 30)` (well within the ~21-char worst case a composed value produces) — nothing anywhere parses a phone value as bare digits, so the added `+<dial> ` prefix breaks nothing downstream; neither the admin users page nor the admin companies page (Stage 92) render a raw phone value anywhere today (grepped, confirmed — they only show the derived `isStub` boolean), so there was nothing to update for display consistency either. Verified live end-to-end (real accounts, real invites, no Playwright — a redeem call was made with an already-composed string, i.e. exactly what `PhoneInput` would have sent, since driving the actual browser dropdown wasn't available): an editor invite redeemed with `phone: "+961 70123456"` and the `users` row stored that exact string; a first-admin invite (a disposable stub company, `Test Company` left untouched since it had already been filled in by real prior usage — confirmed live, not assumed a stub) redeemed with the person on `+961 71234567` and the company deliberately on a *different* country, `+971 501234567` — both rows confirmed correct afterward and the two values confirmed distinct, not conflated; the `compose()` stripping logic itself was independently verified (`"70-123 456"` and `"  70 123 456  "` both reduce to `"70123456"`, empty input reduces to `""` and correctly fails the parent's required-field check) and the flag-emoji generator was spot-checked for LB/US/FR/AE against known-correct flags. All test users/invitations/the disposable company deleted afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. |

| 96 | Access log — Stage A (logging only, no dashboard/reader yet). New append-only `access_log` table (`id uuid PK default gen_random_uuid()`, `user_id`/`company_id`/`resource_id` all `ON DELETE SET NULL` so deleting a user/company/resource never destroys the historical count, `action text` — `'view'`/`'download'`, `created_at timestamptz default now()`), plus `idx_access_log_tenant_created (company_id, created_at)` (the future per-company dashboard's access pattern) and `idx_access_log_resource (resource_id)`. `company_id` is denormalized onto the row from the acting user's JWT at write time — deliberately not derived from the user's *current* `company_id` at read time, so a historical event keeps the company it actually happened under even if the user is later reassigned or deleted. New `lib/accessLog.ts`: `logAccess({ userId, companyId, resourceId, action })` — deliberately not `async`/not returning a promise for the caller to await; it fires the `INSERT` and immediately attaches its own `.catch()` (logs via `console.error`, never rethrows), so a caller that doesn't await it can't accidentally end up waiting anyway. **Two wire sites, both fire only after the existing access check already passed — a 403 never reaches either call:** (1) `GET /api/download/[id]` — after `getDownloadUrl` succeeds, `action: 'download'`, `userId: user.sub`, `companyId: user.companyId ?? null` (a true super admin logs with `company_id: null`, same as their token — not an error, just an admin action with no company). (2) `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` — after its existing cascade access check, `action: 'view'`. **Chosen view signal, stated explicitly:** `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata`, not `GET /api/resources/[id]`. Reasoning: this is the one endpoint `components/ResourceMetadata.tsx` calls on mount, and it has exactly two mount sites — `MetadataDrawer`'s "Details" trigger (all roles, including Viewers) and the full-page metadata editor (admin/editor) — both keyed by `resourceId`, so opening a different asset forces a fresh mount and a fresh fetch; opening the *same* asset's drawer twice fires it twice, which is correct (two real looks). `GET /api/resources/[id]` was rejected as the signal because it's admin/editor-only (`canEditResourceMetadata`) and would silently miss every Viewer's views — the metadata-fetch endpoint is `requireAuth` and open to any role with resource access, matching what "a user looked at this asset" actually means app-wide. Neither a grid/list card render (`GET /api/media`, `GET /api/collections/[id]/files`, `GET /api/collections`) nor a thumbnail blob fetch calls this endpoint, so scrolling a page of many cards logs nothing — confirmed live (see right). Public share views (`GET /api/share/[token]/...`) are explicitly out of scope this stage — no logged-in user/company exists on that path, and forcing a company onto anonymous share traffic would misattribute it; deferred, not built. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB (real accounts, no Playwright): a super admin download logged `company_id: null`; an editor's download of a company-accessible resource logged their real `company_id`; an editor's download/metadata-view of a resource confirmed genuinely out of scope via the app's own cascade CTE (not a naive direct-membership check, which falsely flagged an actually-cascade-accessible resource as "denied" on a first attempt) both correctly 403'd with **zero** new rows in either case; opening the metadata endpoint on an accessible resource logged one `view` row; listing a media page (`GET /api/media`) logged nothing. **Fire-and-forget proven, not assumed:** the `access_log` table was renamed away mid-session (simulating a broken insert) and both `GET /api/download/[id]` and `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` still returned clean 200s with their normal payloads — the request path never saw the insert failure. **Cascade-null proven, not assumed:** a scratch company/user/resource/log-row were inserted, then the resource, user, and company were each deleted in turn (resource before user, respecting `resources.created_by`'s plain FK with no `ON DELETE` clause) — the log row survived every deletion, ending with all three FKs `NULL` and `action`/`created_at` intact. All scratch rows (company/user/resource, and every row written during live verification) deleted afterward; `access_log` left empty, matching "logging starts now" — it cannot and does not claim to reconstruct past activity. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. |

| 97 | Access log — Stage B (per-company usage analytics dashboard, reads Stage 96's `access_log`). New `GET /api/analytics` (`requireAdmin` — super admin or company admin): scope is always taken from the token, never the request — a super admin sees every company; a company admin is forced onto their own `companyId` and an optional `?companyId=` query param naming a *different* company is silently overridden back to their own (same discipline as `POST /api/invitations`), verified live (see below) rather than merely reasoned. `range` (`7d`/`30d`/`90d`/`all`) is a plain whitelist lookup with a `30d` fallback on any other value — same convention as `GET /api/media`'s `pageSize`/`sort`, never a 400. Every count is a parameterized `COUNT(*)`/`COUNT(*) FILTER (...)` over `access_log`, riding the Stage 96 `(company_id, created_at)`/`(resource_id)` indexes; a shared `baseConditions()` builds the company+since `WHERE` once and every query clones it before adding its own extra condition (e.g. `action = 'view'`), so no query accidentally mutates another's filter. Response: `totals` (views/downloads in range); `byCompany` (an `INNER JOIN companies` — naturally excludes `company_id IS NULL` rows, i.e. a super admin's own activity, which has no company to attribute to; for a company admin this is always exactly their one row); `topViewed`/`topDownloaded` (`LEFT JOIN resources`, `GROUP BY resource_id` — Postgres groups every `NULL` `resource_id` into one bucket, which is exactly right for "still counts, labeled `(deleted asset)`, not a separate row per lost identity"); `activeUserCount` (`COUNT(DISTINCT user_id)`, which already ignores `NULL`s) and `topUsers` (`LEFT JOIN users`, a `NULL user_id` bucket labeled `(deleted user)` the same way); `timeSeries` (a `generate_series`-driven day-by-day count with no gaps — for `7d`/`30d`/`90d` the anchor is `today − (N−1) days`, the same anchor the totals/breakdown queries use so the series always sums to the headline totals; for `all`, the anchor is `MIN(created_at)` of the in-scope rows, or `null` → an empty series, never an unbounded query). Dashboard `app/admin/analytics/page.tsx` (`requireAdmin`-gated the same way as `admin/users`, not super-admin-only — a company admin reaches it too): range `<select>` (`bg-card dark:bg-card`, matching the Stage 76 convention) in the frosted top bar; three summary stat tiles (views/downloads/active users); a per-company two-series bar list (super admin: every company; company admin: their own single row, explicitly framed "Your company's activity"); a daily grouped-bar time series; two single-series ranked bar lists (top viewed/downloaded assets); a most-active-users ranked list; a clear "No activity recorded yet in this period" empty state gated on `totals.views + totals.downloads === 0`, replacing every chart section rather than rendering them broken/blank on a sparse log. **Charting approach, stated as asked:** no new dependency — plain CSS/flexbox bars (rounded 4px data-ends, a shared numeric scale per chart, direct value labels) for every chart, following this session's dataviz-skill pass; the one exception is the two-series color pair, which *was* computed rather than eyeballed — `--color-chart-downloads` (new token, `app/globals.css`, promoted the same way as the Stage 74/75 brand/neutral tokens: `:root` light value `#c9541c`, `[data-theme="dark"]` value `#d9622c`, re-declared in `@theme inline` for a real `bg-chart-downloads` utility) was validated against `--color-brand`/`--color-brand-dark` with the dataviz skill's `validate_palette.js` against this app's own card surfaces (`#FFFFFF` light / `#131E2B` dark), not the skill's generic defaults: CVD ΔE 20.5 (light, protan) / 22.4 (dark, protan), both clear of the 8.0 target, and deliberately a hue distinct from every semantic color already in use elsewhere (destructive red, the amber "Unassigned" badge, success emerald) so a chart series never impersonates a status color. `AppShell.tsx` gained an `AnalyticsIcon` and a new `"analytics"` nav item (`adminOnly: true, superAdminOnly: false` — visible to both admin tiers, hidden from editors/viewers, same gating shape as "Users"). Read-only over `access_log` (+ joins for names) — no write, and no change to logging, download, or view behavior anywhere. **Seeded and verified against real, generated data, not synthetic fixtures:** created a disposable second company ("Analytics Test Co") + a disposable user, promoted to editor then to company admin via the real `POST /api/companies`/`PATCH /api/users/[id]` endpoints, granted access to a real collection (`designers`) via the real Stage 88 access editor — **caught and fixed a real self-inflicted bug during setup**: the access editor's whole-set-replace `PUT` (documented in "Conventions that bite") wiped Test Company's own pre-existing grant on that same collection when the first `PUT` named only the new company; re-issued with both companies' ids and confirmed Test Company's editor could download again before any real seeding began. Drove real events through the real routes across three identities (a Test Company editor, the scratch company's user, and the super admin with no company) — 3 distinct downloads + 3 distinct views for Test Company, 2 distinct downloads + 2 distinct views for the scratch company, 1 download + 1 view for the super admin (15 rows total, confirmed by a direct `psql` count grouped by `company_id`/`action` before ever calling the API) — then confirmed `GET /api/analytics` as super admin reproduced every number exactly: `totals` 7 views/8 downloads, `byCompany` 3/4 and 3/3, `topViewed`/`topDownloaded` matching the exact per-resource counts generated, `activeUserCount: 3`, `topUsers` matching each identity's exact view/download split, and a `timeSeries` with every prior day at 0 and the seed day carrying the full 7/8. **Scoping proof:** logged in as the scratch company's now-company-admin user and confirmed their own view showed only their 3/3 numbers and a single-row `byCompany`; then repeated the identical request with `?companyId=<Test Company's real id>` in the query string and got **byte-identical output** — the override was silently ignored, never Test Company's 3/4. **Deleted-asset proof:** permanently deleted one of the seeded resources (`peretto.jpg`, which had one real view event) via the existing `DELETE /api/resources/[id]` — confirmed via `psql` its `access_log` row survived with `resource_id` now `NULL`, and the next `GET /api/analytics` call showed it as `{resourceId: null, filename: "(deleted asset)", count: 1}` with the grand total unchanged. **Empty-state proof:** a third disposable company with a promoted admin and zero events returned `totals: {views: 0, downloads: 0}` and an all-zero (`7d`/`30d`) or genuinely empty (`all`, since `MIN(created_at)` has no rows to anchor on) `timeSeries` — exactly what the dashboard's empty-state gate expects, never a broken/partial chart. **Editors/viewers confirmed blocked:** a real editor token (`canAdmin: false`) got `403 "Admin access required"` from `GET /api/analytics` directly. **Cleanup — log rows deleted, not left as anonymized history (the choice this stage's own build note called for making explicit):** all 15 seed/verification `access_log` rows were deleted afterward (this was Stage-B build verification, not real end-user activity, so nothing legitimate would have been lost); the two disposable companies and their two disposable users were deleted too, which — thanks to `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `company_collection_access.company_id` — automatically removed the scratch grant from the `designers` collection, re-confirmed via `psql` to show only Test Company's original grant remaining, unchanged. The one non-reverted change is intentional, not residue: `peretto.jpg` stays permanently deleted, since deleting it live was the point of the deleted-asset proof. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. |

| 98 | Access log widened — a fuller "who did what" history (still logging only; the activity-feed UI reading this richer log is Stage D, not built). New `ALTER TABLE access_log ADD COLUMN metadata jsonb` — core columns (`user_id`/`company_id`/`resource_id`/`action`/`created_at`) stay universal; event-specific shape lives in this one flexible column instead of a wider, mostly-`NULL` fixed schema. `lib/accessLog.ts`'s `logAccess()` widened additively: `AccessAction` gained `'share_create' | 'share_access' | 'upload' | 'login'` alongside the existing `'view' | 'download'`; `resourceId` became optional (`string | null`, default `null` — logins and collection-target shares have none) and a new optional `metadata?: Record<string, unknown> | null` param is `JSON.stringify`'d before the insert, same convention `metadata_fields.options` already uses. Every existing call site (the Stage 96 download/view logs) keeps working unchanged — both are backward-compatible widenings, not breaking ones. **Five new/changed wire sites, every one fire-and-forget and only reached past its own existing access/auth check — a 403 or a failed login is never logged as activity:** (1) **`share_create`** — `POST /api/shares`, after the share row is inserted, logs `resourceId` (the target if it's a resource, else `null`) and `metadata: {targetType, targetId, accessLevel, expiresAt}`; the raw token is never touched by this call (shares are referenced by id only, matching the existing `token_hash`/`token_encrypted` discipline). (2) **`share_access`** — new `lib/shares.ts` exports: `resolveShareOwningCompany(share)` (best-effort, not a guess — walks every collection the shared resource/collection could be reached through, at any depth, the same cascade every other access check in this app uses since Stage 88, and returns a company id **only when exactly one company's grant reaches the target**; zero or more than one both resolve to `null` rather than picking arbitrarily) and `logShareAccess(share, action, resourceId)` (a fire-and-forget wrapper — the resolution query is itself a real DB call that can fail independently of the eventual insert, so this wraps resolution + `logAccess` as one non-blocking unit, never awaited by either call site). Two sub-events, deliberately split rather than one blended signal: `'viewed'` fires once per gallery load in `GET /api/share/[token]` (the "link was opened" moment, `userId: null` since the accessor is anonymous by construction); `'downloaded'` fires only in `GET /api/share/[token]/file/[resourceId]`'s `mode=download` branch, after the `accessLevel !== 'download'` 403 gate — `mode=view`/`mode=original` (the share page's per-thumbnail/lightbox preview fetches) are deliberately never logged, the share-page equivalent of the app-wide "no card-render spam" rule. (3) **`upload`** — wired into **both** `POST /api/upload/complete` and `POST /api/upload/from-url` (the task's own touched-file note said "the upload-completion path," singular; both were done anyway since thumbnail generation already fires fire-and-forget from both client pages after either endpoint's 201, and logging only one would leave URL-imported assets invisible to "who's contributing" — a deliberate scope decision, stated here rather than left silent), in every branch of both routes (with/without `collectionId`), right after `COMMIT`, `metadata: {filename, collectionId}`. (4) **`login`** — `lib/auth.ts::login()`, after the JWT is signed (i.e. only on a real, successful login — both `AppError` throws for a bad email/password happen earlier and are never reached), `resourceId: null`, `metadata: {roleName}`; no password or token ever enters the metadata. (5) **`view` widened to lightbox opens** — new `POST /api/resources/[id]/view` (`requireAuth`, the identical cascade access-check block copied from `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata`/`GET /api/download/[id]`), logging `action: 'view', metadata: {source: 'lightbox'}` only past that check; wired fire-and-forget (`.catch(() => {})`, not awaited) into both `openLightbox()` functions (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/admin/media/page.tsx`), fired unconditionally for every file type (not just images, since video/PDF lightbox opens hit no other network call at all today) at the top of the function, before the existing image-only original-fetch logic. **Kept, not dropped, the pre-existing metadata-panel view signal** (`GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata`, Stage 96) — now tagged `metadata: {source: 'metadata'}` for symmetry; **no double-counting**, stated explicitly: opening the "Details" drawer and opening the lightbox are two structurally separate UI controls never triggered by the same click, so a user who does both for one asset genuinely looked at it twice, and the two signals stay distinguishable via `source` in the log rather than silently blended. **Stage B analytics confirmed unaffected, not just assumed:** its `totals`/`byCompany` queries filter on `action IN ('view','download')` (well, `= 'view'`/`= 'download'` per-column) exactly as before — verified live with all six action types present simultaneously in the log (`download`×1, `login`×7, `share_access`×3, `share_create`×2, `upload`×1, `view`×1): `totals` still read exactly `{views: 1, downloads: 1}`, matching only the two resource-centric rows, with the other 13 rows correctly invisible to those two filters. `activeUserCount` (`COUNT(DISTINCT user_id)`, no action filter, unchanged from Stage 97) now naturally reflects activity across every action type, not just view/download — a real, expected broadening ("active" now means any logged action, including a login or an upload), not a regression the task's own "unchanged" language was warning about. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB (real accounts, no Playwright): a real login produced exactly one `login` row with `roleName` and no `company_id`/`resource_id`; a bad-password attempt produced zero new rows; a real upload (presigned URL → PUT → `upload/complete`) produced one `upload` row with the exact filename; a real share created on a resource whose collection (`designers`) carries a grant to exactly one company (Test Company, confirmed via `psql` first) produced a `share_create` row with `company_id: null` (the creator is always a super admin, Stage 6) and the correct target/accessLevel/expiresAt in `metadata`; anonymously loading that share's public gallery (no auth header at all) produced a `share_access`/`viewed` row correctly attributed to Test Company, `mode=view` on a file produced no row, and `mode=download` produced a `share_access`/`downloaded` row — both correctly resolved to Test Company, proving the single-grant resolution path; a second share created on a resource with **zero** company grants anywhere in its ancestor chain (confirmed via the same cascade query the resolver itself runs) produced a `share_access` row with `company_id: null` — the stated "unresolvable, not a guess" behavior, proven, not assumed; a real lightbox-open call to `POST /api/resources/[id]/view` produced one `view`/`{source: "lightbox"}` row, while a `GET /api/media` and a `GET /api/collections/[id]/files` call (grid/list renders) produced zero new rows. **Fire-and-forget proven for every new event, not assumed:** `access_log` was renamed away mid-session and a real login, a real share creation, a real `upload/complete`, and the lightbox-view endpoint all still returned clean success responses with their normal payloads — none of the five new/changed wire sites let a broken insert reach the caller. All test shares, the one test-uploaded resource, and every `access_log` row generated during this stage's verification were deleted afterward — except the one row that predated this session's testing (real, pre-existing user activity, left untouched, not test residue to clean up). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. |

| 102 | **Closes the discoverability trap Stage 101 left documented** — the media page's Upload/Import URL could still create an Unassigned resource for a company admin, which Stage 101's scoping then makes invisible to them forever (reachable only by a super admin from then on). Audited every resource-creation path first (`grep` for `INSERT INTO resources`): exactly two exist, `POST /api/upload/complete` and `POST /api/upload/from-url` — no third path. The collection detail page's own Upload/Import (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`) was never a vector: it's page-scoped to one collection and always sends that `collectionId`, for every role, unchanged. Server-side (the actual boundary): both routes now reject a company admin (`user.canAdmin && !isSuperAdmin(user)` — precisely role 2, not editors, whose only upload surface already always sends a `collectionId` so this would never fire for them anyway) with `400` when `collectionId` is absent, checked before the existing "absent → dedupe into Unassigned" branch runs. The reachability check for a *supplied* `collectionId` needed no change — both routes already had it since Stage 100/101 (`companyHasCollectionAccess` cascade), so an out-of-scope id still 403s exactly as before. A true super admin's behavior is untouched: no new condition applies to them, still able to create Unassigned resources deliberately. UI (`app/admin/media/page.tsx`): a shared `uploadTargetCollectionId` state feeds a `CollectionTreePicker` (no `allowNone` — forces a real pick) rendered only for a non-super-admin; both the Upload and Import-URL submit buttons are disabled (`title` explains why) until it's set, and both handlers carry a defensive early-return mirroring the same condition in case either is ever reached anyway. A super admin sees no picker at all — their controls are pixel-for-pixel what they were before this stage. Verified live with two real companies (Test Company + a disposable second-company admin, deleted after): company admin got `400 "Choose a collection for this upload"` / `400 "Choose a collection for this import"` with no `collectionId`, `403 "Access to collection denied"` with an out-of-scope one (a real collection created by the second company's own admin), and `201` with a real, reachable Test-Company collection, for both routes; super admin's no-`collectionId` upload still succeeded and the resulting resource still showed up under `unassigned=true` — proving the super-admin path is genuinely unchanged, not just un-tested. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. All test resources/collections and the throwaway account were deleted afterward — `GET /api/media`'s total confirmed back at exactly 136, 0 unassigned. |
| 100 | **Company-admin widening (INTERIM — reverses Stage 6/89's "Coperon alone controls collections" call, pending the returning decision-maker's review) + two adjacent fixes found and closed in the same pass.** Classified all 22 `requireSuperAdmin` call sites first (reported before any edit): 15 became `requireAdmin` + company-scoped, 7 stayed super-admin-only (company create/list, metadata field *definitions*, the media library, and the media-library-only "add existing resource to a collection" route — all genuinely global or, for media, unsafe to scope without a second access path since `resources` carries no `company_id`). **Widened to company-scoped** (a company admin may act only within their own company's `company_collection_access` reach; a true super admin is unrestricted): collection create (`POST /api/collections` — a company admin's new collection, top-level or sub, is auto-granted to their own company only, `companyIds` from the request body ignored for them same as invitations; a sub-collection's parent must be reachable), rename/delete (`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/collections/[id]`), `GET .../descendant-count`, asset rename/permanent-delete (`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/resources/[id]`), thumbnail regen (`POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail`), shares (`POST`/`GET /api/shares`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/shares/[id]`, `GET /api/shares/[id]/link` — `shares` has no `company_id` column, so `GET` filters row-by-row via the same two cascade helpers, not a second query path), and URL import (`POST /api/upload/from-url`, widened to `requireUpload` to match `upload/complete`'s existing pattern — this also happened to fix a pre-existing bug where editors already saw the "Import URL" button but always got 403). New shared helpers in `lib/permissions.ts`: `companyHasCollectionAccess` (exported sibling of the existing `companyHasResourceAccess`, same any-depth cascade, seeded from the collection directly) — every widened route calls one of these two, no second access path anywhere. **Self-granting guard** (`GET`/`PUT /api/collections/[id]/access`): a company admin may open it only on a collection their company can reach, and the response contains **only their own company's row** (a dedicated `fetchOwnCompanyGrantState` query, never the full `companies` table filtered client-side) — `PUT` rejects outright (403) any `companyIds` entry that isn't their own company, and never runs the super admin's whole-set replace (which would delete every *other* company's grant too); instead it does a single targeted insert/delete of just their own row. The entire rule is gated behind one exported constant, `COMPANY_ADMIN_CAN_EDIT_ACCESS` (`lib/permissions.ts`) — flipping it to `false` reverts every company admin to 403 here with no route edits. **Fix #1 (separate from the widening, not a scope change):** `DELETE /api/collections/[id]/files/[fileId]` (remove-from-collection) was gated only by `requireAuth` + the company cascade — any authenticated user who could reach a collection, including a **viewer**, could unlink a file from it. Now `requireUpload` (editor and above), closing the gap; a viewer keeps read-only access as everywhere else. **Fix #2:** `components/AppShell.tsx`'s Shares nav item flipped `superAdminOnly: true → false` (Companies/Media/Metadata stay `true` — unchanged, still genuinely super-admin-only) so a company admin sees the nav entry for the API they can now actually use, instead of a working endpoint with no way to reach it. **Editor-delete audit finding:** editors (`canAdmin: false`) already could not hit either permanent-delete route (`requireSuperAdmin` before this stage; `requireAdmin` after — role 3 is excluded either way) or the collection-delete route — no change in editor capability anywhere, single or bulk; confirmed by direct API call with a real editor token, not just by reading the code. UI: `app/home/page.tsx`'s "New collection" gate widened `superAdminOnly → canAdmin`, with the company-picker checkbox list (which needs `GET /api/companies`, still super-admin-only) shown only to a true super admin — a company admin's collection is created with no picker at all. `app/admin/shares/page.tsx` gate widened the same way; its "Resource" share-target option (which depends on the still-super-admin-only `GET /api/media` for search) is hidden for a company admin, leaving only "Collection" — see the documented gap below. Every other `canAdmin`-gated control on `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` (Delete permanently, rename, thumbnail regen, the Access kebab item, "New sub-collection") was **already** rendered for a company admin pre-this-stage (Stage 6 only ever blocked them server-side) and needed no UI change — confirmed by reading the gating conditions, not assumed. ~~**Documented, not fixed (explicitly out of scope — no second access path):** a company admin has no media-library-equivalent view — no cross-collection asset list, no bulk operations outside a single collection at a time — because `GET /api/media` stays super-admin-only and `resources` carries no `company_id` to scope by. This is a real functional gap against "same access as super admin in their scope," accepted as a consequence of the media library's design rather than worked around.~~ **Resolved, Stage 101.** `GET /api/media` is now company-scoped via a correlated `EXISTS` reusing the resource-cascade helper — see Stage 101 for the full build, the unassigned-is-always-invisible consequence, and the verification. Verified live end-to-end with two real companies (Test Company + a second real company, both pre-existing, plus two disposable throwaway accounts created and deleted within this session — a company admin in the second company, a viewer in Test Company): company admin created a collection (auto-granted to their own company only, confirmed via `psql`), renamed it, added a sub-collection, uploaded a file (`upload/complete`, unaffected/already-scoped), renamed and permanently deleted the asset, created/revoked/deleted a share, deleted the collection — all succeeded; the second company's admin got 403 on every one of those same actions against the first company's collection/resource, plus 403 on `POST`/`GET /api/companies`, `GET /api/media`, `POST /api/metadata-fields`, and inviting a `roleId: 2` admin; the self-granting guard rejected adding the other company's id and rejected an other-company-only set, and — an edge case surfaced live, not just reasoned about — a company admin who revokes their own company's last grant on a collection immediately loses the ability to re-grant it themselves via this route too (reachability is the gate; a super admin restored it, after which the company admin could reach it again) — accepted as a faithful, if sharp, consequence of "may edit access only on a collection they can reach," not a bug; the viewer-unlink fix was confirmed live (an unlink attempt now 403s "Upload permission required" where it previously 200'd) while the same viewer's read access and the editor's own remove-from-collection stayed working. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. All test collections/resources/shares and both throwaway accounts were deleted afterward — no residue left in the dev DB. |
| 101 | **Media library scoped to company admins — closes the gap Stage 100 explicitly left documented.** `GET /api/media`: `requireSuperAdmin` → `requireAdmin`; a super admin's results are byte-for-byte unchanged (same `WHERE`/`HAVING` path, no new condition added to their branch). A company admin gets one more `WHERE` clause: a correlated `EXISTS` reusing the exact any-depth cascade `lib/permissions.ts::companyHasResourceAccess` runs for a single resource, inlined here (own CTE alias `root_finder`, own join alias `cr3`) so it composes with type/q/collectionId/unassigned in one query instead of N+1 per-row lookups — the established "fresh alias, never constrain the existing `cr` LEFT JOIN" convention this route's own collectionId filter already documents. Consequence, stated plainly: an unassigned resource has zero `collection_resource` rows, so the `EXISTS` is vacuously false for it — a company admin can never see an orphan, by construction, not by a special-case check. `unassigned=true` therefore composes to an always-empty result for them (no special-case code needed, same "naturally yields zero rows" reasoning the collectionId/unassigned interaction already relied on) — confirmed live via direct API call. The filtered `COUNT(*)` needed no separate change: it already shared the page query's `whereClause`/`havingClause` string-for-string (the Stage 61/62 convention), so the new condition landed in both automatically. `POST /api/resources/[id]/collections` (add-to-collection): `requireSuperAdmin` → `requireAdmin` + **both-sides** scope check — the resource via `companyHasResourceAccess`, the target collection via `companyHasCollectionAccess` (both already-exported from Stage 100) — a company admin needs both to pass; checking only one side would let them either pull in a resource they don't own or expose their own resource into a collection they don't control, which is exactly why this route stayed super-admin-only when Stage 100 classified it. `components/AppShell.tsx`: Media nav item `superAdminOnly: true → false` (Companies/Metadata unchanged, still genuinely global). `app/admin/media/page.tsx`: the page's own auth gate widened `roleName === 'super_admin'` → `canAdmin` (without this the nav item would link to a page that still 403'd every company admin — the exact bug class this whole arc keeps closing); "Unassigned only" checkbox hidden entirely for a company admin (always-zero-result control, same reasoning as hiding Shares' "Resource" target option in Stage 100) rather than left visible. **Action-matrix audit, every kebab/bulk item on this page, verified against a real company-admin token, not just read:** Details/Edit metadata (`GET`/`PUT /api/resources/[id]/metadata`, unchanged since Stage 78 — `canEditResourceMetadata` already grants a company admin access via their always-true `canUpload`) — works; Rename, Generate/Regenerate thumbnail, Permanent delete, bulk-delete (all `requireAdmin` + `companyHasResourceAccess` since Stage 100) — work within scope; Add-to-collection + bulk-add-to-collection (this stage) — work within scope; bulk-download (`GET /api/download/[id]`, company-scoped since Stage 96, predates Stage 100 entirely) — works, every resource a company admin's list can show them is by definition already reachable. **Zero actions found visible-and-403** on this page. `CollectionTreePicker` (used by both the filter dropdown and every "Add to collection" picker on this page) calls `GET /api/collections`, already company-scoped since before Stage 100 — confirmed, not just assumed, by reading its fetch calls. Editors/viewers: unchanged, still a clean 403 from `requireAdmin` and no nav item (role 3/4 never carry `canAdmin`). ~~**New finding, documented but explicitly not fixed (outside this stage's touch list):** the page's "Upload" and "Import URL" top-bar actions still create resources with no `collectionId`, i.e. Unassigned — for a company admin this means the upload silently succeeds but the resulting file is immediately invisible in their own now-scoped list (not a 403, a discoverability trap). Not part of the kebab/bulk audit this stage scoped to, and fixing it would mean changing upload behavior, not scoping — left for a future stage.~~ **Resolved, Stage 102** — both routes now require a reachable `collectionId` from a company admin; see Stage 102. Verified live end-to-end with two real companies (Test Company + a second real company, plus two disposable throwaway accounts created and deleted within this session): super admin's total held at 136 (unchanged) throughout; company admin's total (63) matched an independently hand-written `psql` cascade query exactly; a resource created in the second company (via its own admin, its own new collection) was invisible to the first company's admin in the unfiltered list, in a substring search that coincidentally matched one of the first company's *own* filenames (proving the scope held even under a real search collision, not just an empty-case test), under a type filter, and via a direct `collectionId=` probe of the other company's collection (empty result, not an error); a genuinely unassigned resource (super-admin-uploaded, no collection) was visible to the super admin via `unassigned=true` and invisible to the company admin under every query tried; pagination summed correctly across two pages (50 + 13 = 63) and an independent filtered count (`type=image`, 43) matched a hand-written `psql` query; add-to-collection succeeded within scope and 403'd in both directions (foreign resource into an own collection; own resource into a foreign collection), with the super admin's own unrestricted cross-company link (used only to prove the negative) reverted afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. All test collections/resources/links and both throwaway accounts were deleted afterward — `GET /api/media`'s total confirmed back at exactly 136, 0 unassigned, matching the pre-test baseline. |
| 99 | Chronological, filterable activity feed reading `access_log` — the "Stage D" UI the Stage 98 note left open, closing the access-log arc end to end. New `GET /api/activity` (`requireAdmin`): scope taken from the token exactly like `GET /api/analytics`/`GET /api/users` — a super admin sees every company, a company admin is force-scoped to their own `companyId` with no request parameter able to widen it (no `?companyId=` is even read for a company admin, unlike analytics' opt-in super-admin drill-down); `userId` is uuid-validated but never separately checked against the caller's company, since the existing `al.company_id = $ownCompany` predicate already makes a foreign `userId` filter return zero rows rather than another company's data — verified live, not just reasoned. Filters: `action` (repeatable, whitelisted against the six known values, unrecognized values silently dropped), `userId`, `range` (`7d`/`30d`/`90d`/`all`, same whitelist-with-fallback convention as analytics). Pagination: reverse-chronological, page size 50 (one extra row fetched to derive `hasMore` without a second `COUNT`), opaque cursor = base64url `{t: created_at, id}` compared via a row-wise `(al.created_at, al.id) < ($t, $id)` — a malformed/tampered cursor is decoded defensively and just falls back to "no cursor" rather than 400ing, since it's an internal detail the client never hand-constructs. Display joins (never N+1): `users`/`companies`/`resources` for actor/company/asset; two further **conditional** `LEFT JOIN collections` (one for `upload`'s `metadata.collectionId`, one for `share_create`'s collection-type `metadata.targetId`) and a `LEFT JOIN shares` (for `share_access`'s `metadata.shareId`, to resolve a collection-share's own collection name when the row's `resource_id` is null) — each guarded by `al.action = '...'` in its own `ON` clause so it only ever matches the rows it's meant for; every `->>'...'::uuid` cast is safe against non-`share_create`/`share_access`/`upload` rows because their `metadata` simply has no such key, and `->>'missing_key'` is SQL `NULL`, not an error. Returns structured fields (`actor`/`resource`/`target`/`detail`), not prerendered strings — phrasing lives client-side, matching the task's own preference for future-i18n-friendliness. **Bug found and fixed during live verification, not merely reasoned about:** `access_log.resource_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so a row whose asset was later permanently deleted is column-for-column identical to a row whose action never carried a resource at all (e.g. `login`) — the first implementation collapsed both into an absent `resource` field, silently dropping the asset from the line instead of rendering "(deleted asset)". Fixed by making the null-vs-deleted read action-aware: `upload`/`view`/`download` always log a real `resourceId` at write time (see `lib/accessLog.ts`'s call sites), so a null here can only be the delete cascade — always surfaced as `{id: null, filename: null}`; `share_access` resolves the same way but with a third case (the share row itself has since been deleted too, so even `shares.collection_id` can't disambiguate) collapsing to the same deleted-asset shape rather than fabricating a collection target that was never right. Caught by uploading a real file, generating a `download`/`view`/`share_access` trail on it, permanently deleting it, and watching the previously-correct rows go stale — re-verified fixed the same way afterward. Page `app/admin/activity/page.tsx` (new "Activity" sidebar nav item beside Analytics in `components/AppShell.tsx`, identical gating — `adminOnly: true, superAdminOnly: false`, so both admin tiers see it and editors/viewers get neither the route nor the nav item): reverse-chronological list grouped by day ("Today"/"Yesterday"/full date, computed client-side from local-midnight boundaries); filter controls — a portal-rendered checkbox popover for the multi-select action filter (same `.dam-glass-topbar`-escaping pattern as `CollectionTreePicker.tsx`, since the top bar's own `backdrop-filter` traps a naively-positioned dropdown, see "Conventions that bite"), a native `<select>` for the user filter (populated from the already company-scoped `GET /api/users`, so a company admin's dropdown can never list a foreign user to begin with), a native range `<select>` (`dark:bg-card`, matching the analytics page's exact convention) — "Load more" appends via the cursor, never re-fetching a row already on screen. Each row: a small action-specific icon, the phrased line (actor/target names bold, share_create's expiry and login's role muted), a relative-time label (`relativeTime()`, a ~20-line local helper — "just now"/"Ns/m/h/d/mo/y ago", no library) with the exact timestamp on hover via `title`. `share_access` rows never invent an actor — always "Someone … via a shared link," visually distinguished with a muted/italic treatment and an amber "External" pill. A super admin additionally sees a small company-name tag per row (`companyName`, populated for every row regardless of caller so the client can choose whether to render it); a company admin's header reads "Your company's activity" instead. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB (real accounts, promoted/reverted through the real API — no Playwright): one real event of each of the six actions generated and phrasing-checked (`login`, `upload` into a real collection, `view`/`download` of a real asset, `share_create` on a real resource share, anonymous `share_access` `viewed`+`downloaded` via the real public share routes); **scope-escape proof** — created a second real company, moved a real user into it and logged them in (a genuine cross-company event with real historical data, not a synthetic row), then confirmed the first company's admin got `events: []` filtering by that user's real id and an unchanged own-scope response when a foreign `companyId` was added to the query string, while the super admin correctly saw the event tagged with the second company's name; pagination — generated 73 real events (60 of them extra logins), fetched two pages via the cursor, confirmed zero overlap, zero duplicates, and strictly descending `(created_at, id)` order held across the page boundary; a genuinely empty scoped-and-filtered query (`share_create` under the company-admin scope, since that creator was a company-less super admin) rendered the clean "No activity recorded in this period" state; an editor/viewer token got a clean 403 from the endpoint directly and no nav item. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; no new dependency. All test-account role/company reassignments, the test share, and the test resources were reverted/deleted afterward — the one thing that couldn't be cleaned up via the app itself is the disposable second company, since no `DELETE /api/companies` endpoint exists (see "Known characteristics / tech debt"). |
| 103 | Company-scoped metadata field definitions — closes the last global-only piece of the metadata system now that the app is multi-tenant. Schema: `metadata_fields.company_id uuid REFERENCES companies(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (nullable — `NULL` = global/Coperon's, applies to everyone; set = that one company's own field), applied directly via `psql` (added to the direct-DDL list below). **Uniqueness on `name` changed from global to per-scope**: the old `metadata_fields_name_key` (a single `UNIQUE(name)`) was dropped and replaced with two partial unique indexes — `metadata_fields_global_name_key ON metadata_fields (name) WHERE company_id IS NULL` and `metadata_fields_tenant_name_key ON metadata_fields (company_id, name) WHERE company_id IS NOT NULL` — a plain `UNIQUE(company_id, name)` wouldn't work here since SQL treats every `NULL` as distinct from every other `NULL`, which would have silently let two different global fields share a name. The one scope rule every touched site now shares: a viewer's visible field set = global fields plus their own company's — never another company's, and never keyed off which company "owns" the asset (an asset reachable by two companies shows each viewer only their own fields and values). One predicate does this everywhere with no branch: `mf.company_id IS NULL OR mf.company_id = $company` — a `null` company (a super admin, or a fail-closed missing-company token) makes the `OR` collapse to exactly `mf.company_id IS NULL` on its own, since `x = NULL` is never true in Postgres. **Sites changed** (every field-read site in the app, confirmed by grepping every `metadata_fields`/`/api/metadata-fields` reference): `GET`/`PUT /api/resources/[id]/metadata` (`app/api/resources/[id]/metadata/route.ts`) — both scope-filtered now; this is the one route `MetadataDrawer`, `ResourceMetadata` (all three mount sites: the drawer, the standalone full-page editor, and its Save-and-next workflow mode), and required-field enforcement (`enforceRequired`) all read through, so none of those components needed a single line changed — they only ever render whatever this route returns. `GET /api/search` — the searchable-metadata `EXISTS` predicate in both the super-admin and non-admin branches gained the same scope filter (global-only for a super admin; global-or-own for everyone else). `lib/exif.ts`'s `applyExtractedMetadata` — gained a required `uploaderCompanyId` parameter filtering the `exif_source`-mapped fields it auto-fills; both call sites (`upload/complete`, `upload/from-url`) now pass the uploader's own `companyId ?? null`. `app/api/metadata-fields/route.ts` (`GET`/`POST`) and `.../[id]/route.ts` (`PATCH`/`DELETE`) — the field-*definition* management routes: `requireSuperAdmin` → `requireAdmin` on all four; `GET` returns every company's fields (with `company_name` joined) for a super admin, global + own only for a company admin; `POST`'s `companyId` is silently overridden from a company admin's token (same discipline as `POST /api/invitations`) or optionally chosen by a super admin (validated against `companies`, defaults to global/`NULL`); `PATCH`/`DELETE` add an ownership check — a company admin gets `403 "Cannot modify this field"` on a global field or another company's, a super admin is unrestricted; the existing `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `resource_field_data.field_id` is unchanged and, confirmed live, only ever removes that one field's own values. Admin UI (`app/admin/metadata-fields/page.tsx`, rewritten) and nav (`components/AppShell.tsx`): the page's gate widened `roleName === 'super_admin'` → `canAdmin`, and the "Metadata" nav item lost `superAdminOnly: true` — editors/viewers still get neither (confirmed: a role-3 and role-4 token both 403 the API directly). A company admin sees "Your fields" (fully editable, no Scope control — always their own company) and "Global fields" (read-only, no Order/Actions column, headed by a "Global — managed by Coperon" badge). A super admin sees "Global fields" (editable, the Add-form's default scope) plus one further panel per company that has defined its own fields, headed by that company's real name — grouping instead of a flat Company column, which also keeps Move-up/down unambiguous: it computes a row's new `sortOrder` from its position within its own scope's subset only, since global and each company's fields keep fully independent `sort_order` sequences (the one ordering convention every read site now shares: global first by `sort_order`, then each company's own, by `sort_order`). Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server, DB, and MinIO with two real companies (the existing "Test Company" plus a newly created "Beta Corp Test", each with a disposable real company-admin account registered/promoted/deleted within this session — no Playwright): `GET` scoping correct for both company admins and the super admin; `POST`'s companyId-spoof silently overridden (verified the response carried the caller's own company, not the spoofed one); `PATCH`/`DELETE` 403s in both directions (global, and cross-company) and success within scope. **The core cross-company scenario:** granted both companies access to the same collection (via the super admin's Access editor), uploaded a real file into it as Beta, set a value on Beta's own required field, then confirmed Acme — now able to reach that very resource (`200`, not `403`) — saw global fields plus only their own, with no `Beta Required Field` and no its value, while Beta's own view showed it; a direct attempt by Acme to write into Beta's field id by guessing it got `400 "Unknown field id(s)"` with the DB value confirmed unchanged; `GET /api/search?q=secret` found the file for Beta, found nothing for Acme on that same reachable resource, and nothing for the super admin (not a global field); the EXIF scope query, run directly against real rows with both companies' fields mapped to the identical `exif_source`, returned exactly the calling company's field and nothing else, and nothing for a null/super-admin caller. All test fields, the test resource/collection, the disposable company, and both disposable accounts were deleted afterward — `metadata_fields` confirmed back to exactly its original 12 global rows, `companies`/`users`/`company_collection_access` counts confirmed back to their pre-session baselines. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; no new dependency. | ✓ tested — API (curl, two real companies, a real cross-company scope collision) + DB (`psql`, EXIF query + full cleanup verification); UI code-reviewed against the established per-tier gating/dirty-state/Save patterns, not browser-verified this session |

never read from any request value). `JwtPayload` gained optional `isInternal` (old tokens fail closed to non-internal). **Third branch on every tenant-scoped route** — target-specific access checks swapped their bypass from `isSuperAdmin` to `isInternal` (super ⇒ internal, so super admins are byte-for-byte unaffected): `collections/[id]` GET/PATCH/DELETE, `collections/[id]/files`, `files/[fileId]`, `ancestors` (both sites), `descendant-count`, `download/[id]`, `resources/[id]` PATCH/DELETE, `thumbnail`, `resources/[id]/collections` (both-sides check), `resources/[id]/view`, `resources/[id]/metadata` (access check), `upload/complete` + `upload/from-url` (reachability only — the Stage-102 "choose a collection" rule deliberately still applies to internal admins, since an unassigned resource would be invisible in their tenant-scoped media view too), `shares` POST, `shares/[id]` PATCH/DELETE + `link`, `lib/permissions.ts::canEditResourceMetadata`, and `collections/[id]/access` GET/PUT (internal gets the **full super-admin behavior** — every tenant's grant state, whole-set replace — since access editing is not on the exceptions list); `metadata-fields/[id]` PATCH/DELETE got a two-tier ownership rule (internal may modify ANY tenant's field, but a **global** field 403s for anyone below super admin); sub-collection-creation parent check bypassed. **List routes** scope internal admins by the acting tenant instead of bypassing, so navigation reads as one tenant's view: a new `lib/session.ts::actingTenantId(payload, request)` reads the `dam_acting_tenant` cookie (uuid-validated) and honors it **only** for internal callers — for everyone else it always returns their own token tenantId, so a non-internal user sending the cookie has it *silently ignored* (proven live: a plain tenant-B admin sending `cookie=TenantA` still got exactly B's media/users). Applied to `GET /api/collections` (both branches + POST's auto-grant target — an internal admin's new collection is granted to the tenant they're acting as), `GET /api/media`, `GET /api/search`, `GET /api/users`, `GET /api/shares` (list filter), `GET /api/analytics`, `GET /api/activity`, `GET`/`POST /api/metadata-fields`, and the field-scope filters inside `resources/[id]/metadata` GET/PUT. **The exceptions (all 403, all verified live):** `POST /api/invitations` rejects an internal non-super admin outright — not even editors/viewers into their own tenant ("internal grants access, not authority to onboard"); `POST /api/tenants` + `?detail=true` stay `requireSuperAdmin`; global metadata-field definitions stay super-admin-only; and **the self-escalation guard** — `PATCH /api/users/[id]`'s new `isInternal` body field is super-admin-only (an internal admin attempting it on anyone, themselves included, → 403), with every successful flag change logged to `access_log` as `action: 'internal_flag_change'` (`tenant_id: null` — the actor is a super admin — so tenant-scoped feeds never surface it; `metadata: {targetUserId, targetEmail, newValue}`, kept from write-time so the event survives the target's later deletion). The same PATCH also widened: an internal admin may edit role-3/4 users in ANY tenant (the same-tenant check is skipped for them) but the peer-admin lock (target role 1/2 → 403), the role-assignment limit (3/4 only), and tenantId-setting (super-only) are all unchanged. `GET /api/tenants` (bare shape only) widened from `requireSuperAdmin` to internal — the switcher's data source; a non-internal admin still 403s every shape. **Tenant switcher (`components/AppShell.tsx`):** internal non-super users get an amber "Acting as" pill + native tenant `<select>` in the shell's own topbar — on every page, at all times, so whose data is on screen is never ambiguous; selection persists in the `dam_acting_tenant` cookie (chosen over localStorage precisely so the browser sends it on every API request with zero per-fetch-call-site wiring — the server-side `actingTenantId` reads the cookie header directly, working for both `Request` and `NextRequest`); **default = their own tenant** (stated choice: switching is always an explicit act, never a surprise; no "all" option — every internal view is some real tenant's view); changing it writes the cookie and hard-reloads so every page refetches under the new tenant; sign-out clears the cookie so the next login in the same browser never inherits a stale selection. **Users page:** new "Internal" column — role-1 rows show a static "Always" badge (nothing to toggle, hardcoded), other rows a toggle switch for super admins (wired through the existing pending/dirty/Save flow, not an instant write) and a read-only Internal/— badge for everyone else; the "Invite a teammate" section is hidden for internal viewers (their POST would 403 — the UI/server-mismatch rule). **Activity page:** `internal_flag_change` added to the action whitelist/filter, icon, and phrasing ("X granted/removed internal access to/from Y").
