| 104 | **Terminology rename: `company` → `tenant`, throughout the database and codebase.** Pure mechanical rename — zero behavior change, same access/capabilities/everything, only names differ. UI copy is explicitly exempted and still says "Company" everywhere a user sees it (nav item, table headers, form labels, the tenant-management page's own copy, every inline/toast error message) — only identifiers changed. **Schema** (one transaction, full statement list in the Database section's direct-DDL entry): `companies` → `tenants`, `company_collection_access` → `tenant_collection_access`; `company_id` → `tenant_id` on `users`, `invitations`, `access_log`, `metadata_fields`, and `tenant_collection_access` itself; every FK/PK constraint and the two named indexes carrying "company" renamed to match. No new columns, no dropped data — verified by row-count diff (2 tenants, 8 users, 12 global metadata fields, 4 grant rows, 136 resources, 32 collections, 6 shares, all unchanged across the rename). **Code**: `companyId`→`tenantId` (JWT claim + every route/component), `company_id`/`company_name`→`tenant_id`/`tenant_name`, `companyHasResourceAccess`/`companyHasCollectionAccess`→`tenantHasResourceAccess`/`tenantHasCollectionAccess`, `COMPANY_ADMIN_CAN_EDIT_ACCESS`→`TENANT_ADMIN_CAN_EDIT_ACCESS`, `resolveShareOwningCompany`→`resolveShareOwningTenant`, `isCompanyStub`→`isTenantStub`, plus the less-obvious ones a mechanical pass tends to miss: `COMPANY_ADMIN_ASSIGNABLE_ROLES`/`_ROLE_IDS` (two separate constants, `lib/invitations.ts` and `app/admin/users/page.tsx`/`app/api/users/[id]/route.ts`), the `companyDetails`/`needsCompanyDetails` invitation-redemption fields (both request-body and response-shape, kept in sync across `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx` and the three invitation routes), and the analytics/activity `scope.type: 'company' | 'all'` discriminant (renamed to `'tenant' | 'all'` in `app/api/analytics/route.ts`, `app/api/activity/route.ts`, and both consuming admin pages — missed on the first codemod pass since it's a string literal, not an identifier token). `GET`/`POST /api/companies` moved to `/api/tenants` (route folder renamed, response keys `{companies}`→`{tenants}` on both the bare and `?detail=true` shapes); `app/admin/companies/page.tsx` moved to `app/admin/tenants/page.tsx`; `components/AppShell.tsx`'s nav `key` renamed `"companies"`→`"tenants"` (the `label` stays "Companies", unchanged). **Method**: a from-scratch grep inventory (980 case-insensitive hits across 54 files) classified into rename/keep/obsolete; a two-pass Node codemod handled the ~800 unambiguous identifier tokens (camelCase/snake_case fields, function/constant names, route paths — including a second pass for capitalized compound forms like `setCompanyFilter` that the first pass's lowercase-anchored patterns missed), a comment-only pass (line-trimmed-starts-with-`//`/`*` guard, so it structurally cannot touch a quoted string) handled recurring prose phrases, and the remainder — every bare `Company`/`company`/`companies` occurrence, one file at a time — was read and hand-classified: `scripts/migrate-to-companies.mjs` left untouched (already obsolete, references the dropped `group_collection_access`/`user_groups` tables); every genuine UI-facing string left as "Company"; everything else renamed. Final grep confirms zero identifier hits remain — every surviving "company" is either that one obsolete script or a deliberately-kept UI string. Verified live end-to-end with two real tenants (the existing "Test Company" plus a disposable "Beta Corp Test", both with disposable admin/editor/viewer accounts created and deleted within the session, no Playwright): login (JWT decoded to confirm `tenantId` claim, every other claim byte-for-byte unchanged), collection create/access/cross-tenant-403, media library scoping, a real upload through the full presigned-URL pipeline, tenant-scoped metadata fields (including the Stage 103 cross-tenant scope-collision case — a resource reachable by both tenants correctly showed each admin only their own field/value), search isolation, share creation + anonymous public access, an invitation created/listed/revoked (response shape `{tenant: {...}}`, renamed from `{company: {...}}`), analytics (`scope.type: 'tenant'`) and activity feed, the users-page `roleId`+`tenantId` filters, and editor/viewer 403 boundaries. **Old-token fail-closed, explicitly proven**: a JWT hand-minted with the pre-rename shape (`companyId` claim, no `tenantId`) was run against `GET /api/media` (empty result, not an error), `GET /api/metadata-fields` (empty list), `GET /api/analytics` (zero-scope response), and `POST /api/collections` (clean 403) — every route treated the missing `tenantId` as "no tenant," never crashed. | 107 | **Profile page** — closes the gap Stage 105's own entry flagged ("a tenant admin will fill [tenant address/phone] via the profile page (Stage 4 of this arc, not built)"). Every authenticated role gets `app/profile/page.tsx` (inside `AppShell`, `active="profile"` — a new `ActiveSection` value with no matching `NAV_ITEMS` row, so nothing in the sidebar ever highlights for it; reached only via a new "Profile" item in the account-menu popover, between the dark-mode toggle and Sign out, unconditional — no role gate on the link itself). Four sections, three genuinely new, one reusing an existing endpoint: **Name** — new `GET`/`PATCH /api/profile` (`requireAuth`; self-service, the mirror-image restriction of `PATCH /api/users/[id]`'s admin-only/no-self-edit rule); blank name rejected 400, matching Stage 66's "name is required" rationale. **Password** — no new backend at all; the form posts straight to the existing `POST /api/auth/change-password`, restating `lib/users.ts::validatePasswordStrength`'s rules as a live checklist (byte-identical array to `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`'s own restatement, same "must be kept in sync by hand" caveat). Success message states plainly that other sessions are **not** invalidated — JWTs are stateless with no revocation list, so a password change only stops a *future* login with the old password, never an already-issued token before its 1-day expiry; this is a factual statement, not a new capability. **Email — the real new feature:** submitting a new address never touches `users.email`. New `lib/emailChange.ts` mirrors `lib/auth.ts`'s forgot/reset-password token shape exactly (sha256-hashed-at-rest, single-use via `used_at`, direct `WHERE token_hash = $1` lookup — deliberately not bcrypt like `lib/shares.ts`/`lib/invitations.ts`, which need an id+token composite; sha256 needs only the raw token) rather than inventing a second pattern. Schema: `email_tokens.purpose` CHECK widened to add `'email_change'` (`DROP`/`ADD CONSTRAINT`, run so uniqueness was never briefly unenforced) and a new nullable `email_tokens.new_email text` column (holds the pending address; only set for this purpose). `POST /api/profile/email-change` (`requireAuth`) validates format/length, 400s if identical to the current address, 409s if another account already owns it (`SELECT ... WHERE email = $1`, same case-sensitive exact-match convention as every other email-uniqueness check in this app), deletes any of the caller's own still-pending `email_change` rows first (only one pending change at a time — a fresh request supersedes, never stacks), inserts a new 24h-expiring row, and emails the confirmation link to the **new** address via a new `sendEmailChangeConfirmation` in `lib/mail.ts` (same Gmail/nodemailer transporter already proven delivering for reset/invitation email) — proving control of the new inbox is the entire point. `DELETE /api/profile/email-change` cancels (deletes the pending row; `GET /api/profile`'s `pendingEmailChange` field reflects `null` on the very next fetch). New public page `app/confirm-email/page.tsx` (`AuthShell`, `?token=` query param matching `/reset-password`'s own shape) deliberately splits read from write, same as invitations: `GET /api/profile/email-change/validate` (public, no auth — the link is emailed to the new address, which may not be logged in on this device at all) is read-only and never marks the token used, so an email client's link-prescanner (Outlook Safe Links etc.) can't itself burn it; only an explicit "Confirm change" button click calls `POST /api/profile/email-change/confirm` (public), which does the actual atomic single-use swap — same accept-then-act `UPDATE ... WHERE used_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now() RETURNING ...` shape as `POST /api/invitations/redeem`, one fixed generic message for every failure mode (never-existed/wrong-token/expired/cancelled/already-used all collapse identically, no oracle) — with one deliberate exception: a conflict discovered *at confirm time* (another account claimed the exact address in the interim) gets its own distinct 409, since that's a genuine data race, not a dead-token problem. **Company details** — new `PATCH /api/profile/tenant` (`requireAuth` + `user.roleId === 2`, not `canAdmin` — a true super admin has `canAdmin` too but `roleId 1`, and must never reach this route). Scoped **from the token's `tenantId` alone** — `lib/session.ts::actingTenantId()` is never imported here, so the acting-tenant switcher (internal admins only) has zero effect on which tenant this edits, a deliberate answer to "is that allowed": **no**, editing "my company" always means the caller's own home tenant, switcher selection or not, enforced simply by the route never reading the cookie. `GET /api/profile` only ever returns a `tenant` object when `role_id === 2`; true super admins (`role_id === 1`, no tenant at all) get `tenant: null` and the Profile page omits the section entirely for them (the task's other offered option — "let them pick" — was rejected: that duplicates the already-existing, dedicated `/admin/tenants` super-admin surface for editing an arbitrary tenant, and this section is specifically "edit *my own* company"). Validation/duplicate-name-check mirrors `POST /api/tenants` exactly (name/address/phone all required if present in the body, capped 200/255/30, case-insensitive duplicate 409 excluding the tenant's own current row so re-saving an unchanged name isn't a false conflict). Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB with real accounts and real Gmail SMTP (no Playwright): a disposable tenant + tenant-admin account (created via a crafted-invitation-row `psql` insert + real redeem, same technique Stage 105 used) exercised every path — name save + blank-name 400; wrong-current-password 401, weak-new-password 400, correct change 200 with the **old password confirmed dead and the new one working** on real subsequent logins; email-change same-as-current 400, duplicate-existing-user 409, a **real** SMTP send to a real Gmail inbox (200, DB row confirmed sha256-hashed with no plaintext token), pending state surfaced via `GET /api/profile` and cleared instantly on cancel; a separately-crafted known token exercised `validate`→`confirm`→**real `users.email` row updated**→re-confirm same token generically invalid→expired-token generically invalid→**login with the old email now fails, login with the new one works**; a crafted confirm-time collision against a real existing user's address correctly 409'd with the distinct message, left both users' emails unchanged, and (a genuine finding, not asserted) left the token **un-consumed** since the whole transaction rolled back together — a legitimate retry-later behavior, not a bug, so left as-is; company-details validation (blank 400, duplicate-name 409 against the real "Test Company," own-name re-save not a false 409) and a real three-field update, confirmed via `GET /api/profile` and `psql`; an editor account confirmed `tenant: null` and a 403 on the tenant route while still successfully renaming itself; **the switcher-ignored proof** — flipped the disposable tenant admin to `is_internal: true`, sent a live `PATCH /api/profile/tenant` request carrying a spoofed `dam_acting_tenant` cookie pointing at the real Test Company, and confirmed via `psql` that only the caller's own disposable tenant changed — Test Company's row was byte-for-byte untouched. All disposable accounts/tenants/tokens deleted afterward (cascades handled the email_tokens/invitation rows); one real account's `name` was touched incidentally during a role-boundary check and reverted to its prior `NULL` immediately after. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; no new dependency. | ✓ tested — API (curl, real SMTP send, real password/email swaps confirmed via subsequent real logins) + DB (`psql`, hash/plaintext check, baseline restore) + the switcher-ignored enforcement proven live with a spoofed cookie, not just reasoned |

| 108 | **Per-tenant editor/viewer permissions.** Admin (role 2) and super-admin (role 1) capability stays fixed — never configurable, the guard against a tenant admin escalating their own role; only editor(3)/viewer(4) capability is now per-tenant configurable, resolved from the DB per request (never the JWT), so revocation is immediate with no re-login. New table `tenant_role_permissions` (`tenant_id, role_id, permission_key, enabled`, composite PK) — **absence of a row = the code-defined default** in `lib/permissions.ts`, so a new tenant needs no seeding and a future permission key gets a sensible default automatically for every existing tenant. **Defaults, audited route-by-route against the real running code (not guessed), reported here since the task asked for it explicitly:** editor — `upload` true, `edit_metadata` true, `remove_from_collection` true (all three already reachable via `canUpload`/`canEditResourceMetadata` before this stage); `rename_asset`, `regenerate_thumbnail`, `add_to_collection`, `create_share`, `create_collection`, `rename_collection`, `delete_permanently` all **false** (every one of these was `requireAdmin`-only on disk — an editor could not reach any of them at all before this stage). **Correction worth flagging:** `add_to_collection` reads as "probably on" if you assume it pairs with `remove_from_collection`, but `POST /api/resources/[id]/collections` was actually `requireAdmin`-only (Stage 101) — its default is false, a genuinely new opt-in editor capability, not a widened existing one. Viewer — `download` true, `view_metadata` true (both already open to any role with resource access before this stage), `create_share` false (was `requireAdmin`-only, same as the editor column). Existing tenants see zero behavior change on migration day — confirmed by construction (no rows exist yet, so every lookup falls through to these exact defaults) and re-confirmed live below. **Resolver** (`lib/permissions.ts`): `hasPermission(user, key)` — internal/super-admin/admin(role 1/2) → true unconditionally; `download`/`view_metadata` → true unconditionally for role 3 too (`EDITOR_IMPLICIT_TRUE`, since neither is in `EDITOR_DEFAULTS` at all — an editor's baseline already included both, so these are the one asymmetric pair: viewer-configurable only); role 3/4 → DB-backed, falling back to the code default; everyone else (pending, or a key not defined for that role, e.g. a viewer checking `upload`) → false, fail-closed. **Per-request memoization**, exactly as the task required ("don't issue N queries per route"): a `WeakMap<object, Promise<Map>>` keyed on the `JwtPayload` object reference itself — `requireAuth`/`jwt.verify` mints a fresh object every request, so the cache entry is reachable only for that one request's lifetime and needs no manual cleanup, no module-level mutable state, no cross-request/cross-user leak risk; every `hasPermission` call (sequential or concurrent) for the same request's `user` object shares the one query. `requirePermission(request, key)` wraps `requireAuth` + `hasPermission` in the same `JwtPayload \| Response` shape as `requireAdmin`/`requireUpload`/`requireDownload`, so a route swap is a one-line change. `getEffectivePermissions(user)` resolves all 12 distinct keys at once (the union of both role lists, `create_share` counted once) — the frontend's "my permissions" bundle. **Every capability check replaced, reported per the task's own ask:** `POST /api/upload/url`, `POST /api/upload/complete`, `POST /api/upload/from-url` (`requireUpload` → `requirePermission(…, 'upload')`); `DELETE /api/collections/[id]/files/[fileId]` (`requireUpload` → `'remove_from_collection'`); `GET /api/download/[id]` (`requireDownload` → `'download'`); `lib/permissions.ts::canEditResourceMetadata` (gained an `edit_metadata` check ahead of the existing `tenantHasResourceAccess` call — a no-op for role 2, since `hasPermission` already returns true for it); `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` (was bare `requireAuth` → gained a `'view_metadata'` check after the existing cascade access check); `PATCH /api/resources/[id]` (`requireAdmin` → `requirePermission(…, 'rename_asset')`, floor only — the route's own tenant-scope check is unchanged, and `description` edits ride the same `rename_asset` boundary since the task defines no separate key for it); `DELETE /api/resources/[id]` (`requireAdmin` → `'delete_permanently'`); `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` (`requireAdmin` → `'regenerate_thumbnail'`); `POST /api/resources/[id]/collections` (`requireAdmin` → `'add_to_collection'`); `POST /api/collections` (`requireAdmin` → `'create_collection'` — both the top-level and sub-collection branches, unchanged otherwise, since neither ever special-cased "admin" beyond the floor); `PATCH /api/collections/[id]` (floor widened `requireAdmin` → `requireAuth`, then field-split: `name` requires `'rename_collection'`, `coverStorageKey` stays a direct `canAdmin`/`isInternal` check — no permission key exists for "change thumbnail," deliberately, per the task's own key list); `POST /api/shares` (`requireAdmin` → `requirePermission(…, 'create_share')` — independently resolved per the caller's actual role, so an editor's and a viewer's `create_share` toggles are two unrelated DB rows). **Admin UI** — new `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/permissions` (`requireAdmin`; tenant resolved from the token for a regular tenant admin, from `actingTenantId` for an internal admin, from `?tenantId=`/body **only** for a true super admin — never the other way, and every write rejects `roleId` 1/2 outright with a fixed message even though the UI never sends it) and `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx` (new "Permissions" sidebar item, `adminOnly: true, superAdminOnly: false` — same tier as Users/Media/Analytics; a super admin gets an in-page tenant `<select>`, a tenant admin's own tenant is implicit) — a 12-row × 2-column matrix (rows in the task's own given order, a key with no cell for a role rendered as a plain "—", never a toggle that would 403), each cell an instant-toggle (no batched Save, matching the app's established is_internal/dark-mode instant-toggle convention) showing Default/Custom and a "Reset" link when overridden. **UI affordances follow permissions**, audited for the visible-but-403 pattern the task named explicitly: every button/kebab item on `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` (the one page both editors and viewers actually use — `admin/media` is admin-tier-only and thus unaffected, since admin capability never changes) that maps to one of the ten now-configurable keys was re-gated from a bare `canAdmin`/`canUpload` check to `(canAdmin \|\| myPerms.KEY)`, fed by a new `GET /api/permissions/mine` fetched once on mount into a `myPerms` bundle — initialized optimistically to mirror **exactly** pre-Stage-108 visibility (`upload`/`edit_metadata`/`remove_from_collection` following the already-known `canUpload`, `download`/`view_metadata` defaulting true) so there is no render flash for the common case, corrected the moment the real fetch resolves. Every site touched: the top-bar Import URL/Upload controls (`upload`); the top-bar collection kebab, split so "New sub-collection"/"Rename" check `create_collection`/`rename_collection` while "Change thumbnail"/"Access"/"Delete collection" stay hard `canAdmin` (no key); both sub-collection kebabs (grid + list, identical split, `rename_collection` only — Access/Delete have no key); the per-file kebab in both grid and list views — Rename/Edit metadata/Generate-or-Regenerate thumbnail/Remove from collection/Delete permanently, each its own key; the bulk-action bar's Download selected/Remove from collection/Delete permanently (the first of these had **no gate at all before this stage** — always visible — now correctly follows `download`); the per-card and per-row Download button and "Details" (metadata-drawer) trigger, previously unconditional for every role, now follow `download`/`view_metadata` respectively; `MetadataDrawer`'s `canEdit` prop (`edit_metadata`). **`create_share`'s new UI surface** — deliberately called out, since unlike every other permission this widens an *existing* button, no editor/viewer share-creation UI existed anywhere before this stage (the only share UI was the admin-only `/admin/shares` page). Added a minimal "Share" kebab item (grid + list, gated on `myPerms.create_share`, which widens the kebab's own outer trigger to include a viewer with the permission) opening a small self-contained modal (access level, expiry, POST straight to the now-permission-gated `/api/shares`, one-time-shown link with copy) — intentionally not a duplicate of the full admin shares page, just enough to make the permission clickable. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB with two full real tenants and five disposable real accounts (a tenant admin + editor + viewer in each of "Perm Test A"/"Perm Test B", created via crafted-invitation-row `psql` insert + real redeem, same technique prior stages used; no Playwright): the default matrix fetched via `GET /api/permissions` matched the audited defaults above exactly, byte for byte; a real editor's `GET /api/permissions/mine` matched; every default-denied action (`rename_asset`, `delete_permanently`, `create_collection`, `create_share`, `add_to_collection`) 403'd for a real editor and every default-allowed one (`upload`, `remove_from_collection`) succeeded; a real viewer's `download`/`view_metadata` succeeded and `create_share`/`upload` 403'd; **the core "no re-login" proof** — a tenant admin toggled `rename_asset` on for Tenant A's editors mid-session, and the *exact same, already-issued* editor JWT (no fresh login) went from 403 to 200 on its very next request, then back to 403 after a `DELETE`-triggered reset-to-default, all with the identical token; **cross-tenant isolation proven in both directions** — Tenant A's toggle left a real Tenant B editor's `rename_asset` untouched (still false), and a super admin enabling `create_collection` for Tenant B's editors left Tenant A's editor still 403ing; a regular tenant admin's `?tenantId=` query naming the *other* real tenant was silently ignored, confirmed by the response echoing their own tenant id back regardless; a super admin's explicit `?tenantId=` correctly targeted the named tenant; **the fixed-capability boundary held under a forced API call**, not just an absent UI control — a real tenant admin's `PUT` with `roleId: 2` or `roleId: 1` both got the same fixed 403 message, and an invalid key for a role (`roleId: 3` + `permissionKey: "download"`) got 400; a real editor and a real viewer both got a clean 403 from `GET`/`PUT /api/permissions` directly (no nav item either, same `adminOnly` gate every other admin page uses). All five disposable accounts and both disposable tenants deleted afterward (cascading their `tenant_role_permissions`/`tenant_collection_access`/`invitations` rows); one harmless residual finding, left alone rather than reverted: two real, pre-existing tenants ("Test Company," "Activity Scope Test Co") each carry one `tenant_role_permissions` override row matching their own code default exactly (`create_share: false`, `rename_collection: false`) — most likely from the user exploring the new `/admin/permissions` page live in a real browser during this session; functionally inert (resolves identically to the default either way) and not test residue this session created, so not deleted. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; no new dependency. | ✓ tested — API (curl, two full real tenants + five disposable real accounts, the no-re-login proof with a single unrefreshed token, bidirectional cross-tenant isolation, the fixed-capability boundary forced via direct API calls) + DB (`psql`, baseline restore); UI code-reviewed against the established instant-toggle/dirty-state conventions, not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint |

| 111 | Three small UI/data fixes. **Permissions-page header missing a space** (`app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`) — rendered as "…in Activity Scope Test Cocan do." Root cause: JSX trims leading whitespace on the first line of a multi-line text node, so a literal space in the source (right after `{matrix?.tenantName ?? "this company"}</span>`) was silently dropped at compile time, never visible except in the rendered browser output. Fixed with an explicit `{" "}` immediately after the `</span>` (the same pattern already used elsewhere on the same line, and on `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`). Every other tenant-name interpolation in the app was checked for the identical shape (a `</span>`/expression immediately followed by lowercase text that continues onto further source lines) — no other instance found. **Native-`<select>` dark-mode opacity audit** — all 31 `<select>` elements app-wide (activity, analytics, media, metadata-fields, permissions, shares, tenants, users, collection detail, home, `AppShell`, `PhoneInput`) checked for the recorded translucent-popup issue; every one already uses `bg-card` (itself theme-reactive via the `[data-theme="dark"]` token swap) or `bg-white/NN dark:bg-card` — the Stage 76 convention had already propagated everywhere. No changes made. **`IL` (Israel) removed from `lib/countries.ts`** — a business/legal requirement for operating in Lebanon; confirmed by grep it's the only reference to `IL`/`Israel` anywhere in `app`/`components`/`lib`. List is now 230 entries (was 231), alphabetically contiguous (Iraq → Ireland, no gap); `PhoneInput`'s `DEFAULT_COUNTRY_CODE` (`LB`) and the `COUNTRIES[0]` fallback (Afghanistan) are both unaffected. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. | ✓ built — `tsc`/`next build` clean; select audit and countries-list grep done directly against the source, not browser-verified (no Playwright this session) |

| 112 | Auto-login on invite redemption. `POST /api/invitations/redeem` no longer just creates the user and stops — after the transaction `COMMIT`, it re-reads the new user via `lib/users.ts`'s `findByEmail` (the exact same query `login()` uses) and mints a session token via a newly-extracted `lib/auth.ts::mintSessionToken(user)` — `login()`'s own payload-building/`jwt.sign` block, pulled out into a shared function so both call sites can never drift apart. The 201 response now carries `access_token` alongside the existing `message`/`email` fields. A failed or reused redemption (bad/expired/already-accepted token, the single-use `UPDATE` finding 0 rows, a duplicate-email `23505`) returns before the `COMMIT` and never reaches the mint call, so it never sets a session — unchanged from before. `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`: on a 201 response, stores `access_token` to `localStorage` under the same `"token"` key `app/page.tsx`'s login form uses, then redirects to `/home` (was `/` after ~1.8s, showing "Redirecting you to log in…") instead of the login page. Nothing else about the invitation flow changed — email is still taken from the invite record only, the single-use gate is still the same atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE accepted_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now()`, phone/password validation is untouched, no new dependency, no schema change. Verified live against the real dev server/DB (no Playwright): crafted an invitation row directly via `psql` (bcrypt-hashed raw token, editor role, Test Company) and redeemed it over the real API — response carried a working `access_token`; decoded and diffed its payload against a fresh `POST /api/auth/login` for the same account with `iat`/`exp` stripped — byte-for-byte identical (`sub`, `email`, `name`, `canDownload`, `canUpload`, `canAdmin`, `tenantId`, `roleId`, `roleName`, `canAccessAllTenants`, `canInvite`); the minted token authenticated a real `GET /api/collections` call (200); replaying the same invite link afterward returned the generic invalid-link error with no `access_token` in the body. Test invitation and user deleted afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. | ✓ tested — API (curl + psql-crafted invitation, payload diff against a real login, authenticated follow-up call, reuse-fails-cleanly check); UI code-reviewed against `app/page.tsx`'s established `localStorage`/redirect pattern, not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint |

| 113 | Permanent tenant deletion — closes the gap this project has carried since Stage 99 (the stray "Activity Scope Test Co" residue, undeletable through the app's own API, documented under "Known characteristics / tech debt" below). New `DELETE /api/tenants/[id]` (`requireSuperAdmin` — a tenant admin gets 403 even for their own tenant, no self-service path exists at all) plus a `GET /api/tenants/[id]` read-only preview sharing the exact same exclusivity computation, so the confirmation modal's counts can never drift from what the delete actually does. **FK audit first** (six FKs reference `tenants`): `access_log.tenant_id` — `SET NULL`, untouched, this is the whole point; `invitations.tenant_id`, `tenant_collection_access.tenant_id`, `metadata_fields.tenant_id`, `tenant_role_permissions.tenant_id` — all `CASCADE`, so deleting the tenant row alone already cleans up three of the task's five required deletions plus the grants; `users.tenant_id` — `NO ACTION` (deliberately: a user must never be left with a dangling tenant reference), so the tenant's users must be explicitly `DELETE`d *before* the tenant row, in the same transaction. **"The tenant's collections," defined and computed once, reused by GET and DELETE alike:** a collection is exclusive to a tenant only if no ancestor-or-self in its chain carries a grant for any other tenant — one recursive CTE walks every collection to its root and groups the tenant ids found in each chain (chain = only the target tenant → exclusive; chain = target plus at least one other → shared, survives, only the target's own grant disappears via the tenant-row cascade). A resource is exclusive only if *every* `collection_resource` row it has points into the exclusive set — one surviving membership (a shared collection, or another tenant's entirely) keeps the resource, just drops the one link. **The cascade-safety subtlety this stage exists to get right:** `collections.parent_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, which knows nothing about grants — deleting an exclusive collection would blindly delete a *shared* collection nested beneath it too, unless stopped first. Fix: before deleting, any shared collection whose direct parent is in the exclusive set is `UPDATE`d to `parent_id = NULL` (becomes a new root); proven sufficient in a single non-recursive pass because every collection beneath a shared collection is provably shared too (the same inherited grant covers it) — verified live below with a three-level chain built specifically to exercise this (an exclusive root → an exclusive child with no grant of its own → a leaf with its own separate grant to a second tenant). Surviving collections' `cover_storage_key` is nulled first if it points at a file about to be deleted (same hygiene as `DELETE /api/resources/[id]`). One transaction: lock the tenant row (`FOR UPDATE`, mirrors `DELETE /api/users/[id]`), require `confirmName` matching the tenant's current name exactly (case-sensitive, trimmed — 400 on mismatch, nothing touched, checked before any destructive query), refuse (409) if any `role_id = 1` user somehow has this `tenant_id` (shouldn't happen — super admins have no tenant — fails loudly rather than ever deleting one), detach shared subtrees, delete exclusive resources then exclusive collections, delete the tenant's users, delete the tenant row (cascades the rest). **MinIO cleanup only after commit** — each deleted resource's `storage_key` and `thumbnail_storage_key` deleted in a loop; a failed object delete is logged and skipped, never rolled back into the DB state. Logs `tenant_delete` to `access_log` (`tenantId: null` — actor's own, always null for a super admin; target id/name and every count in `metadata`, same "describe the gone target from write-time metadata" convention as `user_delete`) — added to the activity feed's action whitelist, icon, and phrasing (`app/api/activity/route.ts`, `app/admin/activity/page.tsx`). UI: a red "Delete" button per row on `app/admin/tenants/page.tsx` opens a modal naming the company, stating plainly what's destroyed, showing real counts from the GET preview, and requiring the exact typed name before "Delete permanently" un-disables; Cancel is the only reachable action otherwise. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB/MinIO with two real tenants and five real accounts, no Playwright: built a five-collection tree under Tenant A — an exclusive root → exclusive child → a leaf granted to *both* A and B (the detach-test case), plus a separate solo-exclusive collection and a separate root granted to both A and B directly — and four real uploaded-through-the-real-presigned-URL files (one exclusive-only, one in the shared leaf, one in the shared root, one linked into *both* an exclusive and a shared collection to prove mixed-membership survival), a tenant-A-scoped metadata field, a `tenant_role_permissions` override, a redeemed admin + editor in Tenant A, a redeemed admin in Tenant B, and a still-pending invitation; `GET` preview matched exactly (2 users, 3 collections to delete, 2 skipped as shared, 1 resource to delete) before anything ran; a wrong `confirmName` 400'd with zero rows touched; Tenant A's own admin got 403 deleting their own tenant; the real delete returned the identical counts the preview promised; afterward — both users gone, all three exclusive collections gone, both shared collections survived (the nested one correctly detached to `parent_id: null`), the exclusive-only file gone from both the DB and a direct MinIO `HeadObject` check, the three surviving files' MinIO objects confirmed still present, the mixed file's link to the deleted collection gone while its link to the shared collection remained, the pending invitation/metadata field/permission override all gone (cascade); **the critical check** — Tenant B's own admin, via real API calls (not DB inspection), still listed both surviving collections at the top level and successfully downloaded all three surviving files; pre-existing `access_log` rows for Tenant A's now-deleted editor (a login and a download, generated before deletion) came back with `user_id`/`tenant_id` both `null` via the real `GET /api/activity` response, rendering as "(deleted user)" client-side exactly like every other FK-SET-NULL actor; the `tenant_delete` event itself carried the actor (the super admin who ran it, not null) and `detail.targetTenantName`/counts pulled from metadata. Both disposable tenants (including Tenant B, which by then exclusively owned the two surviving collections — deleted using this same new feature, a real dogfooding pass) and all test data/MinIO objects cleaned up afterward; the pre-existing "Activity Scope Test Co" residue was deliberately left alone (now deletable, but contains what looks like a real, non-synthetic user account — removing it wasn't asked for). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; no new dependency. | ✓ tested — API + DB (`psql`) + MinIO (direct `HeadObjectCommand` check) end to end with two real tenants, five real accounts, and four real uploaded files; UI (the delete modal) code-reviewed against the established modal/dam-glass conventions, not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint |

| 114 | Custom `Select` component, replacing every native `<select>` app-wide. Root cause: a native `<option>`'s text is drawn by the browser itself, in the OS font — no CSS reaches it — so every option popup rendered inconsistently beside `components/CollectionTreePicker.tsx`, which is a real DOM component and already uses the app font. New `components/Select.tsx`: a flat listbox, mechanically `CollectionTreePicker` with the tree flattened to a plain option list — same `createPortal(..., document.body)` at `position: fixed` computed from the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect()` (the Stage 71 backdrop-filter/overflow escape hatch), same outside-click/Escape handling. Extended with full listbox keyboard parity a flat list needs but a tree never did: Up/Down to move, Enter/Space to select, Escape to close, Home/End, and typeahead (600ms buffer, searches forward from the active option and wraps). Props: `value`, `onChange(value: string)`, `options: {value,label}[]`, `placeholder?`, `disabled?`, `id?` (so an existing `<label htmlFor>` still resolves — `<button>` is a labelable element per spec), `ariaLabel?`, `className?` (full override, same convention as `CollectionTreePicker`'s own `className` prop). Unresolved-value fallback (`…`) reproduces the one existing case that needed it — `AppShell`'s acting-tenant switcher showing a placeholder while `tenantOptions` is still loading. **All 31 native `<select>` elements converted** (grep-audited before and after — zero remain, comments aside): `components/AppShell.tsx` (acting-as switcher, topbar); `components/PhoneInput.tsx` (230-option country code — now benefits from typeahead, the one site whose own comment had argued for staying native, superseded here); `app/home/page.tsx` (sort, per-page); `app/admin/activity/page.tsx` (user filter, date range); `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` (share-modal access level + expiry, sub-collection sort, sub-collection per-page, files per-page); `app/admin/analytics/page.tsx` (date range); `app/admin/media/page.tsx` (type filter, sort filter, per-page); `app/admin/shares/page.tsx` (target type, access level, expiry — inside a `<form>`); `app/admin/users/page.tsx` (role filter, company filter, per-row company, per-row role, invite-role — the last inside a `<form>`); `app/admin/metadata-fields/page.tsx` (per-row field type, per-row EXIF source, new-field type, scope, EXIF source — the last three inside a `<form>`); `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx` (tenant picker); `app/admin/tenants/page.tsx` (invite company, invite role — inside a `<form>`). **Form audit, reported since the task asked for it:** none of the 31 carried `required` or `name` — all were fully React-controlled (`value`/`onChange`), so none ever participated in native form-data serialization. Three sites sit inside a `<form>` (shares, users' invite section, tenants' invite section); each already had a real `<button type="submit">` and already contained a `CollectionTreePicker` (itself a `<button>`, never Enter-submittable) before this stage, so converting the remaining selects to buttons removed no Enter-to-submit behavior that wasn't already inconsistent within the same form. **Dead code removed:** 8 now-unused local `ChevronIcon()` helpers (one per file that had rendered its own chevron beside an `appearance-none` select) — `app/admin/activity/page.tsx`, `app/admin/analytics/page.tsx`, `app/admin/media/page.tsx` (no local helper — used inline), `app/admin/shares/page.tsx`, `app/admin/users/page.tsx`, `app/admin/metadata-fields/page.tsx`, `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`, `app/admin/tenants/page.tsx`; `app/admin/activity/page.tsx` kept its `ChevronIcon`, still used by the unrelated `ActionFilter` checkbox popover. **Known characteristic, stated explicitly:** the panel is positioned once at open time from the trigger's bounding rect and does not reposition on scroll — scrolling the page while a panel is open visually detaches it from its trigger, exactly matching `CollectionTreePicker`'s own pre-existing behavior (no scroll listener there either); kept deliberately for consistency rather than inventing new scroll-close logic for only the new component — this characteristic now affects 31 dropdowns instead of 1 (see "Known characteristics" below). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean; no new dependency. | ✓ type-checked — `tsc`/`next build` clean, grep-confirmed zero native `<select>` remain; **not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint** — the visual pass (option-text font match beside `CollectionTreePicker`, focus rings, click/scroll behavior) is pending, flagged in "Known characteristics" below with the four highest-value spots to check first |

In `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`'s `PermissionCell`: removed the per-row `handleReset()` function and its "Reset" `<button>` (the `DELETE /api/permissions` call it made — untouched, still reachable exactly as before via nothing in the per-row UI now, since the endpoint itself wasn't touched, only its one caller); removed the `Default`/`Custom` `<span>` label. The cell now renders only `<Toggle>` (plus the inline error line on a failed write) — the em-dash fallback for a role/key pair that doesn't apply is unchanged. Both possible cell states are now fixed-width (a toggle, or an em-dash), so the Editor/Viewer columns align cleanly instead of shifting per row. Presentation only: `handleToggle` (`PUT /api/permissions`, writes an override) is byte-for-byte unchanged, the header's `handleResetAll`/"Reset all to defaults" button is untouched, the resolver/defaults/`DELETE /api/permissions` endpoint were never touched — only the per-row UI affordance calling that endpoint was removed. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. | ✓ type-checked — `tsc`/`next build` clean, grep-confirmed no per-row Reset/Default/Custom text remains, only the header's "Reset all to defaults"; **not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint** — the visual pass (column alignment, toggle still writes, "Reset all" still clears) is pending, see "Known characteristics" below |
| 116 | UI localization — Stage A: infrastructure + first converted page. Hand-rolled i18n, no new dependency (`next-intl` rejected — its locale-prefixed routing model doesn't fit a client-side toggle with no URL/SEO need here). New `lib/i18n.tsx`: `TranslationProvider` (context) + `useTranslation()` → `{t, locale, setLocale}`; `t(key, params?)` does `{{param}}` interpolation and CLDR two-form (`one`/`other`) pluralization auto-selected when a numeric `count` param is passed; a key missing from the active locale falls back to English, missing from every locale renders the raw key + `console.warn` in dev (never blank). New `locales/en.json`/`locales/it.json` — flat dotted keys (`media.filters.allTypes`, not nested objects), identical key sets enforced programmatically. Persistence: `localStorage['dam:locale']` (`'en'`\|`'it'`, default `'en'`), applied pre-paint by extending (not duplicating) the existing Stage 75 blocking `<head>` script in `app/layout.tsx` — sets `<html lang>` and `data-locale`; the script never hardcodes locale codes, so adding a language needs no edit there. No-flash relies on the same "render nothing until the page's own auth effect fires" gate every authed page already has (`AppShell`, `admin/media`) — locale state settles in the same pre-paint effect batch as `authorized`, exactly like the pre-existing sidebar-collapse preference. Language selector added to `AppShell`'s account menu, between Profile and the dark-mode toggle — current language shown in its own name ("Italiano," not "Italian"), switching applies instantly via context (no reload, nothing to refetch). Converted `app/admin/media/page.tsx` (every visible string: filters, upload/import, bulk actions, kebabs, pagination, confirms, errors) plus `AppShell`'s nav labels and account menu, as the proof-of-pattern reference page. Extensibility verified, not just claimed: a throwaway `locales/fr.json` + one `LOCALES` array entry were added, `tsc --noEmit` run clean with zero edits to `layout.tsx`/`AppShell.tsx`/the media page, then both reverted. 116 keys after this stage (14 `common`, 11 `nav`, 7 `account`, 84 `media`). Scope: UI strings only — API error text, emails, and user data (names, filenames, tenant/collection names) are never translated. | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean; en/it key sets verified identical programmatically; not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint |

Namespace counts: `common` 44, `nav` 11, `account` 7, `media` 83, `users` 34, `tenants` 31, `permissions` 20, `metadataFields` 43, `shares` 46, `analytics` 32, `activity` 57. `common.*` extended aggressively rather than duplicated per page — reused directly: `notAuthorized`/`backToHome`/`loading`/`cancel`/`deleting`/`deletePermanently`/`download`/`editMetadata`/`listSeparator`; added then found reused across several pages: `you`/`none`/`always`/`saved`/`save`/`saving`/`company`/`role`/`name`/`email`/`status`/`expires`/`pending`/`expired`/`error.couldNotReachServer`/`error.generic`/`role.editor`/`role.viewer`/`role.admin`/`delete`. Three promotions mid-pass (renamed, not duplicated, once a second page needed identical text): `media.list.headerType`→`common.type`, `users.error.loadUsersFailed`→`common.error.loadFailed`, and the whole `users.invite.*` group (7 keys) → `common.invite.*`. Interpolation, never concatenation: a small local `interpolateJSX()` helper (`app/admin/activity/page.tsx`) splits a *localized* template on its `{{token}}` placeholders and substitutes bold JSX nodes at whatever position the translator put them — keeps actor/resource/target names bold in the activity feed while still translating as one whole sentence per action (e.g. `activity.line.shareCreate`: `"{{actor}} shared {{target}} as {{accessLevel}}"`), never built by gluing separately-translated fragments. Two-independent-count sentences (tenant-delete's users/collections/files stat line, activity/analytics summaries) use CLDR `.one`/`.other` per count, joined via `common.listSeparator` — a single key can't pluralize two numbers at once. `common.download` (an imperative button verb, "Scarica" in Italian) was deliberately *not* reused for `share_create`'s access-level noun slot since the grammar doesn't match across that slot — a dedicated `activity.line.accessLevelDownload` was added instead; this is the general rule for `common.*` reuse (same English word, different grammatical role → separate key, never forced). One structural change, narrowly scoped and only because independent pluralization required it: the tenant-delete confirmation's one sentence bolding two counts ("**3** collections and **12** files…") was split into two list bullets, one count per line — not expressible as a single CLDR key. Deliberately untranslated, same as Stage 116: user data, raw role-enum values shown as-is (`ROLES.name`, `inv.role.name`, `share.accessLevel`), and the Coperon brand name (still appears inside otherwise-translated sentences, e.g. metadata-fields' global-field hints). | ✓ tested — `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean on all seven pages; en/it key sets verified identical (408/408) programmatically; grep-confirmed no leftover hardcoded English strings (JSX text nodes, `placeholder`/`aria-label`/`title` attributes) on any of the seven files; not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint — the visual pass is pending, especially the permissions matrix header (`Visualizzatore` is markedly longer than `Viewer`) and the activity feed's bold-name wrapping at narrow widths |

New namespaces: `home.*`, `collections.*`, `resourceMetadata.*`. Heavy `media.*` reuse (~35 keys) — pagination, select-mode, upload/import flow, and kebab thumbnail actions on the collection-detail Files section are the same UI as the media library, so they read the media page's own keys directly rather than duplicating them under a new namespace; `shares.*`/`nav.collections` reused for the per-file Share modal and page titles. Pluralization (CLDR `.one`/`.other`) covers file/collection counts, pagination, and the delete-with-descendants confirm — the **0-subcollections case is a separate, non-pluralized key** (`collections.deleteCollection.confirmNoChildren`), not folded into the CLDR form, since deleting a childless collection is a genuinely different sentence, not just the "other" plural of "0 sub-collections." `components/ViewToggle.tsx`'s two `aria-label`s (Grid view/List view) were left hardcoded English on purpose — the same gap already existed, unaddressed, on the already-shipped media page, so fixing it here first would have been inconsistent; deferred to Stage B group 4 (see "Not yet built"). New namespaces: `invite.*`, `confirmEmail.*`, `profile.*`, `share.*`. 581 keys total after this stage's own additions (80 new), before the login revert below. Password-rule checklist (invite redemption + profile change-password, both restating the same server-side rule set) restructured so each rule carries a `labelKey` + optional `labelParams`, resolved via `t()` at render time — `common.passwordRule.length` interpolates `{{n}}` rather than baking "8" into the string. ## lib/ modules built so far

| File | Exports |
|---|---|
| `lib/errors.ts` | `AppError(status, message)` |
| `lib/db.ts` | default `db` (pg Pool singleton, survives hot reload via `global._pgPool`). **2026-08-31:** pool size and both timeouts are now configuration, not constants — `PG_POOL_MAX` (default 10, floor 1), `PG_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `PG_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS`, all read through a local `envInt()` that honours an explicit `0`. Defaults reproduce the previous behaviour except that waiting for a connection is now bounded rather than infinite. Set `PG_POOL_MAX=1` on serverless — and read the deadlock note in "Conventions that bite" before doing so. |
| `lib/session.ts` | `requireAuth`, `requireAdmin` (role 1 or 2 — `canAdmin`, which both tiers carry as of Stage 89), `requireSuperAdmin` (role 1 only, Stage 89), `isSuperAdmin(payload)` (`roleId === 1`), `requireUpload`, `requireDownload`. **Stage 106:** `isInternal(payload)` (renamed `canAccessAllTenants(payload)` at Stage 110 — see that stage; row wording below quotes the Stage 106 build, current name is `canAccessAllTenants`) — role 1 hardcoded-true, else the token's `canAccessAllTenants` claim (was `isInternal`); the bypass primitive every tenant-scoped target-access check now calls instead of `isSuperAdmin` — and `actingTenantId(payload, request)` — resolves the tenant a cross-tenant-access user is acting as from the `dam_acting_tenant` cookie (honored only for cross-tenant-access callers, silently ignored otherwise; falls back to the caller's own tenantId), used by every tenant-scoped *list* route. **Stage 109:** `canInvite(payload)` — role 1 hardcoded-true, else the token's `canInvite` claim; same shape as `canAccessAllTenants`, used by `POST /api/invitations` in place of the old blanket "cross-tenant admins can't invite" block. **Stage 110:** `isInternal` → `canAccessAllTenants`, pure rename, zero behavior change — see the Stage 110 terminology note near the JWT contract below |
| `lib/users.ts` | `validatePasswordStrength`, `findByEmail`, `updatePassword` — **`createUser` deleted in Stage 105** along with `POST /api/auth/register` (its only caller); the only user-creating code path left anywhere is `POST /api/invitations/redeem`'s own INSERT. **Stage 109:** `findByEmail` also selects `u.can_invite`, feeding the JWT mint (see `lib/auth.ts`) |
| `lib/auth.ts` | `login`, `changePassword`, `forgotPassword`, `resetPassword`; **Stage 112:** `mintSessionToken(user)` extracted from `login()`'s payload-building/`jwt.sign` call — the single JWT-minting path, same `findByEmail`-shaped input, reused by `POST /api/invitations/redeem` so a redeemed user's session can never drift from a normal login's claims |
| `lib/mail.ts` | `sendResetEmail` — Gmail/nodemailer, SMTP credentials in `.env.local`, **confirmed delivering**; `sendInvitationEmail(to, inviteLink, roleName, tenantName)` added Stage 90, same transporter, also confirmed delivering (param renamed from `companyName` at Stage 104, the company→tenant terminology rename — see that stage); `sendEmailChangeConfirmation(to, confirmLink, currentEmail)` added Stage 107, same transporter, sent to the NEW address only, confirmed delivering (200, no SMTP error) |
| `lib/emailChange.ts` (Stage 107) | `requestEmailChange(userId, currentEmail, newEmail)` — validates format/length/not-already-mine, 409s on an existing owner, deletes the caller's own prior pending row (one pending change at a time), inserts a 24h-expiring `email_tokens` row (`purpose='email_change'`, sha256-hashed like `lib/auth.ts`'s reset flow — not bcrypt, no id+token composite needed), emails the confirmation link to the new address. `getPendingEmailChange(userId)`, `cancelEmailChange(userId)` — read/delete for the Profile page's pending-state banner and Cancel button. `validateEmailChangeToken(rawToken)` — read-only load-time check (never marks used), separate from `confirmEmailChange(rawToken)` — the atomic single-use swap (`UPDATE email_tokens ... WHERE used_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now() RETURNING ...` then `UPDATE users SET email`, one transaction, same accept-then-act shape as `POST /api/invitations/redeem`), which also re-checks for an address conflict at confirm time (its own distinct 409, not the generic invalid-link message). See Stage 107. |
| `lib/invitations.ts` | `validateInvitationToken(param)` — fail-closed `{id}.{rawToken}` gate (shares-shaped lookup, bcrypt-compare), collapses invalid/bad-token/expired/already-accepted into one generic `'invalid'`; `INVITABLE_ROLES` (2/3/4), `TENANT_ADMIN_INVITABLE_ROLES` (3/4). See Stage 90. **`isTenantStub` removed in Stage 105** — the stub concept is gone; tenant details are entered at creation (`POST /api/tenants`), never at redemption. **`ROLE_TO_LEGACY_GROUP_ID` removed in Stage 94** — it existed only to satisfy `users.group_id`'s FK while the column still existed; both are gone now. (`isCompanyStub`/`COMPANY_ADMIN_INVITABLE_ROLES` renamed at Stage 104 — see that stage.) |
| `lib/countries.ts` (Stage 93) | Static, dependency-free country/dial-code data for `components/PhoneInput.tsx`. `COUNTRIES: Country[]` — 230 entries (`{code, name, dial, flag}`), alphabetical by name; `flag` computed from `code` via a regional-indicator-emoji helper, not hand-typed. `DEFAULT_COUNTRY_CODE = "LB"`. `findCountry(code)`. **`IL` (Israel) removed, Stage 111** — business/legal requirement for operating in Lebanon; the only reference to it anywhere in the app. |
| `lib/storage.ts` | `getUploadUrl(key, contentType)`, `getDownloadUrl(key)`, `deleteObject(storageKey)` — presigned 300 s, MinIO `forcePathStyle: true`; `putObject(key, body, contentType)` — direct (non-presigned) server-side write, used by `upload/from-url`; `getObject(key)` — direct (non-presigned) server-side read, returns object bytes as a `Buffer`, used by the video-thumbnail generator to pull a video locally for FFmpeg |
| `lib/shares.ts` | `validateShareToken(param)` — the fail-closed gate for every public share endpoint: parses `{id}.{rawToken}`, `bcrypt.compare`s against `token_hash`, checks `revoked`/`expires_at`; returns `{status: 'valid'\|'invalid'\|'expired'\|'revoked'}` (not-found and bad-token both collapse to `'invalid'`, no oracle); `touchShareLastUsed(shareId)` — writes `last_used_at = now()`; `isResourceInShareScope(share, resourceId)` — independent anti-scope-escape check (exact-id for a resource share, downward recursive CTE through `parent_id` for a collection share's subtree; never joins `group_collection_access`). See Stage 47. **Stage 98 added `resolveShareOwningTenant(share)`** (renamed from `resolveShareOwningCompany` at Stage 104) — best-effort tenant attribution for anonymous share access, resolving to a tenant only when exactly one tenant's `tenant_collection_access` grant reaches the shared target at any depth (Stage 88 cascade), `null` otherwise (zero or multiple — never guessed); and `logShareAccess(share, action, resourceId)` — a fire-and-forget wrapper around resolution + `lib/accessLog.ts`'s `logAccess`, so neither the resolution query's own failure nor the insert's can reach or delay a public share request. See Stage 98, Stage 104. |
| `lib/fileTypes.ts` | `shortTypeLabel(mimeType, filename?)` — branch-selected/whitelisted MIME→short-label map (images, video/audio families, PDF, Office types, archives, text/JSON) with a filename-extension fallback for anything unrecognized, every return capped at 5 characters. Replaces `mimeLabel()` (deleted) at every call site — no raw MIME subtype is ever rendered in the UI. See Stage 53. |
| `lib/crypto.ts` | `encrypt(plaintext)` / `decrypt(packed)` — AES-256-GCM via Node's built-in `crypto` (no dependency); key from `SHARE_TOKEN_KEY` env var (base64, validated to decode to exactly 32 bytes, throws a clear error if missing/wrong length); packs `iv:authTag:ciphertext` (all base64) into one string; GCM's auth-tag check makes `decrypt` throw — never return a silently-wrong value — on a tampered/corrupted input or the wrong key. Used only for share-token at-rest encryption (admin link retrieval); the public share route's validation stays entirely on the separate `token_hash` bcrypt path in `lib/shares.ts` and never calls this file. See Stage 72. |
| `lib/permissions.ts` | `canEditResourceMetadata(user, resourceId)` — super admin (`isSuperAdmin`, Stage 89 — was `canAdmin`, see below) → true unconditionally; editor-or-better (`canUpload`, which a tenant admin also carries) → true only if the resource's collection membership reaches, at any depth (Stage 88), a `tenant_collection_access` grant for the user's tenant (Stage 86 — was `group_collection_access`/group); everyone else → false. Internally calls a private `tenantHasResourceAccess(tenantId, resourceId)` (renamed from `groupHasResourceAccess` in Stage 86, then from `companyHasResourceAccess` at Stage 104) — the exact `root_finder` cascade already used by `GET /api/download/[id]` and `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata`'s non-admin read check, factored out rather than duplicated. Also exports `tenantHasCollectionAccess` (same Stage-104 rename) and `TENANT_ADMIN_CAN_EDIT_ACCESS` (renamed from `COMPANY_ADMIN_CAN_EDIT_ACCESS`, see Stage 104). **Stage 89:** the top bypass switched from `user.canAdmin` to `isSuperAdmin(user)` — once a tenant admin also carried `canAdmin: true`, the old bypass would have let them edit/read any tenant's resource metadata unconditionally; a tenant admin now falls through to the same tenant-scoped check an editor gets. Governs write access to per-asset metadata only; does not affect the broader read access every authenticated user with resource access already has via `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata`. **Stage 106:** the top bypass switched again, `isSuperAdmin` → `isInternal` — an internal admin (role 2 + `is_internal`) now edits any tenant's resource metadata unconditionally, same as a super admin. **Stage 108** added the whole per-tenant permission system to this file: `EDITOR_PERMISSION_KEYS`/`VIEWER_PERMISSION_KEYS`/`ALL_PERMISSION_KEYS` (`PermissionKey` union type), `EDITOR_DEFAULTS`/`VIEWER_DEFAULTS` (exported, read by `app/api/permissions/route.ts` to build the admin matrix), `hasPermission(user, key)` (the resolver — internal/super-admin/admin always true; `download`/`view_metadata` always true for editors too, viewer-configurable only; role 3/4 otherwise DB-backed via a per-request-memoized `WeakMap`-cached override lookup, falling back to the code default; everyone else false), `requirePermission(request, key)` (a `requireAdmin`/`requireUpload`-shaped route guard), and `getEffectivePermissions(user)` (all 12 keys at once, the frontend's "my permissions" bundle, `GET /api/permissions/mine`). `canEditResourceMetadata` itself gained one more check — `hasPermission(user, 'edit_metadata')` — ahead of its existing `tenantHasResourceAccess` call, a no-op for role 2 since `hasPermission` already short-circuits true there. See Stage 78, Stage 86, Stage 89, Stage 104, Stage 106, Stage 108. |
| `lib/exifSources.ts` | Pure data, no server deps (safe for "use client" import): `EXIF_SOURCES` (the fixed 23-source whitelist), `isValidExifSource`, `exifSourceAppliesTo`. See Stage 81. |
| `lib/exif.ts` | `extractMetadata(buffer, mimeType)` — parses embedded EXIF/IPTC/XMP via `exifr`, never throws; `coerceExifValue(raw, fieldType, options)` — per-field-type coercion incl. the local-getter EXIF-date fix; `applyExtractedMetadata(resourceId, buffer, mimeType, uploaderTenantId)` — writes mapped values into `resource_field_data`, never clobbering an existing value; **`uploaderTenantId` param added Stage 103** (renamed from `uploaderCompanyId` at Stage 104) — only applies `exif_source` mappings from global fields or the uploader's own tenant, never another tenant's (verified against real rows with two tenants mapped to the identical `exif_source`). Re-exports everything from `lib/exifSources.ts` too. See Stage 81, Stage 103, Stage 104. |
| `lib/i18n.tsx` (Stage 116) | `TranslationProvider` (React context, mounted in `app/layout.tsx` wrapping `children`), `useTranslation()` → `{locale, setLocale, t}`, `LOCALES` (the array of `{code, name, dict}` — the one place to touch when adding a language), `LOCALE_KEY` (`"dam:locale"`), `Locale`/`Dictionary` types. `t(key, params?)` interpolates `{{param}}` tokens and auto-pluralizes (CLDR `.one`/`.other`) when `params.count` is a number; falls back active-locale-miss → English → raw key + dev `console.warn`. **Stage 119:** also exports `interpolateJSX(template, params)` — promoted from a local helper in `app/admin/activity/page.tsx` (Stage 117) so any page needing to interpolate JSX (not just plain-string params) into a translated sentence can share one implementation; the activity page keeps its own pre-existing local copy, unchanged. See Stage 116, Stage 119. |
| `locales/en.json`, `locales/it.json` (Stage 116) | Flat dotted-key translation dictionaries (e.g. `"media.filters.allTypes"`), not nested objects — one JSON object per language, statically imported by `lib/i18n.tsx`. **572 keys as of Stage 119**, identical key sets enforced programmatically on every merge (408 at Stage 117 → 501 at Stage 118 → 581 mid–Stage 119 → 572 net, after the Stage 119 login revert removed the 9 `login.*` keys it had briefly added). Namespace convention: `namespace.subpath[.pluralSuffix]` — `common.*` (cross-page words), `nav.*`/`account.*` (the shell), then one namespace per page (`media.*`, `users.*`, `tenants.*`, `permissions.*`, `metadataFields.*`, `shares.*`, `analytics.*`, `activity.*`, and, as of Stages 118–119, `home.*`, `collections.*`, `resourceMetadata.*`, `invite.*`, `confirmEmail.*`, `profile.*`, `share.*`). `app/page.tsx` (login) deliberately has no namespace — it's the one page that stays hardcoded English, see its own row above. Adding a language: one new `locales/<code>.json` with the same key set + one entry in `lib/i18n.tsx`'s `LOCALES` array — no component edits (verified live at Stage 116 with a throwaway `fr.json`, then reverted). See Stage 116, Stage 117, Stage 118, Stage 119. |
| `lib/accessLog.ts` (Stage 96) | `logAccess({ userId, tenantId, resourceId?, action, metadata? })` — fire-and-forget insert into `access_log`; deliberately not `async`, fires the `INSERT` and attaches its own `.catch()` (swallows + `console.error`s, never rethrows) so a caller can't accidentally block on it even if it forgot not to await. Callers must call this only after their own access check already passed. `action` is `'view' \| 'download' \| 'share_create' \| 'share_access' \| 'upload' \| 'login' \| 'all_tenants_flag_change' \| 'invite_flag_change' \| 'user_delete' \| 'tenant_delete'` (the middle four added Stage 98; `all_tenants_flag_change` — named `internal_flag_change` until Stage 110, see that stage — added Stage 106, written by `PATCH /api/users/[id]` when a super admin sets/unsets `can_access_all_tenants`, always `tenant_id: null` so tenant-scoped feeds never surface it; `invite_flag_change` added Stage 109, identical shape, for `users.can_invite`; `user_delete` written by `DELETE /api/users/[id]`, target's email/name/role/tenant captured in metadata since the row is gone by the time this fires; `tenant_delete` added Stage 113 by `DELETE /api/tenants/[id]`, same "describe the gone target from metadata" shape, `tenant_id: null` since only a super admin — who has no tenant — ever reaches that route). `resourceId` is optional (`null` for a login or a collection-target share, Stage 98); `metadata` is an optional free-form object, `JSON.stringify`'d before the insert, never a password/token/raw share token (Stage 98). Read side (`GET /api/analytics`) lives in its own route file, not this module — see Stage 97. `tenantId` param renamed from `companyId` at Stage 104. See Stage 96, Stage 98, Stage 104, Stage 109, Stage 110, Stage 113. |

## Scripts

Standalone Node scripts run manually from `frontend/` — not API routes, no boot hook, not part of the Next.js build or route tree.

| File | Purpose | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/check-env.mjs` | **New 2026-08-31 — environment preflight, the thing to run after pasting a new connection string.** Verifies that whatever `DATABASE_URL` and `S3_*` point at is actually usable: database connects, `pg_trgm`/`pgcrypto` present, tables exist and are not an empty database, row counts against the 136/9/32 baseline, `resources.search_vector` really is a generated column, and the state of the `schema_migrations` ledger. For storage: bucket reachable, credentials accepted, object count and size, plus an optional real put/get/delete round-trip (`--write`) and a **real CORS preflight** (`--origin=https://…`) — the one check that catches R2 denying the browser's cross-origin PUT/GET, which nothing server-side reveals. Knows the two providers: warns on a Neon *direct* endpoint where the app wants `-pooler`, on `sslmode=require` instead of `verify-full`, and on `PG_POOL_MAX` not being 1; fails on an R2 endpoint whose `S3_REGION` is not `auto`. Read-only unless `--write`. Never prints a credential. Point it anywhere for one command: `DATABASE_URL="…" node scripts/check-env.mjs --db`. | Working — run against the live dev environment 2026-08-31, all green except the ledger warning below |
| `scripts/backfill-covers.mjs` | One-time backfill for `collections.cover_storage_key`. **Widened in Stage 64** — for every collection with no cover, ranks candidates via a single query: a recursive `subtree` CTE (depth-tracked, `$1`-bound) feeding a 4-way `UNION ALL` (priority 0 = own `image/*`, 1 = own resource with `thumbnail_storage_key`, 2 = descendant `image/*`, 3 = descendant thumbnail), `ORDER BY priority ASC, depth ASC, created_at ASC LIMIT 1` — own files still win, then the nearest descendant rather than an arbitrarily deep leaf. Originally (Stage 59) this only ever searched a collection's own member resources; a container collection (holding only sub-collections, no direct files) could never get a cover under that rule, no matter how many images lived underneath it. Still applies the same `AND cover_storage_key IS NULL` conditional UPDATE the live write-sites use, so re-running is safe — a collection already covered (by a live upload or a prior run of this script) is never touched. Prints a summary distinguishing covered-from-own-files / covered-from-a-descendant / no-candidate. Run manually: `node scripts/backfill-covers.mjs`. | ✓ run once against the dev DB (Stage 59) — 25 of 32 collections were coverless; 16 got a cover set from their own files; 9 had no eligible member (all pure containers) and were left alone; an immediate re-run touched 0, confirming idempotency. **Re-run after the Stage 64 widening: those same 9 went to 0 coverless, all 9 covered from a descendant (0 from own files)** — matching a diagnostic query run beforehand that confirmed every remaining coverless collection had zero direct files; a further immediate re-run again touched 0, confirming idempotency still holds |
| `scripts/regenerate-thumbnails.mjs` (Stage 65) | Bulk force-regeneration for stale video/PDF thumbnails — drives the real `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail?force=true` endpoint over HTTP rather than duplicating any ffmpeg/pdfjs logic. Enumerates video/PDF resources that already have a `thumbnail_storage_key` (optional `--type=pdf`/`--type=video` filter), calls the endpoint sequentially with an admin JWT supplied via the `ADMIN_TOKEN` env var (never written to disk), tolerates per-resource failures (`regenerated`/`skipped` on 400/404/`failed` on other errors) without aborting the batch, prints a running log and a final summary. Run manually: `ADMIN_TOKEN=... node scripts/regenerate-thumbnails.mjs [--type=pdf\|video]`. | ✓ run against the dev DB with `--type=pdf` — 17 PDF resources processed, 0 failed/skipped; independently confirmed to fix a seeded stale (320×452) thumbnail back to a real 800×1132 render |
| `scripts/migrate-to-companies.mjs` (Stage 84) | Multi-tenancy Stage 1 backfill — populates `companies`/`roles`/`company_collection_access`/`users.company_id`/`users.role_id` from the existing `group_id`/`group_collection_access` data; nothing in the app reads any of it yet. Finds-or-creates a single "Test Company"; maps `role_id` from `group_id` (1→1 super_admin, 2→3 editor, 3→4 viewer, 4→5 pending — role 2 `admin` is never assigned here); sets `company_id` to `NULL` for super admins, Test Company's id for everyone else; copies `group_collection_access` into `company_collection_access` (deduped `SELECT DISTINCT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). Every write is guarded (`role_id`/`company_id` only set where still `NULL`; the grants insert is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). Run manually: `node scripts/migrate-to-companies.mjs`. | ✓ run against the real dev DB — 2 super_admin / 0 admin / 3 editor / 1 viewer / 1 pending (7 users total); `company_collection_access` matched `group_collection_access`'s distinct-collection set exactly (4 collections); a second run confirmed idempotent (all "newly set/inserted" counters 0, identical totals) |
| `scripts/migrate.mjs` (2026-08-18) | **Schema migration runner.** Applies every unapplied `.sql` in `db/migrations/` in filename order, each in its own transaction, recording filename + sha256 in a `schema_migrations` table. `--status` lists applied vs pending without applying. Refuses to run if an already-applied file changed on disk, which is how silent schema drift gets caught. | Written and syntax-checked; **not yet executed against a database** (Postgres was down). |
