## Route mapping (NestJS → Next.js)

**Terminology note (Stage 104):** every `companyId`/`company_id`/`company`-shaped identifier below (query params, JSON field/response-key names, JWT claim, DB columns/tables) was renamed to `tenantId`/`tenant_id`/`tenant` in a pure mechanical rename — same access, same behavior, only names changed. Individual rows below still say "company" wherever they're quoting what a given Stage actually built at the time (not rewritten, per this project's convention of not editing historical stage entries) — read current identifier names from the code or from Stage 104's own entry, not from a row's historical wording. User-facing UI copy still says "Company" throughout (nav item, table headers, the tenant-management page's copy) — this rename never touched displayed text, only internal names. The one row whose path/file actually moved (`/api/companies` → `/api/tenants`) is updated below in place, since that's a current fact, not history.

| NestJS | Next.js file | Done? |
|---|---|---|
| ~~`POST /register`~~ | ~~`app/api/auth/register/route.ts`~~ | **Deleted in Stage 105** — self-registration is fully gone (the route now 404s; `app/signup/page.tsx`'s redirect stub deleted too, so `/signup` also 404s). The only way a user is created is `POST /api/invitations/redeem`. See Stage 105 |
| `POST /login` | `app/api/auth/login/route.ts` | ✓ tested — as of Stage 98, `lib/auth.ts::login()` fires `logAccess({ action: 'login' })` right after the JWT is signed (a real, successful login only — both pre-existing bad-email/bad-password `AppError` throws happen earlier and are never reached), `metadata: {roleName}`, never a password or the issued token |
| `POST /change-password` | `app/api/auth/change-password/route.ts` | ✓ tested |
| `POST /forgot-password` | `app/api/auth/forgot-password/route.ts` | ✓ tested |
| `POST /reset-password` | `app/api/auth/reset-password/route.ts` | ✓ tested |
| `POST /upload-url` | `app/api/upload/url/route.ts` | ✓ tested |
| `POST /upload-complete` | `app/api/upload/complete/route.ts` | ✓ tested — as of Stage 59, also auto-sets a coverless collection's `cover_storage_key` from the first `image/*` uploaded into it (conditional `... AND cover_storage_key IS NULL` UPDATE, never overwrites an existing cover); as of Stage 64, additionally walks the collection's ancestors via a recursive CTE and applies the same guarded UPDATE to every still-coverless one in a single set-based query (`WHERE id IN (...) AND cover_storage_key IS NULL`), so an image uploaded deep in a subtree can also cover any coverless collection above it — each ancestor is guarded independently, so the walk doesn't stop at the first one that already has a cover; as of Stage 98, every success branch also fires a fire-and-forget `logAccess({ action: 'upload' })` right after `COMMIT`, `metadata: {filename, collectionId}` |
| `GET /download/:id` | `app/api/download/[id]/route.ts` | ✓ tested — now enforces collection-membership isolation; as of Stage 96, a successful (authorized) serve fires a fire-and-forget `logAccess({ action: 'download' })` (never on a 403) |
| `GET /collections/:id/files` | `app/api/collections/[id]/files/route.ts` | ✓ tested — paginated as of Stage 63 (`page`/`pageSize`, whitelisted `pageSize`, server-clamped `page`), returning `{ files, total, page, pageSize }` — `total` computed from the identical `FROM`/`JOIN`/`WHERE` as the page query; non-admin cascade access CTE unchanged |
| `DELETE /collections/:id/files/:fileId` | `app/api/collections/[id]/files/[fileId]/route.ts` | ✓ tested — removes collection_resource link only |
| `DELETE /resources/:id` | `app/api/resources/[id]/route.ts` | ✓ tested — permanent delete: DB row + MinIO bytes; as of Stage 59, also clears `cover_storage_key` on any collection pointing at the deleted resource's `storage_key` or `thumbnail_storage_key`, before the row/object are removed, so the next image upload re-picks |
| `PATCH /resources/:id` | `app/api/resources/[id]/route.ts` | ✓ tested — accepts `{ description }` and/or `{ originalFilename }` (dynamic SET, `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 — was `requireAdmin`; no company scoping exists on this route, so it stayed super-admin-only rather than being widened) |
| `PATCH /collections/:id` | `app/api/collections/[id]/route.ts` | ✓ tested — accepts `{ name }` and/or `{ coverStorageKey }` (dynamic SET, `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 — was `requireAdmin`) |
| `DELETE /collections/:id` | `app/api/collections/[id]/route.ts` | ✓ tested — deletes collection + sub-tree via cascade; files kept; `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`) |
| `GET /collections/:id/descendant-count` | `app/api/collections/[id]/descendant-count/route.ts` | ✓ tested — `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`) |
| `GET /api/cover?key=` | `app/api/cover/route.ts` | ✓ built — requireAuth; returns presigned URL for a storage key |
| `GET /api/users` | `app/api/users/route.ts` | ✓ tested — `requireAdmin` (role 1 or 2, Stage 89); `LEFT JOIN roles` for `role_id`/`role_name`, `LEFT JOIN companies` for `company_id`/`company_name` (Stage 87); includes `name` as of Stage 49; optional `roleId` filter (validated against `roles`, 400 on unknown) — **Stage 94: replaced the old `groupId` filter** (was validated against `user_groups`, now dropped). Company-scoped as of Stage 89: a super admin gets every user; a company admin gets an additional `AND u.company_id = $companyId` from their own token (never a request param), or `[]` outright if their token has no `companyId`. **Stage 109:** response also includes `can_invite`, feeding the users page's new "Can invite" column |
| ~~`GET /api/groups`~~ | ~~`app/api/groups/route.ts`~~ | **Removed in Stage 94** — the legacy group model (`user_groups`, `group_collection_access`, `users.group_id`) is fully retired; nothing reads it anymore. See Stage 94 in the changelog |
| `GET`/`POST /api/tenants` (Stage 87, no NestJS equivalent; moved from `/api/companies` and renamed at Stage 104) | `app/api/tenants/route.ts` (moved from `app/api/companies/route.ts` at Stage 104) | ✓ tested — both `requireSuperAdmin`. GET: `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAuth`, open to every logged-in user); absent `?detail=true` returns the original bare `SELECT id, name FROM tenants ORDER BY name` array unchanged (Stage 92 confirmed live still exactly what the admin users page's Company dropdown and the home page's "New collection" grant checkboxes expect); `?detail=true` (Stage 92, new) returns `{tenants: [{id, name, userCount, hasAdmin}]}` (key renamed from `companies` at Stage 104; `isStub` dropped from this shape at Stage 105, see below) — `userCount`/`hasAdmin` via correlated subqueries on `users` — for `app/admin/tenants/page.tsx` (moved from `app/admin/companies/page.tsx` at Stage 104) only. **POST (Stage 92; rewritten Stage 105):** collects **all** tenant details in one step — `name`, `address`, and `phone` all required (400 each if blank; caps 200/255/30), 409 on a case-insensitive duplicate name (no DB constraint backs this, checked here). The old name-only-stub flow (address/phone filled at first-admin redemption) is gone — `isStub` no longer appears in either GET shape. See Stage 105 |
| — (new, no NestJS equivalent) | `app/api/tenants/[id]/route.ts` (Stage 113, new) | ✓ tested — both `requireSuperAdmin`. **GET:** read-only deletion preview — 404 if missing, else `{id, name, userCount, collectionsToDelete, collectionsSkippedShared, resourcesToDelete}`, the collection/resource counts from the exact same two queries DELETE runs (below), so the confirmation modal's numbers can never drift from what the real delete does. **DELETE:** permanent tenant deletion, one transaction. Requires `confirmName` in the body to match the tenant's current name exactly (case-sensitive, trimmed) — checked server-side, 400 on mismatch, nothing touched. Refuses (409) if any `role_id = 1` user has this `tenant_id` (shouldn't happen — super admins have no tenant — but fails loudly rather than ever deleting one). Deletes: every user with this `tenant_id` (must precede the tenant row — `users.tenant_id` has no `ON DELETE` action of its own, deliberately, so a dangling reference is impossible); the tenant's **exclusive** collections (see below) and any resource whose *every* collection membership is inside that exclusive set (a resource in an exclusive collection AND a surviving one keeps its row, just loses the one link, via `collections`' own cascade); then the tenant row itself, which cascades `invitations`, this tenant's remaining `tenant_collection_access` rows (on shared/other-tenant collections, its grant simply vanishes), `metadata_fields` (+ their `resource_field_data`), and `tenant_role_permissions`. `access_log.tenant_id`/`.user_id` stay `ON DELETE SET NULL` — never touched, history survives with null actors. **Exclusivity ("the tenant's collections"), computed once and reused by both GET and DELETE:** a single recursive CTE walks every collection up to its root (same shape as `lib/permissions.ts::tenantHasCollectionAccess`, run for the whole table at once) and groups the tenant ids found anywhere in each chain — a chain containing only the target tenant is exclusive; one containing it plus at least one other tenant is shared and must survive untouched, only the target tenant's own grant disappearing (via the tenant-row cascade above). A resource is exclusive only if every `collection_resource` row it has points into the exclusive set — one surviving membership keeps it. **Cascade-safety detach step:** `collections.parent_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, which would otherwise blindly delete a shared collection nested under an exclusive one when the exclusive ancestor is deleted; before deleting, any shared collection whose direct parent is in the exclusive set is `UPDATE`d to `parent_id = NULL` (becomes a new root) — proven sufficient in one pass, never recursive, because every collection beneath a shared collection is provably shared too (the same inherited grant that makes the parent shared covers it). Surviving collections' `cover_storage_key` is nulled first if it points at a file about to be deleted (same hygiene as `DELETE /api/resources/[id]`). **MinIO cleanup runs only after the transaction commits** — each deleted resource's `storage_key` and (if set) `thumbnail_storage_key` are deleted from MinIO in a loop; a failed object delete is `console.error`'d and skipped, never rolled back into the DB state. Logs `tenant_delete` to `access_log` (`tenantId: null` — actor's own, always null since only a super admin reaches this route; `metadata: {targetTenantId, targetTenantName, usersDeleted, collectionsDeleted, collectionsSkippedShared, resourcesDeleted, invitationsDeleted, metadataFieldsDeleted, permissionOverridesDeleted}`, same "describe the gone target from metadata" convention as `user_delete`). Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server/DB/MinIO with two real tenants and real accounts (no Playwright) — full detail in Stage 113's own changelog entry. |
| `PATCH /api/users/:id` | `app/api/users/[id]/route.ts` | ✓ tested — `requireAdmin` (role 1 or 2, Stage 89); self-edit block; dynamic-SET for `name` as of Stage 49 (`groupId` support removed in Stage 94 — see below); **`companyId` added Stage 87** — `null` is valid (sets `company_id = NULL`, no company), a non-null value is uuid-validated and checked against the real `companies` table (unknown id → 400). **Stage 89 rewrite:** same-company check runs before the body is parsed — a company admin may act only on a user whose `company_id` matches their own token, *and* whose current `role_id` is not 1 or 2 (a company admin can never edit an admin-tier user, on any field, not just role — 403 either way, same generic message so the reason isn't distinguishable); `companyId` in the body is now super-admin-only to set at all (403 for a company admin regardless of value, even their own); new `roleId` field (validated against `roles`) — a company admin may only set it to 3 (editor) or 4 (viewer), 1/2 → 403; a super admin is unrestricted by all of the above (the "super admin owns the admin slot" escape hatch). **Stage 109:** new `canInvite` field, same self-escalation-guard shape as `canAccessAllTenants` (Stage 106, named `isInternal` until Stage 110) — only a super admin may set it (403 otherwise, checked before the value is validated); self-grant is already blocked by the pre-existing top-of-handler self-edit block, so no separate guard was needed for that case. A change fires a fire-and-forget `logAccess({ action: 'invite_flag_change' })`, mirroring `all_tenants_flag_change`. **Stage 110:** the body field this route reads for cross-tenant access is now `canAccessAllTenants` (was `isInternal`), same rename applied to the DB column (`can_access_all_tenants`, was `is_internal`) and the log action (`all_tenants_flag_change`, was `internal_flag_change`) — pure rename, see Stage 110 |
| `GET /api/search?q=` | `app/api/search/route.ts` | ✓ tested — requireAuth; scoped to user's access; file matching is now a three-branch predicate — ranked full-text on `search_vector`, `original_filename` ILIKE (trigram-indexed), and searchable-metadata-value ILIKE via `resource_field_data`/`metadata_fields` (was ILIKE-only on filename/description; see Stage 41). **Stage 103:** the metadata-value branch is additionally company-scoped — `mf.company_id IS NULL` only for a super admin's unscoped cross-company search, `mf.company_id IS NULL OR mf.company_id = $companyId` for everyone else — so a match against another company's field value can never surface, confirmed live with a real cross-company value that only its own company's search found |
| `GET /api/media` | `app/api/media/route.ts` | ✓ tested — `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`) — global cross-company media library, no company scoping exists on this route; all files incl. unassigned; type/q/sort/unassigned/`collectionId` filters (`collectionId` added Stage 62 — restricts to a collection + descendants via `EXISTS` + a recursive subtree CTE, fresh `cr2` alias, doesn't touch the existing `LEFT JOIN collection_resource cr`); paginated as of Stage 61 (`page`/`pageSize`, whitelisted `pageSize`, server-clamped `page`), returning `{ files, total, page, pageSize }` — `total` computed from the identical filtered WHERE/HAVING as the page query |
| `POST /api/resources/:id/collections` | `app/api/resources/[id]/collections/route.ts` | ✓ tested — `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`) — links any resource to any collection, no company scoping exists on this route; both ids UUID-format-checked (400) and existence-checked (404); idempotent via `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` |
| — (new, no NestJS equivalent) | `GET /api/collections/[id]` (`app/api/collections/[id]/route.ts`) | ✓ tested — requireAuth; returns `{ id, name }`; 404 if missing; non-admins access-checked via the same ancestor cascade CTE as files/download routes, a grant matching at any depth as of Stage 88 (403 if denied); feeds the collection page's breadcrumb and zip filename on mount (Stage 31) |
| — (new, no NestJS equivalent) | `GET /api/collections/[id]/ancestors` (`app/api/collections/[id]/ancestors/route.ts`) | ✓ tested — requireAuth; 404 if missing; recursive CTE returns the ancestor chain root-first, excluding the requested collection; reuses `GET /api/collections/[id]`'s exact root-ancestor cascade CTE (factored into a local `isAccessible()` helper) for both the leaf-access 403 gate and each ancestor's own `accessible` flag; inaccessible ancestors get `name: null`; feeds the collapsible breadcrumb trail on `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` (Stage 57) |
| — (new, no NestJS equivalent) | `GET`/`PUT /api/collections/[id]/access` (`app/api/collections/[id]/access/route.ts`) | ✓ tested — both `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`) — the Access modal is explicitly super-admin-only under the Stage 89 access model. Originally (Stage 79) root-only: grants lived only on a root collection, `PUT` 400'd on any other id, and the modal showed a read-only "inherited from `<root>`" view for sub-collections. **Stage 86** switched groups → companies (same root-only shape, `groups` → `companies`). **Stage 88 removed the root restriction entirely** — every collection, at any depth, reads/writes its own `company_collection_access` rows directly (`resolveRoot`/`isRoot` deleted, no redirect). `GET` returns `{ collectionId, companies: [{id, name, granted, inheritedFrom: {id, name} \| null}] }` — `inheritedFrom` is context only (the nearest strict ancestor, if any, that grants a company with no grant of its own here, found via a `DISTINCT ON (company_id) ... ORDER BY depth ASC` ancestor walk), never editable, always `null` when `granted` is true. `PUT` (`{ companyIds: string[] }`, uuid-validated against the real `companies` table) is an unchanged whole-set replace on this exact collection's own rows — `DELETE ... WHERE NOT company_id = ANY($ids)` then `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; removing every company is allowed and unguarded since admin access never depends on a grant row. Feeds the Access modal on `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`, now a single always-editable form at any depth (Stage 79 create; Stage 86 companies; Stage 88 any-depth + inherited-context display) |

## Frontend pages

| Page file | Fetch target | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `app/layout.tsx` | — (root layout, no fetch) | ✓ — wraps every page; owns fonts (`Geist`/`Geist Mono` variables) and `metadata` (tab title/description, Stage 60). **Dark-mode anti-flash (Stage 75):** a blocking (no `async`/`defer`) inline `<script>` in `<head>` reads `localStorage['dam:theme']` and sets `document.documentElement.dataset.theme = "dark"` synchronously, before `<body>` is parsed — confirmed the last node in the served `<head>`, ahead of `<body>`. This is what makes the `AppShell` toggle's persisted choice apply before first paint; see "Conventions that bite" for why this must stay a blocking script. **Locale anti-flash (Stage 116):** the same script extended (not duplicated — one script, two independent try/catch blocks) to also read `localStorage['dam:locale']` and set `document.documentElement.lang`/`data-locale` before paint; wraps `children` in `lib/i18n.tsx`'s `TranslationProvider`. The locale block never hardcodes `"en"`/`"it"` — it mirrors whatever's in storage, so it needs no edit when a language is added. |
| `app/page.tsx` | `/api/auth/login` | ✓ — redirects to `/home` on success; original Coperon split-screen brand login (Stage 34) superseded by a full-bleed `login-mm.jpg` illustration left panel with the wordmark on a white plate (top-left, natural blue, no invert filter) and a brand-blue two-line tagline (bottom-left, hidden under `lg`) (Stage 55); form panel (fields/links/`handleLogin`) unchanged; `coperon-technologies.png` and `login-mm.jpg` now present in `/public/` (Stage 56) — no longer pending. **Stage 91:** the "Need an account? Sign up" link (→ `/signup`) removed — replaced with plain, non-linking text "Contact your administrator for access," since public self-registration is gone; nothing else on this page changed. **Deliberately not localized (Stage 119):** was converted to `t()` mid-Stage-119, then reverted byte-for-byte to hardcoded English on explicit instruction — this page stays English-only by design, no logged-out language switcher exists or is planned; the 9 `login.*` keys added for it were removed from both locale files again. |

| `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx` (Stage 91, new) | `GET /api/invitations/validate/[token]` (on mount), `POST /api/invitations/redeem` (on submit) | ✓ tested — public, no auth guard, no `AppShell`; the invite-redemption page the whole invitation feature (Stage 90 backend) was missing. Same split-screen shell as `app/page.tsx` (same `login-mm2.jpg` illustration panel, wordmark, tagline, brand tokens) reproduced locally rather than imported, since the login page doesn't export its pieces. Four states: `loading` (spinner), `invalid` (generic — "This invitation is no longer valid" + a link to login; never distinguishes not-found/bad-token/expired/already-accepted, matching the backend's own no-oracle discipline), `valid` (the form), `success` (brief confirmation, then `router.push("/home")` after ~1.2s; **Stage 112:** the redeem response's `access_token` is stored to `localStorage` first, same key/pattern as `app/page.tsx`'s login form, so the user lands authenticated — no manual login). Email is rendered as a locked, non-`<input>` display block (never editable, never part of the redeem request body) with a lock icon and "Assigned by your invitation" caption. Password rules are restated client-side (length 8+, upper/lower/digit/special — mirrors `lib/users.ts::validatePasswordStrength`, which isn't reachable from a client component) as a live-checked list, plus a confirm-password field checked client-side for a match. When `needsCompanyDetails` is true, an additional "Company details" card (company address + company phone) renders — deliberately separate state (`companyAddress`/`companyPhone`) from the person's own `name`/`phone`, distinct field ids/labels ("Phone" vs "Company phone"), so the two can never be conflated in the request body; **verified live with deliberately different values** (`555-9999-PERSONAL` for the user, `555-1111-COMPANY` for the company) that they land on the correct rows. On submit, a non-OK response is inspected: an exact match on the backend's one fixed token-failure string (`"This invitation link is invalid or has expired."`) switches the whole page to the generic `invalid` state (the atomic token re-check inside `/redeem` is authoritative — this is what makes a link that was valid on load but got redeemed/expired before submit fail the same way as a dead link on load); any other error (weak password, missing name, duplicate email) is shown inline on the form instead, without discarding it. Submit button disabled while in flight. Verified live end-to-end (real accounts, real Gmail SMTP for invite creation, no Playwright): an editor invite validated and redeemed correctly (`needsCompanyDetails: false`, new user landed with the invite's `company_id`/`role_id`, next login's JWT confirmed); a first-admin invite into a disposable stub company (kept separate from Test Company, whose own stub state was deliberately preserved from Stage 90) validated with `needsCompanyDetails: true`, redeemed with distinct user/company phone values, and both the `users` row and the `companies` row (address + phone) were confirmed correct and un-conflated afterward; reusing the now-accepted token produced the identical generic `{status:'invalid'}` on both a fresh `validate` and a fresh `redeem` attempt, with no second account created. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean. **Stage 93:** both plain-text phone `<input>`s replaced with `<PhoneInput>` (`components/PhoneInput.tsx`) — a country dropdown (flag + dial code, default Lebanon) beside the number, composing `+<dial> <number>`; `phone`/`companyPhone` state now holds whatever each instance last emitted directly, no manual trim needed; the person-vs-company state separation from this stage's original build is unchanged, just now carrying a richer value. See Stage 93. **Stage 105:** the "Company details" card, its `tenantAddress`/`tenantPhone` state, and `needsTenantDetails` are all removed — the form is now exactly Name / locked Email / Phone / Password+confirm for every role, and a first-admin invite redeems identically to any other; tenant details are entered by the super admin at tenant creation instead. See Stage 105. **Localized (Stage 119):** every UI string converted to `t()` (`invite.*` + shared `common.*`, incl. the restated password-rule checklist and the "invited to {{tenant}} as {{role}}" sentence via `interpolateJSX`); tenant name and role value stay raw/untranslated (data + enum); the `PhoneInput` country-code `aria-label` localized too. |

list view adds `.dam-glass` tables for each section (sub-collections show name/sub-count/file-count from the Stage 50 API fields; files show thumbnail/type/size/Download/Details/kebab), sharing the same select-mode, bulk-download, and kebab handlers as the grid. **Stage 51/53:** files with no preview show `<FileTypeIcon>` + `shortTypeLabel()` instead of the old grey MIME-text box, in both views. **Stage 52/53:** PDFs with a generated thumbnail display `object-cover object-top` on white (top-of-page crop); the "Generate thumbnail" kebab item and upload auto-generate now cover PDFs as well as videos. **Stage 55:** rebranded to tokens (`bg-brand`, `--color-brand-dark` sidebar, etc.) — presentation only, no logic changed. **Stage 57:** the breadcrumb is now the full ancestor chain (`Home / [ancestors] / current`), collapsing beyond 4 segments into an expandable `…`, mobile always `Home / … / current` — superseding the old "Home / real collection name" breadcrumb; access-checked exactly like every other collection read (see Stage 57's own row above). **Stage 58:** the select-mode bar gained bulk "Remove from collection" (`canAdmin || canUpload`) and bulk "Delete permanently" (`canAdmin`, destructive red) beside "Download selected", looping the same per-file endpoints the kebab items already call. **Stage 63:** the Files section is now server-paginated — `GET /api/collections/[id]/files` takes `page`/`pageSize` (whitelisted `[25, 50, 100]`, default 25); a single `fetchFiles()` replaces the old unpaginated refetch path and is reused by upload, URL-import, and both bulk handlers; `dam:pagesize:collection` persists the page size (`page` itself never persisted); select mode is now per-page (a `useEffect` on `[page]` clears `selectedIds` and exits select mode, label reads "Select all on this page"); the same pager UI as the media library sits below the Files grid/list, shared by both views. **Stage 67:** the Sub-collections section is now independently server-paginated too — `GET /api/collections?parentId=<id>` takes its own `page`/`pageSize` via a new `fetchSubcollections()`, with entirely separate `subPage`/`subPageSize`/`subTotal` state and its own persisted key (`dam:pagesize:subcollections`) and pager UI, sitting below the Sub-collections grid/list; `handleCreateSubcollection` now refetches on success instead of prepending; the two pagers (Sub-collections and Files) are fully independent — neither's page/page-size state affects the other. **Stage 76:** the sub-collection sort select, sub-collection page-size select, and files page-size select changed `dark:bg-white/10` → `dark:bg-card` (dark-mode option-popup legibility fix; see Stage 76). **Stage 77:** the collection-actions dropdown (New sub-collection/Rename/Change thumbnail/Delete collection, opened from the breadcrumb kebab) is now rendered via `createPortal(..., document.body)`, `position: fixed` from the trigger button's `getBoundingClientRect()` (`colKebabTriggerRef`/`colKebabPos`) — escapes the `dam-glass` backdrop-filter stacking-context trap that no `z-index` value could lift it out of; menu items/handlers/the shared click-outside backdrop unchanged; see Stage 77. **Stage 78:** the file-card "Edit metadata" kebab item (grid and list views) widened from `canAdmin &&` to `(canAdmin || canUpload) &&`, and the `MetadataDrawer` call site's `canAdmin={canAdmin}` prop changed to `canEdit={canAdmin || canUpload}` (prop renamed, see `MetadataDrawer`'s own row) — editors can now open both the Details drawer and the full-page editor for files in collections they already have access to; see Stage 78. **Stage 79:** an admin-only "Access" item added to the top-bar collection kebab and to both the grid and list sub-collection card kebabs, all opening one shared modal (plain top-level `fixed` overlay like the lightbox — not inside a `.dam-glass` card, so no portal needed, unlike the Stage 77 collection-actions kebab or `CollectionTreePicker`); the modal fetches `GET /api/collections/[id]/access` for whichever id opened it and renders editable checkboxes (root) or a read-only "inherited from `<root>`" view with a link (sub-collection) purely from the response, never from which kebab it was opened from; see Stage 79. **Stage 88 superseded the root/sub-collection split described above:** the modal is now a single always-editable checkbox form at any depth (no more read-only branch, no more root link) — `AccessCompany` gained `inheritedFrom: {id, name} | null`, rendered as a small "(also via {name})" next to an ungranted-but-inherited company, purely informational and never gating editability; `accessSelected`/`toggleAccessCompany`/the PUT body are otherwise unchanged. See Stage 88. **Localized (Stage 118):** every UI string converted to `t()` (`collections.*` + shared `common.*`/`media.*`/`shares.*` reuse — the Files section's pagination/select-mode/upload/kebab UI is the same as the media library, so it reads `media.*` keys directly); the 0-subcollections delete-confirm is a separate non-pluralized key, not a CLDR case; collection/file names and user data untouched. |

A 12-row × 2-column (editor/viewer) matrix in the task's own given key order; a role with no key for a given row renders a plain "—", never a toggle; each cell is an instant-toggle (`PUT`, no batched Save). **Stage 115:** the per-row "Default"/"Custom" label and conditional "Reset" link (which had called `DELETE /api/permissions`) are gone — the header's "Reset all to defaults" already covers the reset need, and the variable-width label/link was making the column jitter; a cell now renders only the toggle (or the em-dash for a role/key pair that doesn't apply), so both columns are fixed-width and align cleanly. `handleToggle`/`PUT` and the header's `handleResetAll` are unchanged. **Stage 114:** the super admin's in-page tenant picker (top-bar `actions`) is now the shared `components/Select.tsx` instead of a native `<select>`, populated the same way from `GET /api/tenants`; a tenant admin's own tenant is still implicit, no picker shown. | `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` | *(unchanged fetch list — see its own row above)* plus `GET /api/permissions/mine` (Stage 108, on mount) | **Stage 108:** the one page both editors and viewers actually use gained a `myPerms` bundle (fetched once, optimistically pre-seeded to mirror pre-Stage-108 visibility so there's no render flash) driving every now-configurable action's visibility: Import URL/Upload (`upload`), the top-bar collection kebab's New sub-collection/Rename (`create_collection`/`rename_collection`, Change thumbnail/Access/Delete unchanged `canAdmin`-only), both sub-collection kebabs' Rename, the per-file kebab's Rename/Edit metadata/Generate-or-Regenerate thumbnail/Remove from collection/Delete permanently (one key each), the bulk-action bar's Download selected (previously ungated)/Remove from collection/Delete permanently, the per-card Download button and "Details" trigger (previously unconditional for every role, now `download`/`view_metadata`), and `MetadataDrawer`'s `canEdit` prop. New: a "Share" kebab item (gated on `create_share`, the one permission with no pre-existing UI at all) opening a compact modal — access level, expiry, `POST /api/shares`, one-time-shown link with copy — the minimal surface needed to make that permission clickable, not a duplicate of `/admin/shares`. |
| `app/confirm-email/page.tsx` (Stage 107, new) | `GET /api/profile/email-change/validate`, `POST /api/profile/email-change/confirm` | ✓ tested — public, no auth guard, `AuthShell` layout, `?token=` query param (same shape as `/reset-password`). Four states (`loading`/`invalid`/`ready`/`success`); the validate call is read-only and fires automatically on load, but the actual swap only ever happens on an explicit "Confirm change" button click — deliberately never automatic, so an email client's link-prescanner opening this page can't itself burn the single-use token. A confirm-time address conflict (409) is shown inline on the still-`ready` screen; every other failure falls back to the generic `invalid` state. **Localized (Stage 119):** every UI string converted to `t()` (`confirmEmail.*` + shared `common.*`); the ready/success subtexts interpolate the new email address via `t()`/`interpolateJSX`. |

## Shared frontend components

**Brand color (Stage 55):** lives in one place — `frontend/app/globals.css`'s `:root` (`--color-brand`, `--color-brand-hover`, `--color-brand-dark`, `--color-brand-light`, `--color-brand-mid`, `--shadow-color-brand`), re-declared inside `@theme inline` so Tailwind v4 generates real utilities (`bg-brand`, `border-brand-dark`, `ring-brand/15`, …). No component carries a raw brand hex — a rebrand is a five-value edit to that one file. Semantic colors (success green, destructive red, the amber "Unassigned" badge, `FileTypeIcon`'s per-type accents) are untouched and intentionally do not read brand tokens.

**Neutral/tint tokens + dark mode (Stages 74–75):** the same `globals.css`/`@theme inline` pattern now also covers 13 neutral/tint tokens (`--color-ink`/`--color-slate`/`--color-line`/`--color-mist`/`--color-content-base`/`--color-card` + a 7-value pale-tint family) and a `[data-theme="dark"]` block redefining all of them plus the glass and aurora-opacity variables — see Stage 74 (promotion) and Stage 75 (dark values, the toggle, the transition) for the full detail; not re-described here to avoid duplicating those entries.

| File | Purpose | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `components/AuthShell.tsx` | Brand shell for the four secondary auth pages (`forgot-password`, `reset-password`, `change-password`, `signup`) — 60px header strip (navy → `--brand-dark` token as of Stage 55, white-out filter kept) with centered white `coperon-technologies.png` logo (now present on disk — Stage 56), over a centered white card on the mist background. Exports shared style-token class constants (`authLabelClass`, `authInputClass`, `authButtonClass`, `authMessageClass`, `authLinkClass`, `authBackLinkClass`, all reading brand tokens as of Stage 55) so all four pages stay visually identical. Presentation-only — no auth/API logic. | ✓ built (Stage 34b; header strip rebranded Stage 55) — imported by all four auth pages |
| `components/AppShell.tsx` | Persistent logged-in application shell — fixed **solid** sidebar (`--brand-dark` token as of Stage 55, was flat navy; logo, Collections/Companies/Media/Users/Metadata/Shares nav (Companies added Stage 92, between Collections and Media, `superAdminOnly: true` — hidden from tenant admins/editors/viewers by the pre-existing Stage 89 filter with no new gating logic; nav `key` renamed `"companies"` → `"tenants"` at Stage 104, the label itself still reads "Companies") with active-item highlight — the highlight's left accent bar deliberately uses `brand-light`, not full `brand`, for contrast against the pill — bottom account row + Sign out popover) + an aurora-glassmorphism content column: `#EAF1F8` base, three pinned blurred blobs (now `var(--color-brand)`/`var(--color-brand-light)`/`var(--color-brand-mid)` as of Stage 55, same values as before, now tokenized) behind a frosted (`backdrop-filter: blur(16px)`) top bar (`title`/`actions`) and `children`, with a shared `.dam-glass`/`.dam-glass-topbar` stylesheet gated by `@supports` so unsupported browsers get an opaque fallback instead of losing text contrast. Decodes the JWT (`canAdmin`/`email`/`groupName`) to gate nav items and populate the account row; owns its own no-flash auth-guard redirect, centralizing what used to be duplicated per page. **Collapsible (Stage 54):** the sidebar now toggles between 220px expanded and a 64px icon rail — persisted to `dam:sidebar:collapsed`, defaulting to collapsed for viewers (`!canAdmin && !canUpload`) and expanded otherwise; `canAdmin`/`canUpload` are read only for this default, nav-item gating itself is unchanged. Sidebar logo (`coperon-technologies.png`) now renders — confirmed present on disk as of Stage 56. **Dark mode (Stage 75):** the account-menu dropdown gained a "Dark mode"/"Light mode" toggle item (all roles, no gating) — a track-and-knob switch matching the Searchable/Required toggle styling; click flips `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` and persists to `localStorage['dam:theme']` (`'light'`\|`'dark'`, read back in the same mount `useEffect` as `dam:sidebar:collapsed`), wraps the flip in a View-Transitions circular clip-path wipe from the toggle button's own coordinates when supported and motion isn't reduced (instant otherwise — no CSS color-transition fallback). Label/icon/`aria-label` name the action, not the current state (fixed same session): "Dark mode" + moon in light theme, "Light mode" + sun in dark theme; `aria-pressed={darkMode}` still reflects current state. The sidebar's own navy/white styling is unchanged by any of this — see Stage 75's "deliberately not themed" note. **Tenant switcher (Stage 106):** non-super-admin users with cross-tenant access (role 2 with the `can_access_all_tenants` flag, named `is_internal` until Stage 110) get an amber "Acting as" pill + tenant `<select>` rendered in the shell's own topbar — on every page, always visible, so whose data is on screen is never ambiguous; options from `GET /api/tenants` (bare, cross-tenant-access-widened); selection persisted in the `dam_acting_tenant` cookie (the transport too — the browser sends it on every API call, read server-side by `lib/session.ts::actingTenantId`), defaulting to the user's own tenant; changing it hard-reloads so every page refetches under the new tenant; sign-out clears it. (`app/share/[token]/page.tsx`, Stage 47, and `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`, Stage 91, are deliberately **not** in this list — both are public, unauthenticated pages and must never render inside this auth-gated shell.) |

Renders nothing if no field definitions exist; when editable, click-to-edit with the description-editor's Save/Cancel/saving/saved/error UX; otherwise read-only and only the fields that have a value (empty rows hidden). Type-aware per field (Stage 42): `text`/`textarea` plain controls, `date` a native date input, `checkbox_group` renders the field's `options` as checkboxes (parsed from/serialized to a comma-joined value), `tag` a free-type chip input (Enter/comma commits, × removes); both read-only views render `checkbox_group`/`tag` as chip pills. Props: `resourceId`, `canEdit` (renamed from `canAdmin` Stage 78 — same boolean role, widened meaning: "can edit this resource," resolved by the caller; the API's own `canEditResourceMetadata`, `lib/permissions.ts`, is the real boundary, not this prop), (Stage 44) an additive optional `autoEdit?: boolean` (default `false`) — when true and `canEdit`, a `useRef`-guarded effect calls the existing `startEdit()` exactly once on first load, opening straight into the edit form instead of the collapsed click-to-edit summary — (Stage 45) two more additive, optional pieces: `hideActions?: boolean` (default `false`) — hides the internal Save/Cancel footer, for callers that drive saving themselves — and an imperative handle (`forwardRef`/`useImperativeHandle`) exposing `save(): Promise<boolean>`, wired to the same `handleSave` the internal Save button calls — and (Stage 48) an additive optional `enforceRequired?: boolean` (default `false`): when true, `handleSave()` first checks every field whose definition has `required: true` via a new `isValueEmpty(fieldType, value)` helper (`text`/`textarea`/`date` → blank/whitespace; `checkbox_group`/`tag` → no selections); any empty required field sets a per-field inline error ("This field is required," cleared as the field is edited) and `save()` returns `false` **without calling the API** — no partial save. Every other behavior is unchanged. | ✓ built + tested (Stage 40; type rendering extended Stage 42; `autoEdit` added Stage 44; `hideActions` + imperative `save()` added Stage 45; `enforceRequired` validation added Stage 48; `canAdmin` renamed `canEdit` Stage 78) — relocated unmodified into `components/MetadataDrawer.tsx` (Stage 43); has **three** mount sites: `components/MetadataDrawer.tsx` (quick view/edit, passes neither `autoEdit`, `hideActions`, a ref, nor `enforceRequired` — unaffected, still click-to-edit, saves freely even with empty required fields), `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx` standalone mode (Stage 44, passes `autoEdit` only — unaffected by Stage 45 or 48, also saves freely), and the same page's workflow mode (Stage 45, passes `autoEdit hideActions ref={metadataRef}`, joined by `enforceRequired` in Stage 48 — the only mount site where required fields are enforced); no longer mounted inline on the file card itself — the one-fetch-per-rendered-card characteristic remains resolved by Stage 43 (see "Known characteristics / tech debt"). **Localized (Stage 118):** every UI string converted to `t()` (`resourceMetadata.*` + shared `common.*`, incl. the "This field is required" per-field error and the tag-chip remove `aria-label`); field *names* and values are tenant-defined data, left untouched — only the surrounding chrome (Save/Cancel/Saved, required-field errors) is translated |

Header shows the asset's filename + a "type · size" meta line. Owns no metadata fetch/save logic itself — purely a mounting surface and open/close state holder; all read/write behavior (including editable vs. read-only and every Stage 42 field type) comes from the unmodified `ResourceMetadata`. Opened via a "Details" trigger button on each file card, visible to all roles. Takes `mimeType`/`className`; never renders raw MIME text. | ✓ built (Stage 51) — imported by `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` and `app/admin/media/page.tsx` (grid + list), and, as of Stage 53, `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx` and `app/share/[token]/page.tsx` |

Props: `id`, `required`, `disabled`, `defaultCountryCode` (defaults to `lib/countries.ts`'s `DEFAULT_COUNTRY_CODE`, Lebanon), `onChange`. | ✓ built + tested (Stage 93) — both phone fields on `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx` (the person's own, and, when `needsCompanyDetails`, the company's) are separate instances writing to separate parent state, never shared; Stage 114's swap to `Select` is type-checked but not browser-verified this session. **Localized (Stage 119):** the one hardcoded string this component had — the country-code `<Select>`'s `aria-label` — converted to `t("common.countryCode")`; the number `<input>`'s placeholder (`"70 123 456"`, an illustrative digit format, not language content) is unchanged |
| `components/CollectionTreePicker.tsx` | One reusable expand/collapse, lazy-loaded collection picker, replacing every flat em-dash-indented `<select>` in the app. Fetches a level's children only on expand (`GET /api/collections?parentId=<id>`), cached per node for the component's lifetime (never invalidated — see "Known characteristics"); a chevron shows only when `subcollection_count > 0`. Renders its panel via `createPortal(..., document.body)` at `position: fixed` computed from the trigger's bounding rect, sidestepping the `.dam-glass` `backdrop-filter`/overflow popover trap entirely rather than fighting stacking context per site (see "Conventions that bite"). Props: `value`/`onChange` (single-select), `commitOnSelect` (fire-and-close, no persisted selection — the two assign/mutate sites), `allowNone`/`rootLabel` (the media filter's "All collections"), `excludeIds` (already-member exclusion), `excludeSubtreeOf` (disables a node + its whole descendant branch; unused today, built for a future move/set-parent picker), `disabled`, `placeholder`, `className`. Full keyboard support (arrow up/down to move, right/left to expand/collapse, Enter to select, Escape to close) via `aria-activedescendant` + `role="tree"`/`"treeitem"`, not real per-row DOM focus. **Stage 80:** the panel's `className` no longer carries `dam-glass` — it had been paired with `bg-card` and, at equal specificity, `dam-glass`'s translucent background won by source order, so content behind the portal showed through in dark mode; now plain opaque `bg-card` (unchanged `border-line`/`shadow-lg`/`rounded-[10px]`), matching every other dropdown in the app. Presentation only — the fetch/cache/keyboard/positioning logic described above is unchanged. **Stage 114:** this component's own portal/fixed-positioning mechanism (and its no-reposition-on-scroll characteristic, see "Known characteristics") is now shared with `components/Select.tsx` — the flat-list sibling this stage introduced for every native `<select>` in the app; the two components are mechanically the same picker with the tree flattened away, not two independent implementations. | ✓ built + tested (Stage 71) — the four sites: media collection filter (`allowNone`), share target (`app/admin/shares/page.tsx`, controlled), per-file "Add to collection" kebab (`commitOnSelect` + `excludeIds`, grid and list DOM copies both use it, no fork), bulk "Add to collection" (`commitOnSelect`); dark-mode opacity fix (Stage 80) verified via a compiled-CSS before/after comparison, no Playwright |
| `components/Select.tsx` (Stage 114) | Flat single-select dropdown replacing every native `<select>` app-wide — `<option>` text is drawn by the browser in the OS font, unstylable by CSS, which is the entire reason this exists. Mechanically `CollectionTreePicker` with the tree flattened to a plain option list: same `createPortal(..., document.body)` at `position: fixed` from the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect()`, same outside-click/Escape handling, same no-reposition-on-scroll characteristic (see "Known characteristics"). Adds real listbox keyboard parity a flat list needs but a tree never did: Up/Down, Enter/Space, Escape, Home/End, and typeahead (600ms buffer, wraps forward from the active option). Props: `value`, `onChange(value: string)`, `options: {value,label}[]`, `placeholder?`, `disabled?`, `id?` (label association — `<button>` is labelable per spec), `ariaLabel?`, `className?` (full override, same convention as `CollectionTreePicker`'s). Unresolved-value fallback (`…`) for a value not yet present in `options` (e.g. `AppShell`'s acting-tenant switcher while it's still loading). | ✓ type-checked (Stage 114) — `tsc`/`next build` clean, grep-confirmed zero native `<select>` remain across all 31 former sites; not browser-verified this session per the no-Playwright constraint |

## Home page (`/home`)

Client component (`"use client"`). Renders inside `components/AppShell.tsx` (`active="collections"`, `title="Collections"`) — the shell owns the sidebar, the aurora-glassmorphism content background, the frosted top bar, the account menu/Sign out, and its own no-flash auth guard. The page also keeps its own pre-existing token check/redirect (drives data fetching; harmless overlap with the shell's guard) and decodes `canAdmin` client-side to gate the admin-only controls it renders into the shell's `actions` slot.

| Area | Status |
|---|---|
| Sidebar (solid navy, logo, Collections/Media/Users nav, account row + Sign out) | ✓ provided by `AppShell` (Stage 35) — nav + account menu moved here, superseding the inline Stage 32 top-nav/account-menu that used to live directly on this page |
| Top bar — frosted glass (`rgba(255,255,255,0.55)` + `blur(16px)`, opaque fallback where `backdrop-filter` is unsupported); "Collections" title (left) + search input and admin-only "New collection" button (right, passed as `actions`); the old "Collections \| Media" tab row is gone — nav is in the sidebar now | ✓ |
| Search — debounced live dropdown, input restyled to the shared frosted-glass (`.dam-glass`) treatment; "Collections" and "Files" sections; click navigates; closes on outside-click/Escape/navigation; stale-response discard via counter stamp — logic unchanged from before the shell/aurora refactor | ✓ wired to `GET /api/search` |
| Collections grid | ✓ top-level collections from `GET /api/collections`, now server-paginated as of Stage 67 (`page`/`pageSize`); each card is a `<Link>` to `/collections/<id>` restyled to frosted glass (`rgba(255,255,255,0.62)` + `blur(18px)`, `14px` radius, soft shadow `0 8px 24px rgba(0,46,92,0.10)`); cover image displayed if `cover_storage_key` set (fetched as presigned blob, `object-cover`), tinted folder-glyph placeholder otherwise (Stage 59: `cover_storage_key` now auto-populates from the collection's own files when previously unset, so fewer collections show the placeholder over time; a manual "Change thumbnail" always still wins); no secondary meta line (the `Collection` objects carry no group-access field yet) |
| Pager (Stage 67) | ✓ `‹ Prev` / numbered pages with `…` ellipsis for long ranges / `Next ›` (disabled at the ends), "Showing X–Y of {total}", page-size `<select>` (25/50/100, persisted to `dam:pagesize:home-collections`; `page` itself never persisted) — shared by grid and list, no forked logic |
| Sort control (Stage 60) | ✓ "Default order" / "Name A–Z" / "Name Z–A" `<select>` in the top bar; persisted to `dam:sort:collections` (shared with the Sub-collections sort control on the detail page); passed as `sort` to `GET /api/collections`; resets to page 1 (Stage 67) |
| "New collection" form (name + group checkboxes) | ✓ **super admins only as of Stage 89** (was any admin) — button/form and the `GET /api/companies` fetch that feeds it both gate on `roleName === 'super_admin'`, not `canAdmin`, since collection creation is super-admin-only under the Stage 89 access model; same `handleCreate` submit fields as before, opened via the solid `#0057B1` "New collection" button (soft blue glow shadow) into a modal (Cancel/backdrop/Escape all close it); as of Stage 67, on success it refetches the current page (`fetchCollections()`) instead of optimistically prepending the new collection into local state, since a prepend would silently exceed `pageSize` and leave `total` stale now that the grid is paginated |
| Empty state / error state | ✓ |

**Stage 87:** the "New collection" form's grant checkboxes switched from a hardcoded `GROUPS` const (`[{1, Administrators}, {2, Editors}, {3, Viewers}]`, never wired to `/api/groups`) to real companies fetched from the new `GET /api/companies` (admin-only fetch, on mount) — `selectedGroups: number[]`/`toggleGroup` became `selectedCompanies: string[]`/`toggleCompany`, and `handleCreate` now posts `companyIds` to `POST /api/collections`, matching that route's Stage 87 switch to `company_collection_access`.

**Stage 89:** `AppShell`'s `NAV_ITEMS` (the sidebar) gained a `superAdminOnly` flag alongside the pre-existing `adminOnly` — both tiers of admin now carry `canAdmin: true`, so `adminOnly` alone no longer means "super admin." Media/Metadata/Shares are `superAdminOnly: true` (hidden from a company admin); Users is not (a company admin keeps it, scoped to their own company server-side). The three admin sub-pages' own entry gates (`app/admin/media`, `app/admin/metadata-fields`, `app/admin/shares`) switched their auth check from `payload.canAdmin` to `payload.roleName === 'super_admin'` too, so a company admin navigating directly by URL gets the same "Not authorized" screen a non-admin would, rather than a page that loads and then 403s on every API call.

**`AppShell` rollout complete:** all four logged-in pages now render inside `AppShell` — `app/admin/media/page.tsx` (Stage 36), `app/admin/users/page.tsx` (Stage 37), and `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` (Stage 38) followed this page's Stage 35 migration. `components/AccountMenu.tsx` is no longer imported anywhere (last used by the collection detail page pre-Stage 38); the aurora-glass redesign now spans every logged-in surface.

## Collection detail page (`/collections/[id]`)

Client component (`"use client"`). Same auth guard as home (redirects to `/` if no token). Reads `id` from `useParams()`. Decodes `canUpload` and `canAdmin` from the JWT payload client-side (`atob` on the middle segment).

The sub-collections fetch fires inside the mount `useEffect`; the files fetch is a dedicated, paginated `fetchFiles()` (Stage 63) fired by its own `useEffect` keyed on `[id, authorized, page, pageSize]` — no longer part of the original mount fetch. After the file list loads, a separate `useEffect` fires per-image-file to fetch thumbnail blob URLs (async, non-blocking).

| Area | Status |
|---|---|
| Nav — `AppShell` sidebar (Collections active) + frosted top bar carrying the breadcrumb, collection kebab, and (`canUpload`) Import URL/Upload controls | ✓ (Stage 38) — supersedes the old Sign-out-only top-nav; navigation now lives in the sidebar, matching `/home`, `/admin/media`, and `/admin/users` |
| Breadcrumb — "Home / [ancestor chain] / current collection ⋮" with clickable Home + ancestor links | ✓ (Stage 57) — name loaded via `GET /api/collections/[id]`, ancestor chain via `GET /api/collections/[id]/ancestors`, both in the mount `useEffect`; full chain on desktop, collapsing beyond 4 segments into an expandable "…" (dropdown lists the hidden ancestors); mobile always "Home / … / current" (dropdown lists the full chain); an inaccessible ancestor renders as a muted, non-clickable "…", never a name or link; rendered in the `AppShell` frosted top bar (Stage 38) |
| Sub-collections grid | ✓ fetches `GET /api/collections?parentId=<id>` with bearer token, now server-paginated as of Stage 67 (`page`/`pageSize`, independent of the Files section's pagination below); cards link to `/collections/<childId>`; cover image displayed if `cover_storage_key` set (presigned blob, `object-cover`), gray placeholder otherwise (Stage 59: auto-populated from the collection's own files when previously unset). Section header carries a sort control (Stage 60, "Default order"/"Name A–Z"/"Name Z–A", shares `dam:sort:collections` with the home page); changing it resets to page 1 and refetches. |

Fully independent of the Files pager below (separate `subPage`/`subPageSize`/`subTotal` state) — two pagers coexist on this page. |

For any uploaded file whose mime is `video/*`, `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` fires fire-and-forget (no `await`, swallowed `.catch()`) right after `upload/complete` returns — never blocks or affects the batch summary; the generated thumbnail appears on the next list refresh. Cover-image upload (Actions dropdown) stays separate — single file, image-only. |

Video thumbnails are no longer part of this limitation — they're generated server-side via FFmpeg and served as small pre-scaled JPEGs (Stage 33/33b).

The breadcrumb loads the real collection name on first render via `GET /api/collections/[id]` (Stage 31) — it no longer depends on an in-session rename. Renaming (⋮ kebab → Rename) still updates the same `collectionName` state in place, so the breadcrumb stays in sync afterward too.
