## Files-in-collections API

### `POST /api/upload/complete` — extended

Accepts an optional `collectionId` string in the body alongside the existing `key / filename / contentType / size` fields.

- **Without `collectionId`:** original behaviour — inserts one row into `resources`, returns it.
- **With `collectionId`:**
  1. Validates `collectionId` is a string (400) and the collection exists (404).
  2. Non-admins: cascade ancestor check — walks from the target collection upward through `parent_id` and verifies `company_collection_access` for the user's company at **any level in that chain** (switched from `group_collection_access`/group in Stage 86; root-only through Stage 87, any-depth as of Stage 88 — see both). Fails closed on a null `companyId` (no query run). Returns 403 if no match. Admins bypass.
  3. Wraps the `resources` INSERT and the `collection_resource` INSERT in a single transaction; rolls back on any failure.

### `GET /api/collections/[id]/files` — paginated (Stage 63)

`requireAuth`. Returns files in a collection: `id, original_filename, mime_type, size_bytes, status, description, thumbnail_storage_key`. **Stage 63:** now paginated — accepts `page` (1-based, default 1) and `pageSize` (whitelisted `[25, 50, 100]` via `.includes()`, default 25), both bound as `$2`/`$3` into `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`. Response shape is `{ files, total, page, pageSize }`; `total` is a second parameterized query sharing the identical `FROM`/`JOIN`/`WHERE` as the page query (no `ORDER BY`/`LIMIT`/`OFFSET`), so it can't diverge from what's actually paged. An out-of-range `page` clamps server-side to the last valid page and the clamped value is returned.

- **Admins:** direct join, no access filter, paginated as above.
- **Non-admins:** same single-collection cascade CTE (start at `[id]`, walk to root, check the grants table) — **byte-for-byte unchanged by Stage 63; the grants table/id itself switched from `group_collection_access`/`groupId` to `company_collection_access`/`companyId` in Stage 86** (see Stage 86). Returns `{ files: [], total: 0, page: 1, pageSize }` (previously a bare `[]`) if the user's company has no access, or if `companyId` is null — same denial semantics, still does not leak that files exist.

### `GET /api/download/[id]` — secured

Previously: checked only the `canDownload` role flag.

Now (in addition): non-admins must have company access to at least one collection this file belongs to (resolved via cascade ancestor check across all `collection_resource` rows for the file, against `company_collection_access` as of Stage 86 — was `group_collection_access` before; a grant matches at any level in the chain as of Stage 88, not just the root). A file in no accessible collection, or a null `companyId`, returns 403. Admins bypass.

### `DELETE /api/collections/[id]/files/[fileId]` — new

`requireAuth`, not `requireAdmin` — a true super admin (`isSuperAdmin`, Stage 89; was `canAdmin`) bypasses unconditionally, everyone else (including an editor or a company admin) falls through to the same company-scoped cascade check every other access-CTE route uses. Deletes the `collection_resource` row for `(collection_id, resource_id)`. Does **not** touch `resources`, MinIO bytes, or any other `collection_resource` rows (the file remains in other collections). Idempotent: returns 200 even if the row was already absent.

**Cascade CTE shape (all GET/download/search/upload routes):** start at the relevant collection(s), `UNION ALL` walk upward joining `collections c ON rf.parent_id = c.id` while `rf.parent_id IS NOT NULL`, then join `company_collection_access` on `rf.id` and check `company_id` — a grant matches **at any level in the walk** (Stage 88; through Stage 87 this join additionally required `rf.parent_id IS NULL`, restricting matches to the root) and cascades downward from wherever it's placed (**switched from `group_collection_access`/`group_id` in Stage 86** — every call site fails closed on a null `companyId`, never falling back to `group_id`). See Stage 86 for the full list of switched call sites and Stage 88 for the any-depth change. **Stage 89:** the unconditional-bypass condition at every one of these sites switched from `user.canAdmin` to `isSuperAdmin(user)` — once a company admin also carried `canAdmin: true`, the old bypass would have handed them unrestricted cross-company read/download/search access; see Stage 89 for the full list of switched bypass sites (the same file list as Stage 86/88, plus `lib/permissions.ts`).

### `DELETE /api/collections/[id]` — new

`requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`). Verifies collection exists (404 if not), then `DELETE FROM collections WHERE id = $1`. The DB cascade on `parent_id ON DELETE CASCADE` automatically removes the entire sub-collection sub-tree, all their `collection_resource` links, and all `company_collection_access` grants (`group_collection_access` no longer exists as of Stage 94). `resources` rows and MinIO bytes are not touched — files survive.

### `GET /api/collections/[id]/descendant-count` — new

`requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`). Returns `{ count: N }` — the number of sub-collections beneath the given collection (not counting itself), via a recursive CTE that walks down through `parent_id`. Used by the UI to populate the confirm() warning before deleting a collection.

---

## Collections API (`app/api/collections/route.ts`)

**Schema:** `collections` has `parent_id uuid REFERENCES collections(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (nullable — `NULL` = top-level, non-null = nested) and `cover_storage_key text` (nullable — MinIO key for the collection's cover image; not linked to a `resources` row). `company_collection_access` rows (as of Stage 86 — was `group_collection_access`) may live on **any** collection, at any depth, as of Stage 88 — a grant is no longer restricted to top-level collections; access cascades downward from wherever it's placed, and a collection with no grant of its own (direct or inherited from an ancestor) is private.

**POST** — `requireSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 (was `requireAdmin`). Body: `{ name, companyIds?: string[], parentId?: string }` (`companyIds` — **switched from `groupIds: number[]` in Stage 87**; see below). Two paths in one transaction:
- **`parentId` absent/null (top-level):** inserts with `parent_id = NULL`, then one row per companyId into `company_collection_access`. `companyIds` required (array, possibly empty — empty means create with no grants yet); each id is uuid-validated and checked against the real `companies` table before any insert — an unknown id rejects the whole request with 400, listing every bad id. **As of Stage 87** — through Stage 86 this path still wrote `group_collection_access`, which nothing read, making every new top-level collection invisible to non-admins; confirmed by grep to have been the last runtime writer to that table anywhere in the app. See Stage 87.
- **`parentId` present (nested):** validates parent exists (404 if not), inserts with `parent_id` set. `companyIds` ignored — no `company_collection_access` rows. Access inherited from whichever ancestor (if any) carries a grant, at any depth as of Stage 88 — not necessarily the root.
- Both paths use `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK`; failure rolls back completely.

**GET** — `requireAuth`. Optional `parentId` query parameter:
- `parentId` absent or `"root"` → the home page's top-level view
- `parentId=<uuid>` → direct children of that collection (`parent_id = $2`)

Super admins (`isSuperAdmin` as of Stage 89 — was the broader `canAdmin`, which a company admin also carries and would have wrongly taken this branch too): `parentId` absent/`"root"` → true `WHERE parent_id IS NULL` collections; `parentId=<uuid>` → `WHERE parent_id = $1`. No access filter needed, unchanged by Stage 88. A company admin now falls through to the same "Non-admins" cascade below as any other authenticated user, scoped to their own company.

Non-admins, `parentId` present (browsing into a specific collection): a child is included if it, or **any of its own ancestors** (including the requested parent), carries a `company_collection_access` grant for the user's company — a recursive CTE walks each candidate child upward through `parent_id`, matching a grant at any depth (Stage 88; was root-only through Stage 87). A null `companyId` (old token, or a data anomaly) skips the query entirely and returns `[]`/zero-total, never falling back to `group_id`.

Non-admins, `parentId` absent (the home page — **new semantics as of Stage 88, not just an unrestricted version of the old root filter**): returns the *topmost* collections the company can reach — collections directly granted to the company that have **no granted ancestor**. Not `parent_id IS NULL` (a top-level collection may itself be ungranted while something beneath it is granted) and not every accessible collection (a granted child of an already-granted parent doesn't also surface here — it's reachable by browsing into the parent, same as any other child). Implemented as a `root_finder`-style walk tagged with `depth` (0 = the collection itself), keeping only collections with their own grant (`depth = 0` join) and no ancestor grant at `depth >= 1`.

Tested and verified (Stage 86, root-only; re-verified and extended Stage 88, any-depth): an editor and a genuine viewer both see the same 4 Test-Company-granted root collections on `/home` and their nested children on `/collections/<id>` (up from 3 pre-Stage-86 — both roles sharing one company's tenancy); neither sees an ungranted root or any of its children; a hand-minted old-shape token (no `companyId`) gets `[]` everywhere. **Stage 88, against the real collection tree:** a company granted only a mid-depth collection (`products / lucitag`, not `products` itself) saw exactly that one collection at the top level of `/home`, could open it and its cascade-visible children/files, and was denied `products` and its other branches (`ghidini`) at every surface — collection detail, files, download, search, and metadata; a new child created live under the granted collection was immediately visible with no new grant, while a new sibling created under the ungranted root stayed invisible; a root-level grant (`products`, held by the real Test Company) was reconfirmed to still cascade to every descendant, unchanged.

**Stage 50 addition:** every returned collection row also carries `subcollection_count`/`file_count`, computed via correlated subqueries appended to the final `SELECT c.*` in all four branches above — after all the WHERE/JOIN/cascade-CTE logic already described, so the counts ride on the already-access-filtered result set and add no new access surface. Feeds the Stage 50 list-view table columns on `/home` and `/collections/[id]`.

**Stage 60 addition:** an optional `sort` query parameter (`default` | `name_asc` | `name_desc`, whitelisted via `COLLECTION_SORT_MAP`) replaces the previously-unlabeled `ORDER BY c.created_at DESC` in the tail of all three query branches above — `default` keeps that exact prior behavior as the fallback. Substituted only after all WHERE/JOIN/cascade-CTE logic, the same placement discipline as the Stage 50 count columns, so ordering rides on the already-access-filtered result set and adds no new access surface.

**Stage 67 addition — opt-in pagination.** `page`/`pageSize` query params (`pageSize` whitelisted `[25, 50, 100]`, default 25) are only honored when `page` is present on the request (`searchParams.has('page')`); with no `page`, `GET` returns the bare array exactly as it always has — every pre-existing caller that doesn't send `page` (the Media page's "Add to collection" picker and collection-filter tree-walk, the shares-page target-tree walk) is unaffected, verified live. When `page` is present, all three branches (admin top-level, admin `parent_id`, non-admin cascade) run a second `COUNT(*)` query sharing the identical `WHERE`/CTE/JOIN as the row query — for the non-admin branch this meant splitting the `WITH RECURSIVE root_finder ...` CTE into a constant definition prefix and a reusable `FROM ... WHERE gca.group_id = $1 ...` suffix so the differing `SELECT` (row data vs. `COUNT(*)`) could be inserted between them, not a rewrite of the cascade logic itself. Response becomes `{ collections, total, page, pageSize }`; `page` past the last page clamps server-side, same convention as Stages 61/63. Consumed by `app/home/page.tsx` (top-level grid) and `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`'s Sub-collections section, each with independent pager state/persisted page-size key — see Stage 67's own row above.

---

## Database

**PostgreSQL 18**, running **in Docker** (not a native Windows install — corrected 2026-08-18). Connection: `postgresql://dam:<DB_PASSWORD>@localhost:5433/dam` — host port **5433**, because 5432 is taken by another project's container on this machine.

17 tables: `users`, `resource_types`, `resources`, `renditions`, `metadata_fields`, `resource_field_data`, `collections`, `collection_resource`, `email_tokens`, `audit_log`, `shares`, `tenants`, `roles`, `tenant_collection_access`, `invitations` (the last four added in Stage 84 — multi-tenancy schema), `access_log` (Stage 96), `tenant_role_permissions` (Stage 108). **`tenant_collection_access` is live as of Stage 86** — every collection-access read in the app checks it; `tenants`/`roles` are read by the Access editor and the JWT mint site respectively; **`invitations` is live as of Stage 90** — `POST`/`GET /api/invitations`, `DELETE /api/invitations/[id]`, and the public validate/redeem routes are its only readers/writers (see Stage 90). **`user_groups` and `group_collection_access` were dropped in Stage 94**, along with `users.group_id` — the legacy group model they backed had been dead for capability/access purposes since Stages 86/89; see "Not yet built" below for the full retirement. **`tenant_role_permissions` (Stage 108)** — `(tenant_id, role_id, permission_key)` composite PK, `enabled boolean not null`; absence of a row means "use the code default" (`lib/permissions.ts`'s `EDITOR_DEFAULTS`/`VIEWER_DEFAULTS`), so the table only ever holds explicit overrides — read by `lib/permissions.ts::hasPermission` (per-request memoized) and `GET /api/permissions`, written by `PUT`/`DELETE /api/permissions`.

~~Seeded groups: **Administrators** (id=1, all flags), **Editors** (id=2, download+upload), **Viewers** (id=3, download only), **Pending** (id=4, all flags false — default for self-registration, no collection access).~~ **`user_groups` dropped in Stage 94** — capability is now `role_id`-only (`super_admin`/`admin`/`editor`/`viewer`/`pending`, see `roles` below). Self-registration itself was deleted in Stage 105 — every new user now arrives via invite redemption with the invite's explicit `role_id`/`tenant_id`.

`resources` also has a nullable `thumbnail_storage_key text` column (Stage 33) — the MinIO key for a generated video-frame thumbnail; `NULL` until `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` succeeds for that resource.

`metadata_fields` also has a `sort_order integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` column (Stage 39) — controls admin-defined field display order.

`metadata_fields` also has an `options jsonb` column (Stage 42, nullable) — the choice list for `checkbox_group` fields; `NULL` for every other `field_type`.

`metadata_fields` also has a `required boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` column (Stage 48) — enforced only at the Save-and-next upload workflow gate (`components/ResourceMetadata.tsx`'s `enforceRequired` prop); all 12 existing fields defaulted `false` on the alter, and the `PUT /api/resources/[id]/metadata` API itself stays permissive regardless of this flag.

`metadata_fields` also has an `exif_source text` column (Stage 81, nullable = no mapping) — an admin-configured pointer into the fixed whitelist in `lib/exifSources.ts` (e.g. `iptc.ObjectName`, `exif.DateTimeOriginal`); when set, `lib/exif.ts`'s `applyExtractedMetadata` auto-fills that field from a newly-uploaded image's embedded EXIF/IPTC/XMP, but only where the resource has no value for it yet.

`metadata_fields` also has a `tenant_id uuid REFERENCES tenants(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` column (Stage 103, nullable) — `NULL` = global (Coperon's, every field that existed before this stage stayed `NULL`); set = that one tenant's own field, invisible to every other tenant. **This stage also changed `name`'s uniqueness from global to per-scope**: the old single `UNIQUE(name)` constraint (`metadata_fields_name_key`) was dropped and replaced with two partial unique indexes — `metadata_fields_global_name_key` (`name` unique among global fields) and `metadata_fields_tenant_name_key` (`tenant_id, name` unique within one tenant) — a plain `UNIQUE(tenant_id, name)` doesn't work here since SQL never treats two `NULL`s as equal, which would have let two different global fields share a name. Every field-read site in the app (the metadata drawer, the full-page editor, the upload workflow, required-field enforcement, search, EXIF auto-fill, and the admin field-definitions page) filters on `tenant_id IS NULL OR tenant_id = $viewerTenantId` — see Stage 103.

`shares` (Stage 46, new table) — external share-link capabilities: `id uuid PK`, `token_hash text` (bcrypt, never plaintext), `collection_id`/`resource_id uuid` (exactly one non-null, DB CHECK), `access_level text` (`view`/`download`, DB CHECK), `expires_at timestamptz NULL`, `created_by uuid REFERENCES users(id)`, `created_at`, `last_used_at timestamptz NULL` (not yet written — no reader exists until the Stage 2 public route is built), `revoked boolean DEFAULT false`, `label text NULL`. **`token_encrypted text NULL` added in Stage 72** — an AES-256-GCM-encrypted copy of the raw token (key: `SHARE_TOKEN_KEY` env var, `lib/crypto.ts`), decrypted only by the admin-only `GET /api/shares/[id]/link`; `NULL` for every share created before Stage 72, and those rows simply offer no "Copy link" (`hasLink: false`). Indexes on `created_by` and `revoked`.

**Multi-tenancy schema (Stage 84):** `tenants` (`id uuid PK`, `name`, `address`, `phone`, `created_at`) — currently one seeded row, "Test Company". `roles` (`id int PK`, `name unique`) — seeded 1=super_admin, 2=admin, 3=editor, 4=viewer, 5=pending; deliberately fresh sequential ids, not reused from `user_groups`. `tenant_collection_access` (`tenant_id`, `collection_id`, composite PK, both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE`) — **as of Stage 86, this is the table every collection-access read in the app actually checks** (was `group_collection_access` through Stage 85); it started as a straight copy of `group_collection_access`'s grant set (Stage 84's backfill) but the two can now diverge, since the Access editor (`GET`/`PUT /api/collections/[id]/access`) writes only to this table going forward — see Stage 86. `invitations` (`id uuid PK`, `token_hash`, `email`, `tenant_id`, `role_id`, `invited_by uuid REFERENCES users(id)`, `expires_at`, `accepted_at`, `created_at`) — empty, no route writes to it yet. `users` gained `tenant_id uuid REFERENCES tenants(id)` (nullable — `NULL` = super admin, no tenant), `role_id int NOT NULL REFERENCES roles(id)` (`DEFAULT 5` — see the note below), `phone text` (nullable). See Stage 84 for the full backfill/verification detail, Stage 85 for `tenantId`/`roleId`/`roleName` landing in the JWT, and Stage 86 for the read-side cutover.

`users.role_id`'s `DEFAULT 5` was added in Stage 84 because the then-live `POST /api/auth/register` never mentioned `role_id` in its `INSERT` (without a default, `NOT NULL` broke registration — caught live, not by `tsc`/`next build`). **As of Stage 105 the default is inert but harmless:** registration (`createUser`) is deleted, and the only remaining user INSERT — `POST /api/invitations/redeem` — always sets `role_id` and `tenant_id` explicitly from the invite, so the `NULL`-tenant onboarding gap this paragraph used to track can no longer occur. The default is left in place as a schema-level safety net, not a live code path.

`access_log` (Stage 96, new table; read side is Stage 97; widened Stage 98) — append-only event log feeding the per-tenant analytics dashboard: `id uuid PK`, `user_id`/`tenant_id`/`resource_id uuid` each `REFERENCES ... ON DELETE SET NULL` (deleting a user/tenant/resource nulls the FK but keeps the row, so a historical count is never destroyed), `action text` (`'view'`/`'download'`/`'share_create'`/`'share_access'`/`'upload'`/`'login'`, no CHECK constraint — matches the literal spec), `created_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()`, **`metadata jsonb` (Stage 98, nullable)** — event-specific detail (a share's `accessLevel`/`expiresAt`, an upload's `filename`, a login's `roleName`, a lightbox `view`'s `source` tag) that doesn't fit the universal columns; never a password, token, or raw share token. `tenant_id` is denormalized from the acting user's JWT at write time, not looked up fresh from the user's current row — an event keeps the tenant it happened under even if the user is later reassigned; for the two anonymous `share_access` events (no logged-in user exists on that path), it's instead resolved via `lib/shares.ts`'s `resolveShareOwningTenant()` — the one tenant whose grant reaches the shared target, or `null` if that's ambiguous (see Stage 98). Indexes: `idx_access_log_tenant_created (tenant_id, created_at)` (the per-tenant dashboard's actual access pattern as of Stage 97) and `idx_access_log_resource (resource_id)`. Written via `lib/accessLog.ts`'s fire-and-forget `logAccess()`, called from `GET /api/download/[id]` (`'download'`), `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` (`'view'`, `source: 'metadata'`), `POST /api/resources/[id]/view` (`'view'`, `source: 'lightbox'`, Stage 98's widened signal), `POST /api/shares` (`'share_create'`), the public `GET /api/share/[token]` and `.../file/[resourceId]` routes via `lib/shares.ts`'s `logShareAccess()` (`'share_access'`, `'viewed'`/`'downloaded'`), `POST /api/upload/complete` and `POST /api/upload/from-url` (`'upload'`), and `lib/auth.ts::login()` (`'login'`) — every site only after its own access/auth check already passed. Read only via `GET /api/analytics` (Stage 97), tenant-scoped from the token, filtering on `action = 'view'`/`'download'` explicitly — the four Stage 98 action values are simply invisible to those two filters, confirmed live with all six action types present simultaneously (see Stage 98). Records events from ship-time forward only; cannot reconstruct past activity.

`group_collection_access` is root-only **by query shape, not by schema** (confirmed during Stage 79's investigation) — the table itself is a plain `(group_id, collection_id)` pair with **no CHECK/FK constraint** restricting `collection_id` to a `parent_id IS NULL` row; nothing in the schema stops a grant from being inserted against a sub-collection. In practice every existing row points at a root, because `POST /api/collections`'s sub-collection path never writes one, and every consuming `root_finder`/cascade CTE across the app walks a collection **up** to its root before joining this table — a grant on a non-root `collection_id` would simply never be joined by any of them, silently inert rather than rejected. Also confirmed: `requireAdmin` (`lib/session.ts`) checks only the JWT's own `canAdmin` claim and never queries this table, so admin access has never depended on a grant row here — clearing every grant on a collection cannot lock an admin out of it. See Stage 79 and "Conventions that bite" below.

**Schema changes applied directly (not via a migration file — no migration tooling exists yet, so a rebuild from a fresh schema dump would miss these):** `resources.description` (Stage 14), `collections.cover_storage_key` (Stage 16), `resources.thumbnail_storage_key` (Stage 33), `metadata_fields.sort_order` (Stage 39), `metadata_fields.options` (Stage 42), new `shares` table (Stage 46, `CREATE TABLE` + 2 indexes — full DDL in Stage 46's changelog entry, not just an `ALTER`), `metadata_fields.required` (Stage 48, `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN required boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`), `users.full_name` renamed to `users.name` (Stage 49, `ALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN full_name TO name` — the column itself is original to the schema dump, nullable, and was never a later addition; only the rename is a Stage 49 change), `shares.token_encrypted` (Stage 72, `ALTER TABLE shares ADD COLUMN token_encrypted text`, nullable), `metadata_fields.exif_source` (Stage 81, `ALTER TABLE metadata_fields ADD COLUMN exif_source text`, nullable), new `tenants`/`roles`/`tenant_collection_access`/`invitations` tables + `users.tenant_id`/`users.role_id`/`users.phone` columns + the `roles` seed data (Stage 84, `CREATE TABLE` ×4, `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN` ×3, `INSERT INTO roles` ×5 — full DDL in Stage 84's changelog entry), `users.role_id SET NOT NULL` (Stage 84, applied only after the backfill script confirmed every row had one), `users.role_id SET DEFAULT 5` (Stage 84, added after `NOT NULL` broke registration — see the note above), **the legacy group-model drop (Stage 94)** — run in this exact order, for the FKs: `ALTER TABLE users DROP COLUMN group_id;` (also drops `users_group_id_fkey` and `idx_users_group`) → `DROP TABLE group_collection_access;` (drops its own FK to `user_groups`) → `DROP TABLE user_groups;` (now unreferenced). Verified via a full reference sweep first (see Stage 94's changelog entry) that nothing in the app read `group_id`/`group_collection_access`/`user_groups` for any access or capability decision before dropping. **New `access_log` table (Stage 96)** — `CREATE TABLE access_log (...)` (see the Database section's own entry above for the full column list) + its two indexes, applied directly via `psql`, same as every other schema change in this list. **`access_log.metadata` (Stage 98, `ALTER TABLE access_log ADD COLUMN metadata jsonb`, nullable).** **`metadata_fields.tenant_id` (Stage 103, `ALTER TABLE metadata_fields ADD COLUMN tenant_id uuid REFERENCES tenants(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, nullable) plus the per-scope uniqueness swap** — `ALTER TABLE metadata_fields DROP CONSTRAINT metadata_fields_name_key;` → `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX metadata_fields_global_name_key ON metadata_fields (name) WHERE tenant_id IS NULL;` → `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX metadata_fields_tenant_name_key ON metadata_fields (tenant_id, name) WHERE tenant_id IS NOT NULL;` (run in this order so uniqueness is never briefly unenforced). **`users.is_internal` (Stage 106, `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN is_internal boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false;` + `UPDATE users SET is_internal = true WHERE role_id = 1;` — the backfill is informational for role 1, whose internal status is hardcoded in code and never read from this column).** **The company→tenant terminology rename (Stage 104)** — pure renames, no new columns, no dropped data, run in one transaction in this order: `ALTER TABLE companies RENAME TO tenants;` → `ALTER TABLE company_collection_access RENAME TO tenant_collection_access;` → `ALTER TABLE tenant_collection_access RENAME COLUMN company_id TO tenant_id;` → `ALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN company_id TO tenant_id;` → `ALTER TABLE invitations RENAME COLUMN company_id TO tenant_id;` → `ALTER TABLE access_log RENAME COLUMN company_id TO tenant_id;` → `ALTER TABLE metadata_fields RENAME COLUMN company_id TO tenant_id;` → `ALTER TABLE tenants RENAME CONSTRAINT companies_pkey TO tenants_pkey;` → `ALTER TABLE tenant_collection_access RENAME CONSTRAINT company_collection_access_pkey TO tenant_collection_access_pkey;` → `ALTER TABLE tenant_collection_access RENAME CONSTRAINT company_collection_access_collection_id_fkey TO tenant_collection_access_collection_id_fkey;` → `ALTER TABLE tenant_collection_access RENAME CONSTRAINT company_collection_access_company_id_fkey TO tenant_collection_access_tenant_id_fkey;` → `ALTER TABLE users RENAME CONSTRAINT users_company_id_fkey TO users_tenant_id_fkey;` → `ALTER TABLE invitations RENAME CONSTRAINT invitations_company_id_fkey TO invitations_tenant_id_fkey;` → `ALTER TABLE access_log RENAME CONSTRAINT access_log_company_id_fkey TO access_log_tenant_id_fkey;` → `ALTER TABLE metadata_fields RENAME CONSTRAINT metadata_fields_company_id_fkey TO metadata_fields_tenant_id_fkey;` → `ALTER INDEX idx_access_log_company_created RENAME TO idx_access_log_tenant_created;` → `ALTER INDEX metadata_fields_company_name_key RENAME TO metadata_fields_tenant_name_key;`. Verified after: every FK, PK, and index confirmed renamed via `\d` on all five affected tables; row counts (2 tenants, 8 users, 12 global metadata fields, 4 tenant_collection_access rows, 136 resources, 32 collections, 6 shares) unchanged from immediately before the rename. **`email_tokens` widened for email-change confirmation (Stage 107)** — `ALTER TABLE email_tokens DROP CONSTRAINT email_tokens_purpose_check;` → `ALTER TABLE email_tokens ADD CONSTRAINT email_tokens_purpose_check CHECK (purpose = ANY (ARRAY['verify','reset','email_change']));` (run in that order so the CHECK is never briefly absent) → `ALTER TABLE email_tokens ADD COLUMN new_email text;` (nullable — only set for `purpose='email_change'`, holds the pending address until confirmed; see `lib/emailChange.ts`). **New `tenant_role_permissions` table (Stage 108)** — `CREATE TABLE tenant_role_permissions (tenant_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenants(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, role_id int NOT NULL REFERENCES roles(id), permission_key text NOT NULL, enabled boolean NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, role_id, permission_key));` — no seed data by design (absence of a row = the code default; see the Database section's own entry above). **`users.can_invite`** (Stage 109, `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN can_invite boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false;` + `UPDATE users SET can_invite = true WHERE role_id = 2;`) — same shape as `can_access_all_tenants`'s Stage 106 backfill (named `is_internal` until Stage 110), but here the backfill is load-bearing, not informational: every existing role-2 (tenant admin) user could send invitations before this stage under the old blanket rule, so backfilling `true` for all of them preserves that ability; a newly-created admin defaults `false` and needs an explicit super-admin grant. **Backfilled 2 of 2 existing role-2 users** (`newadmincompanytest@gmail.com` and `gkoueik@gmail.com` — see the Test accounts list below, whose stale `gkoueik` entry this stage's verification also caught and corrected), confirmed via `SELECT role_id, can_invite, count(*) FROM users GROUP BY role_id, can_invite` immediately after. **`is_internal` → `can_access_all_tenants` (Stage 110, `ALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN is_internal TO can_access_all_tenants;`)** — pure rename, zero behavior change; see the Stage 110 terminology note near the JWT contract below. Existing `access_log` rows carrying `action = 'internal_flag_change'` were migrated in place (`UPDATE access_log SET action = 'all_tenants_flag_change' WHERE action = 'internal_flag_change';`) rather than left mixed, so the activity feed has one code path for both historical and new rows — **1 row updated**.

Test accounts — **re-verified live against the real DB during Stage 94** (group column dropped, so the list is now stated in role/tenant terms; superseding the old group-based list). Because `dam_backup.sql` is what would seed production, these accounts and their passwords must be dealt with before any production restore — not carried over.

## Environment variables

`frontend/.env.local`:

```
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://dam:<DB_PASSWORD>@localhost:5433/dam
JWT_SECRET=<JWT_SECRET>
S3_ENDPOINT=http://localhost:9000
S3_REGION=us-east-1
S3_ACCESS_KEY=dam_minio
S3_SECRET_KEY=<S3_SECRET_KEY>
S3_BUCKET=damassets
SMTP_USER=georgeskey2004@gmail.com
SMTP_PASS=<app password>
APP_URL=http://localhost:3000
SHARE_TOKEN_KEY=<base64, 32 bytes decoded>
```

**Optional pool tuning (added 2026-08-31, all with working defaults — absent everywhere
today, and local dev is unchanged by leaving them unset):**

| Var | Default | Set it when |
|---|---|---|
| `PG_POOL_MAX` | `10` (pg's own) | **`1` on Vercel.** Every warm serverless instance holds its own pool against the same database; unbounded × N instances is how a serverless deploy exhausts Postgres. Floor is 1 — a smaller value is clamped up. |
| `PG_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` | `10000` | Rarely. A frozen serverless instance's idle sockets get reaped underneath it; discarding them first means the pool never hands out a dead one. |
| `PG_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` | `10000` | Rarely. Bounds the wait for a free connection instead of hanging forever (pg's default). `0` restores wait-forever. |

`APP_URL` drives the password-reset link (`lib/auth.ts`) and every generated share link (`POST /api/shares`); it's read fresh from the env var on every request, never derived from the incoming request's own host/port. **Stage 68:** this had drifted to `:3001` in this dev environment while the server actually runs on `:3000` — corrected; kept in sync from here on. Change to the production domain at deploy time — see "Deployment notes" below.

`SHARE_TOKEN_KEY` (Stage 72) is a base64-encoded 32-byte AES-256 key, used by `lib/crypto.ts` to encrypt share tokens at rest for admin retrieval (`POST /api/shares`, `GET /api/shares/[id]/link`) — required in any environment that creates or reads shares; missing or wrong-length fails clearly (thrown error), not silently. Losing or rotating it makes existing shares' `token_encrypted` permanently undecryptable (Copy-link breaks for those rows; the hash-validated public link is unaffected either way).

## Deployment notes

### Target hosting (decided 2026-08-31 — supersedes the 2026-08-18 plan)

| Tier | Where | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| App (Next.js) | **Vercel, Pro plan** | Root Directory = `frontend`. Pro is mandatory — Hobby is non-commercial use only. |
| PostgreSQL | **Neon**, pooled (`-pooler`) connection string | Codebase verified transaction-pooler safe (plan 3.1). |
| Object storage | **Backblaze B2** — bucket `coperon-dam-assets`, EU Central | Chosen over Cloudflare R2 because **R2 requires a payment method even on its free tier** and the team declined to add one. 10 GB free, no card, permanent. Live and verified 2026-08-31 (presigned URLs, path-style, CORS all proven); **206.5 MB / 307 objects still to copy across**. Caveat: B2 free egress is only ~3x stored bytes per month, where R2 was zero — watch it under real traffic. |
| Transactional email | **A provider (Resend/Postmark), not Gmail SMTP** | Not yet done — see below. |
| Coperon's Windows server | **No longer in the design** | Nothing is deployed to it. |

**What this replaces.** The 2026-08-18 plan put the app on Vercel but kept Postgres and
MinIO on Coperon's own Windows server, and recorded three open problems as a result: no
PgBouncer build for Windows, Docker Desktop being a poor fit for an unattended server,
and Postgres having to be exposed to the public internet with no IP allowlist possible
(Vercel's Pro egress IPs are dynamic) — that last one flagged as "the weakest joint in
the chosen design," never accepted or rejected. **Moving the database to Neon dissolves
all three at once**, and moving object storage to R2 (forced anyway by MinIO's
archival — plan 3.2) removes the "paid egress on every video round-trip" objection.
The Windows server then has no remaining job, which is why IIS is out; its setup notes
are preserved under plan 3.3 in case that reverses.

**The one thing that could still overturn this:** the old plan asserted asset bytes must
stay on Coperon infrastructure. **This file has never recorded a reason** — no contract,
no data-residency clause, just the assertion. If a real requirement exists, R2 is out
and so is most of this table, and the storage path becomes AIStor Free (MinIO's
successor) on hardware Coperon controls. **Settle this explicitly before Phase 3 spends
any money.** Everything else above is configuration.

**Email is an open gap, not a decision.** `lib/mail.ts` sends password-reset,
invitation, and email-change mail through Gmail SMTP with an app password. That is
roughly 500 sends/day, and Google is prone to flagging app-password SMTP arriving from
rotating serverless IPs. A transactional provider is the right answer on any host — the
same `nodemailer` transport, a config-only swap — but serverless makes it urgent rather
than merely advisable. Nothing has been changed here yet.

**Media processing needs no worker and no queue.** `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail`
spawns FFmpeg in-request. On Vercel that is bounded by `maxDuration = 300` (Pro's
ceiling) and by the function's memory and ~512 MB `/tmp`, and the route pulls the whole
asset into a `Buffer` before writing it out — so **very large videos are the practical
limit of this design**, and that limit should be measured against a real Vercel
deployment and then written down. It is not a reason to build a queue; if it ever
becomes one, a DB-backed job table is enough. Redis is not coming back.

### Vercel environment variables (compiled 2026-08-31)

Derived by grepping every `process.env.*` read in `app/`, `lib/` and `components/` — this
is the complete runtime set, not a recollection. Set these on the **Production**
environment; Root Directory is `frontend`.

| Var | Value | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `DATABASE_URL` | Neon **pooled** (`-pooler`) string | Rewrite `sslmode=require` → `sslmode=verify-full`. |
| `PG_POOL_MAX` | `1` | Mandatory on serverless — every warm instance holds its own pool. |
| `JWT_SECRET` | **a newly generated 32-byte value** | See the warning below. Do **not** copy the current one. |
| `S3_ENDPOINT` | `https://s3.eu-central-003.backblazeb2.com` | |
| `S3_REGION` | `eu-central-003` | The slug from the endpoint host. Not `auto` — that is R2-only. |
| `S3_ACCESS_KEY` | B2 `keyID` | |
| `S3_SECRET_KEY` | B2 `applicationKey` | |
| `S3_BUCKET` | `coperon-dam-assets` | |
| `SMTP_USER` | the Gmail address | Also used as the `From:` on every outbound mail. |
| `SMTP_PASS` | the Gmail app password | See the transactional-email gap in "Pending decisions". |
| `APP_URL` | `https://<real deployed domain>` | **No trailing slash.** Drives every share link and the password-reset link. |
| `SHARE_TOKEN_KEY` | **copied verbatim** from `.env.local` | Confirmed 2026-08-31 to be a valid 44-char base64 / 32-byte key. |

**Deliberately NOT set on Vercel:**

- `NODE_ENV` — Vercel sets it; setting it by hand causes more problems than it solves.
- `DIRECT_DATABASE_URL` — migrations cannot run on Vercel at all (`migrate.mjs` resolves
  `../../db/migrations`, and `db/` sits outside the deployed `frontend/` root). It belongs
  only on whatever machine runs migrations.
- `PG_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `PG_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` — defaults are correct; see `lib/db.ts`.

**`JWT_SECRET` must be regenerated, not copied.** Measured 2026-08-31: the live value is
**12 characters, letters only**, and Phase 1.2 records it as a guessable dictionary-word
string. It signs HS256 session tokens whose payload carries `canAdmin`/`roleId`, so
anyone who guesses it mints a super-admin token against the deployed app. Generate 32
random bytes instead. Rotating it signs every existing session out, which costs nothing
today and gets expensive after real users exist — this is the cheapest moment it will
ever have. **This closes half of Phase 1.2.**

**`SHARE_TOKEN_KEY` must be copied exactly, never regenerated.** It AES-encrypts share
tokens at rest (`lib/crypto.ts`); a new value makes every existing share's Copy-link
permanently undecryptable. It is the one secret in this table that must not be rotated.

**`APP_URL` is chicken-and-egg.** The real domain is not known until the first deploy, and
the default falls back to `http://localhost:3000`, which would silently put localhost
links into real invitation and password-reset emails. Deploy once, read the assigned
domain, set `APP_URL`, redeploy — and add that same domain to the B2 CORS rule at the same
time (plan 3.2), since it is needed in both places.

**Scope to Production only, at least initially.** Preview deployments would otherwise
share the one Neon database and the one B2 bucket, putting real data behind an unstable
per-commit URL that is in neither the CORS rule nor `APP_URL`.

### Config that must be right outside local dev, not merely present


- **`APP_URL`** must be the real public domain the app is served from (never `localhost` at any port) — it drives every generated share link and the password-reset email link, and is read fresh from the env var on every request rather than derived from the incoming request (see Stage 68, where this drifted to a stale port in dev).
- **`SHARE_TOKEN_KEY`** must be set in every environment that creates or reads shares, and treated as a backed-up secret like `JWT_SECRET`/`DATABASE_URL` — rotating or losing it makes every existing share's encrypted-token Copy-link (Stage 72) permanently undecryptable (409/500 on retrieval), though the hash-validated public share link itself keeps working regardless.

---

## JWT contract (must not change)

Algorithm: HS256. Expiry: 1 day. Payload: `sub, email, name, canDownload, canUpload, canAdmin, tenantId, roleId, roleName, canAccessAllTenants, canInvite` (claim renamed from `companyId` to `tenantId` at Stage 104 — see that stage; every other claim byte-for-byte unchanged, same values, same source). **Terminology note (Stage 110):** `isInternal` → `canAccessAllTenants` throughout — the DB column (`users.is_internal` → `users.can_access_all_tenants`), the JWT claim, the `lib/session.ts` helper, the `internal_flag_change` → `all_tenants_flag_change` access-log action (one historical row migrated in place, see the direct-DDL list), and the users-page "Internal" column (→ "All tenants"). Pure mechanical rename, zero behavior change — role 1 is still hardcoded `true` independent of the column, only a super admin can still set it, cross-tenant access and the tenant switcher behave identically. Rows below quoting Stage 106 as originally built still say `isInternal`/`is_internal` — that's accurate history, not a live name; read the current name from the code or from this note. A token minted before Stage 110 still carries `isInternal`, not `canAccessAllTenants` — every guard now reads only `canAccessAllTenants`, so such a token fails closed (treated as "no cross-tenant access") rather than erroring; tokens expire in 1 day, so this is purely transient. **`canInvite` added Stage 109** — additive only, identical shape to `canAccessAllTenants` below: minted from `users.can_invite` (role 1 hardcoded `true` at mint AND in `lib/session.ts::canInvite`, never dependent on the column); optional in `JwtPayload`, so a pre-Stage-109 token fails closed to "cannot invite" everywhere the claim is read. Never trusted from any request value — only the verified token. **`canAccessAllTenants` added Stage 106** (named `isInternal` until Stage 110) — additive only; minted from `users.can_access_all_tenants` (role 1 hardcoded `true` at mint AND in `lib/session.ts::canAccessAllTenants`, never dependent on the column); optional in `JwtPayload`, so a pre-Stage-106 token fails closed to no-cross-tenant-access everywhere the claim is read. Never trusted from any request value — only the verified token. **A token minted before Stage 104 still carries `companyId`, not `tenantId` — every guard now reads `tenantId`, so such a token fails closed (treated as "no tenant," i.e. zero access) rather than erroring; tokens expire in 1 day, so this is purely transient.** **`groupId`/`groupName` removed in Stage 94** — dead claims, never read by any guard (capability had already moved to `roleId` at Stage 89); `JwtPayload` (`lib/session.ts`) no longer types them either. A token minted before Stage 94 that still carries them verifies and works identically — extra claims are ignored — but nothing should be written expecting them going forward. Tokens issued by the old NestJS backend are accepted by the Next.js routes (same secret, same algorithm). **`name` added in Stage 49** — additive only, every other claim unchanged; `name` is `string | null` and may be entirely absent on tokens minted before Stage 49 (old tokens still verify fine; every consumer treats a missing/falsy `name` as "fall back to email"). **`companyId`/`roleId`/`roleName` added in Stage 85** (multi-tenancy Stage 2 — see Stage 85) — additive only, appended after the pre-existing claims, every other claim byte-for-byte unchanged; all three are optional in `JwtPayload` (`lib/session.ts`) since tokens minted before Stage 85 lack them entirely (such a token now fails closed wherever `isSuperAdmin`/`roleId` is read — treated as not-super-admin, not as an error). `companyId` is `string | null` (`null` for super admins, who have no company). **`roleId` now drives capability as of Stage 89** — `canDownload`/`canUpload`/`canAdmin` are derived at mint time (`lib/auth.ts::login()`) from `roleId` via `deriveCapabilitiesFromRole()` (super_admin/admin → `canAdmin`; +editor → `canUpload`; all but pending → `canDownload`), not from `group_id`/`user_groups` — the claim *names* on the payload are unchanged, only their source moved. Verified against the live DB that this produced no change in any real user's effective capability (the Stage 84 backfill was clean). `roleId`/`roleName` are also read directly (not just via the derived capability flags) by `isSuperAdmin()`/`requireSuperAdmin` to distinguish a true super admin (role 1) from a company admin (role 2, who also gets `canAdmin: true`) — see Stage 89.

---

## Password reset security

Both password flows are built and confirmed working:
- **Logged-in change-password** (`POST /api/auth/change-password`) — requires current password; `requireAuth` guard; no confirmation email.
- **Logged-out forgot/reset by email** (`POST /api/auth/forgot-password` → email → `POST /api/auth/reset-password`) — reset tokens are hashed at rest in `email_tokens` (bcryptjs, 10 rounds); tokens expire in 30 minutes; single-use (deleted on redemption); `forgot-password` returns a neutral 200 regardless of whether the email exists (prevents enumeration). Gmail delivery confirmed via `lib/mail.ts` (nodemailer, SMTP).

---

## Hard constraints

- **Raw SQL only** via `pg` driver — no ORM, no Prisma, ever.
- Parameterized queries (`$1`, `$2`) — never string-concatenate user input.
- Use **bcryptjs** (not `bcrypt`) — avoids Windows native-build issues.
- 401 = no/invalid token. 403 = valid token, wrong permission. Never swap these.
- 23505 → 409 Conflict. Empty required fields → 400.
- All config from env vars — nothing hardcoded.

---

## Conventions that bite if forgotten

- `ctx.params` in Next.js 16 route handlers is **async** — always `const { id } = await ctx.params`.
- **Never issue a pool query while holding a transaction client.** `db.connect()` checks
  a connection out of the pool; `db.query()` asks for a *different* one. Doing the second
  inside the `try` whose `finally` holds the `client.release()` is a deadlock whenever the
  pool is small: the awaited pool query waits for a connection that only `release()` can
  free, and `release()` sits after an `await` that never returns. It hangs forever — no
  error, no timeout, no log. Both upload routes carried this (post-`COMMIT` `logAccess` +
  `extractMetadataInline`) and it was invisible only because the pool was unbounded.
  Fixed 2026-08-31; `POST /api/invitations/redeem` had the correct shape all along and is
  the model: close the transaction, `release()`, **then** do pool work. Verified by
  reproduction against the live DB at `max: 1` — old shape hung, new shape returned.
  Re-check this before lowering `PG_POOL_MAX`.
- **`outputFileTracingIncludes` keys are picomatch globs, not literal route strings.**
  A dynamic segment written plainly — `"/api/resources/[id]/thumbnail"` — parses `[id]`
  as a *character class* matching a single `i` or `d`, so the key matches no route and
  the includes are silently dropped with no build error. Escape the brackets:
  `"/api/resources/\\[id\\]/thumbnail"` (Next's own `output.md` example does this).
  Verify by grepping the built `.next/server/app/**/route.js.nft.json` for the files you
  expected — that trace file is the only proof the key matched.
- Next.js `.env.local` is read at startup — restart `npm run dev` after editing it.
- **There is no native Postgres on this machine and no local `psql.exe`** — this bullet
  used to point at `"C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe"`, a path that does not
  exist; the "How to run" section corrected the same error on 2026-08-18 but this line
  survived it. Postgres is a Docker container on host port 5433. Reach it with
  `docker compose exec postgres psql -U dam -d dam`, or from Node through `pg` using
  `DATABASE_URL` (which is what every script in `frontend/scripts/` already does).
- `jsonwebtoken` (not `jose`) — exact behavioral match with NestJS.
- Password rule: 8+ chars, uppercase + lowercase + digit + special char (enforced in `validatePasswordStrength` inside `lib/users.ts`).
- Route handler error pattern: lib functions throw `AppError`; handlers catch with `if (err instanceof AppError) return Response.json(...)`.
- Upload flow: `POST /api/upload/url` → PUT to presigned URL → `POST /api/upload/complete`. The `key` from the url response must be passed verbatim to complete.
- Any npm package that resolves a filesystem path at import time (e.g. `ffmpeg-static`) must be added to `serverExternalPackages` in `next.config.ts`, or Turbopack bundles and rewrites the path to a nonexistent build-output location — the video-thumbnail route hit this exact bug (`spawn` → `ENOENT`) before the config was added. **`pdfjs-dist` and `@napi-rs/canvas` (Stage 52) are the same class of module** (the former reads its `standard_fonts/` data from a real disk path, the latter ships a native, prebuilt-per-platform `.node` binary) — both were added to `serverExternalPackages` proactively, and the compiled route's node-file-trace was inspected to confirm they resolve to real files rather than a mangled path.
- **`.dam-glass`'s `backdrop-filter` traps a card's own dropdown below page-level overlays.** Any element with `backdrop-filter` (every `.dam-glass` card) becomes its own CSS stacking context, which caps its descendants' `z-index` — an absolutely-positioned kebab dropdown inside a glass card that declares `z-30` is still only "z-30 within that card," not z-30 on the page. A page-level sibling overlay (e.g. an outside-click-to-close backdrop at `z-10`) that sits *outside* the card will render above the entire card — dropdown included — regardless of the dropdown's own z-index or DOM order, silently swallowing clicks on menu items while the menu still renders visibly underneath. Hit (and fixed, Stage 44) on the file-card and sub-collection-card kebabs in `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx` and `app/admin/media/page.tsx`. Fix pattern: conditionally raise the *currently-open* card's own wrapper to `z-20` (above the backdrop's `z-10`) only while its menu is open — this reuses the same trick `components/AppShell.tsx`'s frosted top bar already had by luck (`dam-glass-topbar relative z-20`), which is why the collection-level breadcrumb kebab was never affected. Apply this pattern to any future dropdown/menu mounted inside a `.dam-glass` (or otherwise `backdrop-filter`'d) container. **Superseded for new components as of Stage 71:** rather than raising the open card's z-index each time, a new popover can instead render via `createPortal` at `position: fixed` (see the bullet below) and avoid the stacking-context trap entirely — this z-20 pattern remains correct for the existing sites listed above, which weren't retrofitted. **Stage 77 confirms this is not just a "raise the number" problem when the trapped element's own ancestor (not the popover's own card) is on the wrong side of the comparison:** the collection-actions kebab dropdown (`app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`) rendered beneath a sibling sub-collection card's kebab button, and no z-index fixed it — `z-30` was tried on the wrong element first, then `z-50` (matching the Import URL popover's own established convention) on the *correct* element, and it still rendered underneath; a `dam-glass` card's `backdrop-filter` stacking context cannot be outranked by a z-index on an element that isn't inside it, full stop. Only `createPortal(..., document.body)` + `position: fixed` from the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect()` (the Stage 71 `CollectionTreePicker` pattern) fixed it. This is the **6th instance** of this project's recurring backdrop-filter/overflow popover gotcha, and the **2nd time** (after the two failed z-index attempts documented in Stage 77 itself) that z-index was reached for first and had to be abandoned for a portal — reach for the portal first for any new popover that might sit inside, or need to rise above, a `.dam-glass` ancestor; don't spend a fix cycle on z-index first.
- **Any container wrapping something that opens a menu/dropdown must have its `overflow` checked — this has now bitten five times.** (1) Stage 44: `backdrop-filter` stacking contexts trapped card kebabs below the page's outside-click backdrop (the bullet directly above) — fixed by raising the open card to `z-20`, a *z-index* problem, not overflow. (2) Stage 50: list-view panels initially risked the same fate from a different cause — a wrapper's `overflow-hidden`, kept off the list-panel wrapper (unlike the grid cards, which do use it) specifically so a bottom row's kebab dropdown isn't clipped by the panel's rounded corners. (3) Stage 54: an early pass put `overflow-hidden` on the whole collapsible sidebar `<aside>`, which silently clipped the account-menu popover only in the collapsed (64px rail) state — fixed by scoping `overflow-hidden` to just the logo row, nav list, and account button individually, leaving the sidebar itself unclipped so the popover renders past the rail width. (4) Stage 57: a breadcrumb wrapper's `overflow-hidden` (added "for width safety" against long ancestor names) silently clipped the new overflow-collapse dropdown — the toggle state flipped correctly (`aria-expanded="true"`) but nothing rendered, because the dropdown extends below a wrapper barely taller than one text line; fixed by removing that `overflow-hidden` and relying on the already-present per-segment `max-w-[…] truncate` to bound row width instead. (5) Stage 71: `components/CollectionTreePicker.tsx` sidestepped the whole family of bugs at the source rather than chasing another per-site overflow/z-index fix — its panel renders via `createPortal(..., document.body)` at `position: fixed` computed from the trigger's own bounding rect, so it's never a descendant of any `.dam-glass`/`overflow-hidden` ancestor in the first place (a `backdrop-filter` ancestor otherwise establishes a containing block for `position: fixed` descendants too, which would still mis-position a naively-fixed popover even with no `overflow-hidden` in play). **This is now the preferred fix for any new popover/dropdown that might be mounted inside a `.dam-glass` card, a list panel, or any other `overflow`/`backdrop-filter` ancestor** — reach for a portal + trigger-rect-computed fixed positioning first, rather than re-deriving a bespoke z-index/overflow fix per site. **Rule of thumb (existing popovers, or anywhere a portal is impractical):** before adding `overflow-hidden` to any wrapper, ask whether a descendant popover/dropdown/menu needs to render outside that wrapper's box — if yes, either omit `overflow-hidden` (list panels, the breadcrumb) or scope it narrowly to just the part that truly needs clipping (grid cards clip only the thumbnail's own rounded corners, not the whole card).
- **Adding a filter to `GET /api/media`? Use `EXISTS` with a fresh alias, never a `WHERE` on the existing `cr` join.** `resources LEFT JOIN collection_resource cr` is load-bearing for the "Unassigned only" filter (`HAVING COUNT(cr.resource_id) = 0`) — constraining `cr` directly in a new `WHERE` (e.g. `cr.collection_id = $1`) silently turns that `LEFT JOIN` into an inner join, which makes every resource with **any** collection membership disappear from the unassigned count too, breaking a filter that has nothing to do with the new one. The Stage 62 collection-subtree filter uses a second alias (`cr2`) inside an `EXISTS` subquery instead, leaving `cr` / `GROUP BY r.id` / the `HAVING` clause byte-for-byte untouched.
- **A filtered `COUNT(*)` must reuse the page query's exact `WHERE`/`HAVING`, or the pager lies.** `GET /api/media`'s `total` (Stage 61) is a second query built from the identical `whereClause`/`havingClause` strings the row query uses — never a separate, simpler count. This held even when a new filter was added on top (Stage 62's `collectionId`): verified each time by comparing the API's `total` against an independent hand-written SQL count, not just by reading the code.
- **Propagating a value up an ancestor chain must guard each row independently — never walk-and-stop at the first row that already qualifies.** Stage 64's ancestor cover propagation is a single set-based `UPDATE ... WHERE id IN (SELECT id FROM ancestors) AND cover_storage_key IS NULL`, not a loop that climbs one level at a time and quits on the first already-covered ancestor. The distinction matters: a manually-covered mid-level collection sitting between an upload and a still-coverless root must not shadow that root from ever being covered — verified explicitly (ROOT → MID [manual cover] → LEAF, upload into LEAF covered LEAF and ROOT while leaving MID's manual cover untouched). Applies to any future "walk up/down and conditionally write" query, not just covers.
- **A theme (or any pre-paint visual preference) must be applied by a blocking script before first paint, never from a React effect.** Stage 75's dark-mode toggle persists to `localStorage['dam:theme']`, but reading it back inside `AppShell`'s normal mount `useEffect` (like every other persisted preference in this app) would apply the theme only *after* the first paint — every dark-mode user would see a light flash on every single load. Fixed with a separate, blocking (no `async`/`defer`) inline `<script>` in `app/layout.tsx`'s `<head>`, which runs synchronously before `<body>` is even parsed and sets `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` directly — confirmed as the last node in the served `<head>` HTML, ahead of `<body>`. `AppShell`'s own mount effect still runs afterward to sync its React `darkMode` state *from* `dataset.theme` (so the toggle's visible label/icon/switch state matches on hydration), but it never sets the attribute itself. Apply this same blocking-script-in-`<head>` pattern to any future pre-paint preference (e.g. a locale or density setting) — a `useEffect` is fine for preferences that don't affect first paint, wrong for ones that do.
- **`color-scheme` does not fix an unreadable native `<select>` option popup — the fix is an opaque background, not a browser hint.** Stage 76's dark-mode selects (`bg-white/NN dark:bg-white/10`, the frosted filter-chip look) rendered a near-white, unreadable option list in dark mode. The plausible-looking fix — setting `color-scheme: light`/`color-scheme: dark` on `:root`/`[data-theme="dark"]` in `globals.css`, on the theory that it tells the browser which default palette to paint its own native popup chrome with — was tried and **confirmed not to work**: even setting `color-scheme: dark` directly on `<html>` via the browser console (bypassing the app entirely) left the popup white. The actual mechanism: a `<select>`'s open option list is native browser/OS chrome, not part of the page's own compositing tree, so a translucent `background-color` on the `<select>` composites against the browser's own default (light) popup canvas rather than the page behind it — `color-scheme` doesn't change that compositing target. The `color-scheme` declarations were removed as dead code. The real fix is to make the `<select>`'s dark-mode background fully opaque (`dark:bg-card`, matching the already-working `bg-card` selects elsewhere in the app) so there's no translucency left to composite incorrectly. If a future `<select>` needs a translucent look in dark mode, don't reach for `color-scheme` to fix its popup — it won't.
- **A native `<select>`'s `<option>` text is drawn by the browser/OS, not the page — no CSS reaches it, ever.** This is a harder limit than the background-color issue directly above (which at least has a CSS-side fix); the font, size, and rendering of option text in an open `<select>` popup is native chrome, full stop. It's why every native select in this app read in the OS font while `components/CollectionTreePicker.tsx` (a real DOM component, not a native popup) read in the app font — confirmed as the root cause and closed at Stage 114 by replacing all 31 native `<select>` elements with `components/Select.tsx`. **The portal-rendered custom listbox is now this app's standard for any dropdown, not just an option for tree-shaped ones** — `createPortal(..., document.body)` + `position: fixed` computed from the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect()` remains the answer both to this (an app-font-styleable option list) and to the pre-existing backdrop-filter/overflow stacking trap documented below. Do not add a new native `<select>` to this app; use `components/Select.tsx`.
- **`group_collection_access` grants are root-only by query shape, not by schema.** The table has no constraint tying `collection_id` to a `parent_id IS NULL` row — nothing in the database stops an `INSERT` against a sub-collection. But every `root_finder`/cascade CTE that reads this table (collections, files, download, ancestors, search, metadata routes) walks a collection **up** to its root first and only joins the grants table there; a grant row on a non-root `collection_id` is never reached by any of them and is silently inert, not an error. Confirmed by Stage 79's investigation before building the access-editing feature (see Stage 79, the Database section, and `app/api/collections/[id]/access/route.ts`'s own `resolveRoot()`). Always resolve to the root before granting or reading a grant — never assume a `collection_id` you're about to write is already one.
- **`@napi-rs/canvas`'s `loadImage()` already auto-rotates according to EXIF orientation — do not also apply a manual rotation.** Stage 82's image-thumbnail generator was planned around reading `Orientation` via `exifr` and rotating with a canvas transform (the "classic image-thumbnail bug"), and that manual-rotation code was actually written and tested first — it **double-rotated** the output. `loadImage()` itself already corrects for all 8 EXIF orientation values internally: `image.width`/`.height` come back already in the *display* (post-rotation) orientation, not the raw stored-raster orientation, and the decoded pixels are already upright. Confirmed empirically two ways, not assumed: (1) a synthetic marker image (unambiguous "blue square top-left, arrow pointing up" reference) with its raw pixels pre-rotated and tagged `Orientation=3/6/8` via exiftool's `-Orientation#=N`, and (2) real camera-orientation test photos (`recurser/exif-orientation-examples`, explicit "top/bottom/left/right" labels) for orientations 1/3/6/8 — both rendered upright through `loadImage` + a **zero-transform** `drawImage`, and both were rendered *wrong* (rotated a second time) the moment a manual orientation-correcting transform was added on top. `renderImageThumbnail()` in `app/api/resources/[id]/thumbnail/route.ts` deliberately never reads or applies `Orientation` — if a future canvas upgrade or decode path ever changes this auto-behavior, the regression test is the same one used here (render a real `Orientation=6/8` photo and confirm the thumbnail is upright), not a reflex reach for manual rotation.
- **`exifr` with `mergeOutput:false` does not group tags the way you'd guess from the tag name.** IPTC lands under `iptc` but with exifr's own translated (unhyphenated) key names — `Byline` not `By-line`, `Caption` not `Caption-Abstract`, `Country` not `Country-PrimaryLocationName`, `CopyrightNotice` not `Copyright` — confirmed by reading `node_modules/exifr/src/dicts/iptc-keys.mjs`, not guessed. XMP Dublin-Core properties (`title`/`description`/`subject`/`creator`/`rights`) land under a **top-level `dc` key, not `xmp`** — exifr's XMP parser assigns each XML namespace as its own top-level output key (`tiff`→merged into `ifd0`, `exif`→merged into `exif`, everything else incl. `dc` gets its own key). `Make`/`Model`/`ImageWidth`/`ImageHeight` are baseline TIFF tags under `ifd0`; the rest of the EXIF IFD (`DateTimeOriginal`, `ISO`, `FNumber`, etc.) is under `exif`. `lib/exif.ts`'s `GETTERS` table is the one place this mapping is encoded — verified empirically against a real exiftool-tagged JPEG (see `docs/sessions/CHANGELOG.md`), not assumed from the docs. **Separately, the classic EXIF-date pitfall:** exifr revives tags like `DateTimeOriginal` into a native `Date` built via *local* `Date` methods from the naive, timezone-less "YYYY:MM:DD HH:MM:SS" string — reading it back with UTC getters (`toISOString()` etc.) re-applies a timezone conversion on top and can shift the date by a day near midnight. Always read such a `Date` with local getters (`getFullYear`/`getMonth`/`getDate`) to exactly undo the local construction — confirmed by reading exifr's own `reviveDate()` in `tiff-revivers.mjs` and empirically, with a photo tagged at `00:45:12` local time that round-trips as the previous day in UTC.
- **JSX trims leading whitespace on the first line of a multi-line text node.** A variable/expression followed by literal text that wraps onto the next source line (e.g. `</span> more text` where "more text" continues across further physical lines within the same JSX text child) can silently lose the space right after the expression at compile time — it's invisible in the source and in `tsc`/`next build`, only visible in the rendered browser output (see Stage 111, the permissions-page header). Use an explicit `{" "}` immediately after the expression/tag whenever the following text isn't guaranteed to stay on one physical line.
- **A check-then-act guard on a global count needs `FOR UPDATE`, not a plain `SELECT COUNT(*)` before the write.** Two concurrent requests can each read the same pre-decrement count and both pass an "is this the last one" check, jointly leaving zero. The pattern that closes this: lock the relevant rows inside the same transaction as the write (e.g. `SELECT id FROM ... WHERE ... FOR UPDATE`, counting the locked rows in application code — Postgres doesn't allow `FOR UPDATE` on an aggregate query) so a second concurrent transaction blocks until the first commits, then re-evaluates against the now-reduced, already-committed set. Apply this to any future "don't let the last X be removed" guard, not just this one.
- **Localized UI strings go through `t()` with `{{param}}` interpolation — never concatenate translated fragments.** Word order differs across languages (Italian vs English), so joining two separately-translated half-sentences (a bolded subject + a hardcoded tail, say) can produce word-salad in a language never tested by eye. Every sentence with a dynamic part is one whole translation key with named `{{tokens}}`, resolved via `lib/i18n.tsx`'s `t()` — `app/admin/activity/page.tsx`'s `interpolateJSX()` helper (Stage 117) is the pattern for keeping a substituted value (e.g. a bold actor name) as real JSX inside an otherwise-fully-translated sentence, by splitting the *localized* template on its own `{{token}}` positions rather than gluing pre-rendered fragments together. The one exception: joining two independently-complete, already-whole sentences/clauses (e.g. Stage 117's tenant-delete stat line, joined via `common.listSeparator`) is fine — that's a list join, not fragment reassembly.
- **Pluralize via a numeric `count` param, never a hand-rolled `s`/`""` ternary.** `t()` auto-selects `${key}.one`/`${key}.other` (CLDR two-form) when `params.count` is a number; English and Italian are both two-form languages, so this covers everything built through Stage 117. A sentence with two independent counts ("X collections and Y files…") can't be one CLDR key — split it into two separately-pluralized clauses (own key each) rather than forcing one key to agree with two numbers at once; Stage 117's tenant-delete confirmation became two list bullets for exactly this reason (see "Known characteristics" below).
- **Don't reuse a `common.*` key across two grammatically different slots, even when the English word is identical.** `common.download` is an imperative button verb ("Scarica" in Italian); a noun/adjective slot needing the same English word ("download" as a share's access level) needs its own key (`activity.line.accessLevelDownload`, Stage 117) — reusing the verb form there would be grammatically wrong in Italian even though it reads fine in English. The same distinction applies to raw role-enum values (`ROLES.name`, `inv.role.name`, `share.accessLevel`) versus human-authored role labels for the same role (`common.role.editor` etc.): the former stay untranslated by design, the latter are real translation keys — don't conflate the two, and don't force a `common.*` merge just because two strings happen to match today.
- **Adding a language is one `locales/<code>.json` + one `lib/i18n.tsx` `LOCALES` entry — never a component edit.** The pre-paint `<head>` script in `app/layout.tsx` doesn't hardcode locale codes either (it just mirrors `localStorage['dam:locale']` onto `<html>`), so nothing else in the app needs to change when a language is added — verified live at Stage 116 with a throwaway `fr.json` + one `LOCALES` row, `tsc --noEmit` clean with zero edits elsewhere, then both reverted.

| Stage | What it deletes | What it keeps | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 — remove file from collection | `collection_resource` link only | `resources` row, MinIO bytes, other links | ✓ |
| 2 — permanent file delete | `resources` row + MinIO bytes + all `collection_resource` links (cascade) | nothing (irreversible) | ✓ |
| 3 — delete collection | collection row + entire sub-tree + their links + their grants (all cascade) | `resources` rows, MinIO bytes | ✓ |
