## Known characteristics / tech debt

- **Unassigned files** (now fully closed-loop): a file can land Unassigned via Stage 1 removal, Stage 3 collection delete, or (now) direct upload in the Media library. Unassigned files still exist in MinIO and `resources` but are invisible/inaccessible to non-admins (download security denies files in no accessible collection) and are excluded from search results (search uses INNER JOIN collection_resource). The media library (`GET /api/media` + `app/admin/media/page.tsx`) surfaces them with an amber badge; admins can upload files there directly (Stage 29 — lands Unassigned) and relink existing ones via the per-card "Add to collection…" kebab item or bulk-select + "Add to collection" (both backed by `POST /api/resources/[id]/collections`) — linking a file makes it immediately visible/downloadable to that collection's group. The full staging workflow (upload → Unassigned → assign to a collection) is complete end-to-end.
- **Filename dedupe (Stage 73) only runs at create time, not at link time.** `original_filename` lives on the `resources` row and is shared across every collection a file is linked into, so "Add to collection" (Stage 26/27) deliberately does not rename on collision — doing so would rename the file in every other collection it already belongs to. A file added to a second collection that happens to already contain a same-named file will show that collision undeduped until manually renamed. Also accepted: a narrow TOCTOU window between the scoping SELECT and the INSERT (same transaction, but READ COMMITTED) — not closed with a lock, since uploads are sequential in the UI today.
- **Image thumbnail and cover loading speed (still applies to images/covers only):** image thumbnails and collection covers both fetch the full-resolution file and scale it in the browser. Collections with many large images load slowly. Real server-side generated image thumbnails are deferred (needs an image-resize library — no longer blocked on "needs a worker," since the video-thumbnail work (Stage 33) proved out the pull-to-temp-file-and-process-on-request pattern without a separate worker process; this could reuse the same approach). Video thumbnails no longer have this problem — see Stage 33/33b.
- **Video thumbnail generation loads the whole file into memory:** `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail` does `getObject` (full `Buffer`) → temp file, rather than streaming the download to disk. Fine for reasonable video sizes; a memory spike for very large ones. Streaming to disk is a possible future optimization, not yet needed.
- **Bulk download memory / time:** bulk download fetches every selected file fully into the browser before zipping (client-side JSZip). Fine for normal-sized selections; large numbers of large files will use proportional memory and time. Acceptable tradeoff — no server infrastructure needed.
- `frontend/README.md` may still say "Open http://localhost:3000" — low priority.
- **LaTeX / Overleaf report is far behind the actual project.** Last known caught up through the file-card UI redesign (2026-06-29, per `CHANGELOG.md`). Everything since is undocumented in the report: delete (all 3 stages), registration→Pending flow, bulk upload/download, admin media library, rename (files + collections), search, admin user management, unassigned-file management + relinking, URL import (SSRF-hardened), real breadcrumb name, avatar/account menu, video thumbnails (Stages 32–33b, this session), and the auth-pages brand redesign (split-screen login + AuthShell cousin layout, Stages 34/34b). This is a documentation catch-up task, not a code task — flagged here so it isn't lost, not scheduled.
- **`coperon-technologies.png` missing from `/public/` — RESOLVED by Stage 56.** The login (`app/page.tsx`), `AuthShell` (`components/AuthShell.tsx`), and `AppShell`'s sidebar (`components/AppShell.tsx`) all reference `/coperon-technologies.png`; the `src` was wired correctly from the start, but the file itself was confirmed absent from `frontend/public/` for a long stretch (re-checked and still missing as recently as Stage 54), so the logo rendered as broken/alt text in all three places and blocked visual verification of the Stage 34/34b/35 redesigns. **This was always a separate asset from the favicon** (Stage 45b: `app/icon.png`/`app/apple-icon.png`, the small square browser-tab mark, done since Stage 45b) — the wordmark is the wide horizontal logo used inline in the UI. Stage 56 landed both `coperon-technologies.png` (678×195, confirmed via disk read, not assumed) and `login-mm.jpg` (916×887, the Stage 55 login-illustration background) directly in `frontend/public/`; the logo now renders correctly in all three pre-existing mount points plus the new Stage 55 login-panel white plate. Stage 54's collapsed sidebar rail was never affected by this gap either way — it deliberately uses the separate, already-present square `/icon.png` favicon asset in place of the wordmark (which doesn't fit a 64px rail anyway).
- **Pending manual cleanups in the dev DB/MinIO** (not code debt, just test residue from browser-verification sessions; safe to leave, harmless if cleaned up manually):
  - Leftover metadata-workflow test data: two resources carry Playwright-verification values in the "Title" field (`resource_field_data`, field id 12) — `0fc9663d-1746-4b47-b576-0c7a7d6c01a9` ("Playwright verified value") and `baf6bebf-2423-43ba-a747-f7531c3c465b` ("Playwright-Verify-Title-A") — plus several duplicate unassigned `test1.png`/`test2.png` resources accumulated across multiple stages' upload-workflow tests (`GET /api/media?unassigned=true` surfaces all of them).
  - Two leftover rows in `shares` (re-checked on disk as of Stage 48 — **not** the "AlexPereira.jpg"/"Album Covers" pair from earlier Stage 47 test notes, which were already deleted at the end of that session): a `view`-access, non-revoked share on the **"UFC"** collection and a `view`-access, non-revoked share on the **"Root B (Editors only)"** collection, both created by manual admin-UI testing (not by an automated test script) and both still active/unexpired. Visible in `/admin/shares`; revoke or delete manually if no longer wanted.
  - One leftover stub company, **"Activity Scope Test Co"** (Stage 99) — created to prove the activity feed's cross-tenant scope-escape prevention with a real second company rather than a synthetic one (a real user was temporarily moved into it, logged in to generate a genuine cross-company event, then moved back). **`DELETE /api/tenants/[id]` now exists (Stage 113)** — this company is no longer permanently stuck — but it was deliberately left alone rather than deleted as part of that stage: it currently holds what looks like a real, non-synthetic user account (`gdkoueik@ndu.edu.lb`), and removing someone's real account as a side effect of shipping an unrelated feature wasn't asked for. Delete it through `/admin/tenants` (or `psql`) if it's confirmed to still be test-only.
- **`ResourceMetadata` fetches per-card, not in bulk (Stage 40) — RESOLVED by Stage 43.** Originally, each rendered file card mounted `<ResourceMetadata>` directly and fired its own `GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata` on mount, so a collection of N files meant N requests. Stage 43 (`components/MetadataDrawer.tsx`) moved `ResourceMetadata` out of the always-open inline card block and into a right-side drawer opened on demand via a "Details" trigger — it now mounts (and fetches) only for the one asset whose drawer is open, not once per rendered card. No further action needed; noted here for history rather than as open debt.
- **`CollectionTreePicker`'s per-node cache isn't invalidated for a picker instance's lifetime (Stage 71).** Once a level's children are fetched they stay cached until the component unmounts — a collection created, renamed, or deleted elsewhere while a picker is open (or already expanded to that level) won't be reflected until the picker is closed and reopened. Acceptable for an admin-only, short-lived popover; not a concern for any of the current four sites, not scheduled for a fix.
- **Neither `CollectionTreePicker` nor `Select` reposition their panel on scroll (Stage 71, extended to `Select` at Stage 114).** Both compute `position: fixed` coordinates once, from the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect()`, at the moment the panel opens — there is no scroll listener in either component. Scrolling the page while a panel is open leaves it at those same viewport coordinates, visually detached from its trigger, until it's closed. Kept deliberately (not a gap introduced by `Select`, just a characteristic inherited from the mechanism both components deliberately share) rather than adding new scroll-close/reposition logic that only one of the two would have had. Now affects every dropdown in the app — 31 `Select` sites plus `CollectionTreePicker`'s four — not just one.
- **Firefox always gets the instant dark-mode flip, never the circular wipe (Stage 75).** Firefox has no `document.startViewTransition` (the View Transitions API is Chromium-only as of this writing); `toggleDarkMode`'s feature-detect (`"startViewTransition" in document`) is `false` there, so it falls straight to the instant-apply branch — same code path as `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` on any browser. This is expected, not a bug: the instant flip is a correct baseline on its own merits (see Stage 75's rationale against a CSS color-transition fallback), not a degraded fallback that needs fixing when Firefox eventually ships the API.
- ~~**The "Test accounts" list under Database is stale**~~ **Resolved — re-verified and rewritten in Stage 84.** First flagged in Stage 78 (`viewer@test.local` actually in Editors, `georgeskey2004@gmail.com` actually Administrators, two undocumented accounts). The Database section's list now reflects a fresh live query (7 accounts, correct groups). `jrahhal@coperon.com` remains the only genuine Viewer-group account with an unknown password — Stage 84's own viewer-role verification again used a disposable temp account (registered, promoted, tested, deleted) rather than guessing it; keep doing that going forward. This list will still drift with future manual testing — re-verify with `psql` rather than trusting it blindly, same caveat as before.
- **Multi-tenancy migration (Stages 84–89) changed live behavior three times, deliberately — worth knowing before reading old bug reports or test notes as if they still apply:** (1) **Viewers gained collections they couldn't see before, once — at the Stage 84 backfill.** Every non-super-admin user's group-based access collapsed onto one company ("Test Company"), so a Viewer/Editor who previously saw only their own group's grants started seeing everything Test Company was granted, the moment Stage 86 switched reads onto `company_collection_access`. Expected and accounted for at the time (this *is* what "collapsing into one company" means for the dev DB's only company) — not a regression if you see it in old session notes. (2) **A token minted before Stage 85 (no `companyId`/`roleId` claims) gets zero access, not full access, everywhere the new checks apply** — every fail-closed check treats a missing `companyId` as "no company" and a missing `roleId` as "not super admin," never as "skip the check." Tokens expire in 1 day, so this is now purely historical. (3) **Capability moved from `group_id` to `role_id` at Stage 89 with no user's effective capability changing** — confirmed by comparing the group-derived and role-derived capability tuple for all 7 real users, both before and after; the Stage 84 backfill's group→role mapping was exact. If a future user's capability ever *does* differ between the two, that's a backfill bug worth chasing, not expected drift.
- **Home's collection cards have no kebab menu at all — Access, Rename, and Delete are only reachable from a collection's own detail page.** `app/home/page.tsx`'s top-level grid/list cards are plain links into `/collections/[id]`; unlike the collection-detail page's cards (which have had a Rename/Delete kebab since Stage 23, now joined by Access in Stage 79), no per-card menu was ever built on home. This was a deliberate scope call in Stage 79 rather than an oversight carried forward: adding an admin-only Access item to home's cards would have meant building a whole new kebab-menu system there for one action alone, with no Rename/Delete to keep it company. Every root collection remains one click away from its own detail page, where all three actions are fully functional — this is a known asymmetry between the two surfaces, not a missing feature.
- **No notification is sent to the old email address when a profile email change completes.** `POST /api/profile/email-change` (Stage 107) sends the confirmation link only to the *new* address (proving control of it is the point); once `POST /api/profile/email-change/confirm` swaps `users.email`, nothing emails the old address to say the account's login email just changed. A real account-takeover path if a session/password were ever compromised first (the attacker could redirect all future logins to an address the real owner never sees). Not built; a future stage should send a heads-up (no action link, no way to undo) to the pre-change address at the same point `sendEmailChangeConfirmation` fires for the new one.
- **Date/time formatting still follows the browser's own locale, not the app's `dam:locale` toggle.** Every date formatter in the app (`toLocaleDateString(undefined, …)`/`toLocaleString(undefined, …)` in the `users`/`tenants`/`shares`/`analytics`/`activity` pages, all predating Stage 116, **plus `app/profile/page.tsx`'s `formatDateTime` and `app/share/[token]/page.tsx`'s expiry-date formatting, both localized in Stage 119 otherwise**) passes `undefined` as the locale argument, which resolves to the browser/OS locale — not the manually-selected app language. An Italian-language session on an English-OS browser will still show dates in English formatting: `app/admin/activity/page.tsx`'s day-cluster headings beyond "Today"/"Yesterday", `app/admin/analytics/page.tsx`'s chart-tooltip day text, `app/profile/page.tsx`'s pending-email-change expiry, `app/share/[token]/page.tsx`'s share-expiry line, and every other `formatDate`/`formatDateTime` helper across the localized pages. Not addressed in Stages 116–119 — it's a formatting-infrastructure change (threading the app's `locale` state from `useTranslation()` into every `toLocaleDateString`/`toLocaleString` call site) rather than a string-translation one, and was explicitly out of scope for all four stages. A future stage should thread `locale` through instead of leaving it `undefined`.
- **Several recent UI surfaces are type-checked but not browser-verified** (per their own stage entries' "no Playwright" constraint) — carried forward and extended by Stages 114–117, still nothing here has had an actual browser pass. Pre-existing, still open: the All-tenants/Can-invite columns on the admin users page, and the profile page (`app/profile/page.tsx`). Newly open, Stage 114 (the `Select` rollout, 31 sites) and Stage 115 (the permissions-cell cleanup): the visual pass needed is (1) option-text font match — `Select`'s popup should read identically to `CollectionTreePicker`'s, side by side; (2) focus rings and keyboard nav (arrows, Enter/Space, Escape, Home/End, typeahead) on a real `Select` instance; (3) click and scroll behavior, including the no-reposition-on-scroll characteristic above; and (4) the four highest-value trapped-context spots flagged when Stage 114 shipped — the media filter toolbar (`app/admin/media/page.tsx`, `Select` rendered directly beside `CollectionTreePicker`), a table row on the admin users page (`Select` inside a `.dam-glass` table), the permissions matrix's tenant picker (`app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`, topbar `.dam-glass-topbar`, plus Stage 115's now-toggle-only cells), and the collection page's "Create share link" modal (`Select` inside a `fixed z-40` overlay). All four are code-reviewed against `CollectionTreePicker`'s already-proven mechanism (same portal, same `position: fixed` math, same z-index layering) and pass `tsc`/`next build`, but that is not the same as having actually rendered in a browser. **Also newly open, Stage 116–117 (UI localization):** switching to Italiano across the media page and the seven `app/admin/*` pages has never been seen rendered — worth checking first, in order of likeliest breakage: the permissions matrix's `Editor`/`Viewer` column headers (`Redattore`/`Visualizzatore` in Italian — the latter is markedly longer than "Viewer" and the table has no fixed column widths to fall back on); the activity feed's bold actor/resource/target names wrapping inside `interpolateJSX()`'s output at narrow viewport widths (never visually confirmed that a long bold name mid-sentence wraps cleanly); the account-menu language selector itself (switch, confirm every nav label/account item relabels, confirm no reload flash); and generally, every page listed above as "Localized" for actual layout breakage from the ~15–20% longer Italian strings (table headers, buttons) that the stage rows claim were checked only by inspection/grep, not by eye. **Further newly open, Stage 118 (group 2):** the home page's collection-card grid/list toggle and pager, the collection-detail page's two independent pagers (sub-collections + files) and the breadcrumb kebab, and the full-page metadata editor's "Step N of M" workflow bar — none seen rendered in Italian; the collection-detail page in particular stacks several longer Italian strings (kebab items, bulk-action bar, upload status strip) in a narrower content column than the media page's, so it's the highest-risk surface from this group for the ~15–20% length increase to actually wrap or overflow. **Further newly open, Stage 119 (group 3 minus login):** the invite-redemption form's two-column-on-desktop layout (name/phone/password/confirm stacked above the live password checklist) has never been checked with the longer Italian rule labels; the public share page's header line (target-type + access-level + optional expiry, three independently-translated pieces joined by literal " · " separators) has never been checked for wrapping at the narrower share-header widths; and the public share page's language auto-detect has been code-reviewed but not run against a real browser in any of its three cases (a `dam:locale` already set, an unset browser reporting Italian, an unset browser reporting anything else) — verifying the detected/fallback locale actually renders correctly, not just that the logic reads correctly, is still pending. Worth a real look next time a browser/Playwright session is available.

These are known-pending features — not forgotten, not in progress. **Exception: the multi-tenancy migration and the UI localization effort below are actively in progress** — everything else in this section being marked DONE should not be read as "nothing is pending" for the app as a whole.

- **UI localization (Italian) — IN PROGRESS. Stage 116 (infrastructure + `app/admin/media/page.tsx` as the reference page), Stage 117 (Stage B group 1: the seven `app/admin/*` pages), Stage 118 (Stage B group 2: `app/home/page.tsx`, `app/collections/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/resources/[id]/metadata/page.tsx`, plus `components/ResourceMetadata.tsx`/`MetadataDrawer.tsx`), and Stage 119 (Stage B group 3 minus login: `app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`, `app/confirm-email/page.tsx`, `app/profile/page.tsx`, `app/share/[token]/page.tsx`, plus `components/PhoneInput.tsx`) are all done; see those stages and `lib/i18n.tsx`'s own row above for the pattern (hand-rolled context + flat-key JSON dictionaries, `{{param}}` interpolation, CLDR pluralization, `interpolateJSX` for bolded-name sentences, no new dependency). 572 keys, en/it identical. **`app/page.tsx` (login) is deliberately excluded, not a gap** — it was converted then explicitly reverted to hardcoded English within Stage 119 itself; there is no logged-out language switcher and none is planned. Only two things remain: **Stage B group 4** — the shared components + lib label modules still carrying hardcoded English (`components/CollectionTreePicker.tsx`'s own internal loading/error/empty strings, `components/ViewToggle.tsx`'s `aria-label`s, `lib/fileTypes.ts`'s `shortTypeLabel()`, `lib/exifSources.ts`'s `EXIF_SOURCES` label list) — not started, not planned as of this note; and the pre-existing **date-formatting gap** — `toLocaleDateString`/`toLocaleString` calls across the app (now including `app/profile/page.tsx`'s `formatDateTime` and `app/share/[token]/page.tsx`'s expiry date, both localized in Stage 119 otherwise) resolve to the browser's locale, not `dam:locale` — see "Known characteristics."
- **Multi-tenancy migration — DONE end to end, including the legacy group-model drop (Stages 84–94).** Shipped: schema (Stage 84), JWT-additive (Stage 85), access reads (Stage 86), access writes (Stage 87), any-depth grants (Stage 88), the `requireAdmin` split + role-derived capability + company-scoped user management (Stage 89), **the invitation backend (Stage 90)** — create/send, list, revoke, public validate, public redeem, all tier-scoped and email-delivered, **the invitation redemption UI (Stage 91)** — `/invite/[token]`, split-screen matching the login page, email-locked, live password rules, the needs-company-details branch, generic invalid state; a real invitee can now go from email link to logged-in-capable account with no direct API calls — **company-management + invitation-management admin UI (Stage 92)** — `POST /api/companies` (name-only stub creation, case-insensitive duplicate check), `app/admin/companies/page.tsx` (super-admin-only: create companies, see user-count/admin-presence/stub status per company, invite anyone into any company), and an "Invite a teammate" section on `app/admin/users/page.tsx` for company admins (their own company, editor/viewer only, no picker) — and **the legacy group-model drop (Stage 94)**: reference-swept the whole `frontend/` tree first (confirmed zero live reads — `group_id`/`user_groups`/`group_collection_access` were write-only or comment-only everywhere they still appeared), then removed the users-page Group column and its dead `groupId` write, converted the page's mislabeled "role filter" (which actually filtered by group) into a real `roleId` filter, stopped registration/invitation-redemption from writing `group_id`, dropped `groupId`/`groupName` from the JWT mint site, deleted `GET /api/groups`, and finally ran `ALTER TABLE users DROP COLUMN group_id` → `DROP TABLE group_collection_access` → `DROP TABLE user_groups` in that FK-safe order. `scripts/migrate-to-companies.mjs` (the Stage 1 backfill that originally populated this data) is left in place marked obsolete — it can no longer run, which is expected. The whole loop an admin needs — create a company, invite its first admin, that admin invites their own team — is reachable entirely from the UI, no direct API calls required. Public self-registration is now **fully deleted (Stage 105)** — `POST /api/auth/register`, `app/signup/page.tsx`, and `lib/users.ts::createUser` are all gone (404s at both layers); the only code path that creates a user is `POST /api/invitations/redeem`. No longer deferred.

- **Real image thumbnails / previews — DONE (Stage 82), re-verified end-to-end with zero gaps (Stage 95).** Server-side resize pipeline shipped, reusing exactly the pull-to-memory/temp, process, upload, record-key pattern this note anticipated — see Stage 82 for the full build (`@napi-rs/canvas`, 640px long edge, no upscale) and the EXIF-orientation finding (`loadImage()` already auto-rotates; no manual rotation needed or wanted). All display surfaces now fetch the small derivative instead of the full original. **Stage 95** audited this feature against a fresh, detailed acceptance spec (route guard, both upload triggers, both kebabs, all four display surfaces, lightbox/download bypass, backfill) and found every touch-point already correct — no code changes were needed. Verified live instead: uploaded a real 5184×3456/6.6MB photo through the real upload pipeline and confirmed the generated thumbnail came back 640×427/63KB (≈1% of original size); built a real JPEG carrying a genuine EXIF Orientation=6 tag (landscape-stored, portrait-display, the classic phone-photo shape — no rotated real photo existed yet in this dev library to reuse) and confirmed the generated thumbnail came back upright, portrait-shaped, with all four quadrant colors landing where a correct 90°-CW rotation puts them; confirmed a real SVG upload 400s cleanly with no `thumbnail_storage_key` written, and a corrupt/undecodable file 422s cleanly (caught, logged, no crash); confirmed `requireSuperAdmin` rejects a viewer, an editor, and a company admin (role 2, `canAdmin: true`) alike — only true super admins (role 1) can generate; confirmed a collection cover pointing at an image's generated thumbnail (constructed live: unassigned upload → linked into a coverless collection → thumbnail generated → second-chance auto-cover fired) still resolves and decodes correctly after `?force=true` regenerates the same deterministic key in place; confirmed `scripts/regenerate-thumbnails.mjs --missing --type=image` finds and fills exactly one real thumbnail-less image, then finds zero on an immediate re-run (idempotent). All test resources/collections deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean.

Small optional follow-up, not scheduled: wiring **URL-import** into the Stage 45 workflow the same way direct upload now is — it was deliberately left out of Stage 45's scope and Stage 48 didn't touch it either.
- **LaTeX report catch-up** — see "Known characteristics / tech debt" above; documentation debt, not a code feature.
- **External share links — DONE end to end (Stages 46 + 47).** Admin half (Stage 46: `shares` table, `POST`/`GET /api/shares`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/shares/[id]`, `/admin/shares` UI) and the public viewing half (Stage 47: `lib/shares.ts` fail-closed validation + scope helper, `GET /api/share/[token]`, `GET /api/share/[token]/file/[resourceId]`, `app/share/[token]/page.tsx`) are both built and tested — see the Stage 47 entry above for the full scope-escape/expiry/revoke/access-level test results. Still not built: kebab deep-link entry points (collection page / file-card kebab → pre-targeted create-share flow) — deferred in Stage 46 and still not done; the standalone `/admin/shares` page covers both collection and resource targets already, so this is a pure convenience follow-up, not a gap in the feature.
- **Stale thumbnails need a force-regenerate path — DONE (Stage 65).** `POST /api/resources/[id]/thumbnail?force=true` now bypasses the short-circuit and re-renders (admin-only, video/PDF branches and 422 behavior unchanged); a "Regenerate thumbnail" kebab item exists on both the collection page and Media library for files that already have a thumbnail; `scripts/regenerate-thumbnails.mjs` bulk-drives the endpoint for existing stale PDFs/videos. No longer pending — see Stage 65 and the "Scripts" section.
- **Office document previews (.docx/.xlsx/.pptx) — DROPPED, not scheduled.** Previously "pending a decision"; the team has since decided against building this — clients (Viewers) can't upload at all, so any Office files in the library only ever arrive via Coperon staff (Admins/Editors), making the volume low enough that the **LibreOffice headless** system dependency (a real install/toolchain decision, unlike `pdfjs-dist`/`@napi-rs/canvas` which needed neither) isn't justified. Word/Excel/PowerPoint files keep falling back to `FileTypeIcon` + `shortTypeLabel()` (Stages 51/53) with no real preview. Revisit only if that upload picture changes.
- **Automatic collection cover thumbnails — DONE (Stage 59).** `cover_storage_key` now auto-populates from a collection's own files (first uploaded image, or a video/PDF's generated thumbnail once one exists) whenever it was previously unset; a manual "Change thumbnail" always takes precedence and is never overwritten. `scripts/backfill-covers.mjs` covered the existing coverless collections in the dev DB. No longer pending — see Stage 59 and the "Scripts" section.
- **Pagination is now complete across every listing surface — DONE (Stages 61, 63, 67).** `GET /api/media` (Stage 61), `GET /api/collections/[id]/files` (Stage 63), and `GET /api/collections` for both the home top-level grid and the collection-detail Sub-collections section (Stage 67, opt-in on `page` presence so every non-paginated caller is unaffected) all paginate, same `page`/`pageSize`/`total` convention throughout. No listing surface in the app remains unpaginated.
- **Five UI/UX feedback items from a screenshot review — ALL RESOLVED (Stages 68–72).** Share links generated with the wrong port (Stage 68), casual image-saving on view-only public shares (Stage 69), share-table one-time-link clarity (Stage 70, later refined by Stage 72), the flat em-dash `<select>` collection pickers plus the nested-collection assign gap they hid (Stage 71), and admins being unable to re-copy a share's link (Stage 72, encrypted-token retrieval). Nothing outstanding from this round of feedback.
- **Dark mode — DONE end to end (Stages 74 + 75).** Stage 74: tokens promoted, glass consolidated, zero visual change. Stage 75: dark values for every token, the `[data-theme="dark"]` block, the Tailwind `dark:` selector-strategy variant, `dark:` variants for the semantic colors and `FileTypeIcon`'s accents, retuned aurora opacities, the account-menu toggle with persistence (`localStorage['dam:theme']`) and an anti-flash blocking script, and the View-Transitions circular-wipe/instant-fallback transition — plus a same-session toggle-label wording fix, folded into Stage 75's entry. Nothing outstanding; see Stage 75 for the full build and the deliberately-not-themed list (login illustration, PDF white plate, real thumbnails, sidebar, lightbox, native `confirm()`).
- **Access log — DONE end to end, including the activity-feed UI (Stages 96–99).** Append-only `access_log` (`user_id`/`company_id`/`resource_id` all `ON DELETE SET NULL`, `company_id` denormalized at write time, `metadata jsonb` as of Stage 98) records `'download'` (`GET /api/download/[id]`), `'view'` (`GET /api/resources/[id]/metadata`, `source: 'metadata'`; **and**, as of Stage 98, `POST /api/resources/[id]/view` for a lightbox open, `source: 'lightbox'` — both kept, no double-count, see Stage 98), `'share_create'` (`POST /api/shares`), `'share_access'` (the public `GET /api/share/[token]`/`.../file/[resourceId]` routes, `'viewed'`/`'downloaded'`, company attributed via `lib/shares.ts`'s `resolveShareOwningCompany()` when unambiguous), `'upload'` (both `POST /api/upload/complete` and `POST /api/upload/from-url`), and `'login'` (`lib/auth.ts::login()`, success only) events, fire-and-forget via `lib/accessLog.ts`, only after each route's own access/auth check passes. Two readers now: `GET /api/analytics` + `app/admin/analytics/page.tsx` (Stage 97) read the `'view'`/`'download'` subset for aggregate charts, and `GET /api/activity` + `app/admin/activity/page.tsx` (Stage 99) read **all six** action types as a raw, cursor-paginated, filterable chronological feed — both scoped from the token exactly like `GET /api/users` (super admin all companies, company admin their own, no request parameter able to widen it, verified live for both readers). A deleted resource/user surfaces as `(deleted asset)`/`(deleted user)` in both readers rather than being dropped or silently omitted (Stage 99 fixed a real bug here — see its own changelog entry — where `resource_id`'s `ON DELETE SET NULL` was initially indistinguishable from "no resource," collapsing a deleted asset into a blank line instead of the intended label). No new dependency for either reader's UI — plain CSS bars for analytics, a small local relative-time helper and a portal popover (reusing `CollectionTreePicker`'s pattern) for the activity feed. Records from ship-time forward; cannot reconstruct past activity. Public share downloads/gallery-opens are logged (Stage 98) as `share_access`, always rendered anonymously ("Someone … via a shared link") in the feed — per-file preview fetches (`mode=view`/`mode=original`) remain deliberately unlogged, same as any other card-render.
- **EXIF/IPTC/XMP metadata auto-fill — DONE (Stage 81).** Admin-configurable per-field `exif_source` mapping (23-source whitelist) auto-populates `resource_field_data` from an uploaded image's embedded metadata on both upload paths, never clobbering an existing value. See Stage 81 for the full build and verification. Not built (deliberate, out of scope for this stage): no backfill script for existing files (follow-up, same shape as `backfill-covers.mjs`), no GPS mapping (privacy — no location field exists to hold it).

---

## Pending decisions

- **Company-admin widening (Stage 100) is INTERIM, not a final call.** Stage 6/89's original decision was "Coperon alone controls collections"; Stage 100 reverses that so a company admin has full operational control (create/rename/delete collections, edit access, upload, share, delete assets) within their own company's reach, pending a team member's return to weigh in. The self-granting piece of this (a company admin editing `company_collection_access` on their own company's row) is gated behind a single exported constant, `COMPANY_ADMIN_CAN_EDIT_ACCESS` in `lib/permissions.ts` — flip it to `false` to revert that one piece without touching any route. The broader widening (company-scoped `requireAdmin` in place of `requireSuperAdmin` across the routes listed in Stage 100, plus the media library in Stage 101, plus the upload/import collection requirement in Stage 102) is not behind a single flag — reverting it in full means re-reading Stage 100/101/102's route lists. Stage 101 closed the gap Stage 100 had left documented (media library scoping); Stage 102 closed the narrower finding Stage 101 left open (Upload/Import URL creating invisible Unassigned resources for a company admin). No further gap is currently known in this arc.
- **Duplicate "back to sign in" links on forgot-password, change-password, and signup** (Stage 34b) — each of those three pages now shows both its pre-existing back-to-login link (e.g. "Back to login", "Already have an account? Log in") and the newly added "← Back to sign in" link, since the task required keeping existing links while adding the new one. Decide whether to drop the old wording and keep only the new link, or keep both. Non-blocking.
- ~~**Editor metadata access** — pending a team decision~~ **Resolved, team-approved, shipped in Stage 78.** Editors (`canUpload`) can now author per-asset metadata on any resource in a collection their group can access, via `lib/permissions.ts`'s `canEditResourceMetadata`; assets in no collection stay admin-only (no "or I uploaded it" clause) and field *definitions* (`/admin/metadata-fields`) stay admin-only. See Stage 78.
- **Login page background** — pending a team decision; not part of the five feedback items resolved in Stages 68–72, left open here so it isn't lost.
- ~~**`PDF_JPEG_QUALITY = 0.8` has the same unit bug as the image thumbnails**~~ **Resolved, same session, folded into Stage 83.** Fixed to `80` and all 17 existing PDF thumbnails force-regenerated — see Stage 83's updated entry.
- ~~**New user registrations get `company_id: NULL` and are invisible to every read** (Stage 84's gap, made urgent by Stage 86)~~ **Superseded by Stage 91 — the replacement it was waiting for has shipped.** Stage 87's reasoning was that public self-registration would eventually be replaced by invite redemption (which sets `company_id` from the invite), so a stopgap DB default wasn't worth building; Stage 91 is that replacement — the UI no longer offers self-registration at all (`/signup` redirects to `/`), and invitation redemption is now the only UI path to a new account, always with a real `company_id`/`role_id`. ~~**New, narrower open item left by Stage 91 itself:** `POST /api/auth/register` was never disabled server-side, only unlinked from the UI.~~ **Resolved, Stage 105** — the route, the signup page stub, and `lib/users.ts::createUser` are all deleted; no code path creates a user except invite redemption.
- ~~**`POST /api/collections`'s top-level creation still only writes `group_collection_access`, not `company_collection_access`**~~ **Resolved, Stage 87.** Creation-time grants now write `company_collection_access` (uuid-validated `companyIds`, fail-closed on an unknown id); the home page's create form fetches real companies via the new `GET /api/companies` instead of a hardcoded group list. Confirmed by grep to have been the last runtime writer to `group_collection_access` — nothing in `app/`/`lib/` writes that table anymore (only Stage 84's migration script still reads it). See Stage 87.
- ~~**Postgres exposed to the public internet** (opened 2026-08-18)~~ **Moot as of 2026-08-31 — the design that caused it is gone.** It was the consequence of self-hosting the database on Coperon's Windows server while the app ran on Vercel; the "managed Postgres" alternative it listed as not-yet-ruled-out is now the chosen path (Neon, plan 3.1), so there is no self-managed socket to expose and no allowlist to fail to write. Nothing is owed here. The same move retires the sibling worries about PgBouncer having no Windows build and Docker Desktop being unfit for an unattended server.
- **Do asset bytes have to stay on Coperon hardware?** (opened 2026-08-31, and the one open question that can still overturn the hosting plan.) The 2026-08-18 plan asserted it; this file has never recorded a *reason* — no contract, no data-residency clause, just the assertion. If it is real, Backblaze B2 is out — the bucket already created in Amsterdam would have to be abandoned — and the path is self-hosting on Coperon-controlled hardware (AIStor or Garage, not archived MinIO); if it was only an assumption from when self-hosting looked easier, the Vercel + Neon + B2 plan stands as built. **Answer this before Phase 3 spends money.** Nothing else in the plan is blocked on it.
- **Transactional email provider** (opened 2026-08-31) — `lib/mail.ts` still sends through Gmail SMTP with an app password (~500/day, and Google flags app-password SMTP from rotating serverless IPs). Moving to Resend/Postmark is a config-only swap on the same `nodemailer` transport. Right on any host; urgent on a serverless one. Not started.
- **Whether to move the repo into a GitHub organization** (opened 2026-08-18) — `CoperonDev` is a personal account, so access control is one person's account rather than team-managed. GitHub can transfer a repo into an org later without losing history, so this is deferrable, not urgent.
- **When to rotate the live secrets** (opened 2026-08-18) — see "Security — outstanding". Rotation logs everyone out and needs Docker/Postgres running, so it was deliberately not done mid-session.
